# HASHOUT_LOG · engine (backend/ and kernel/)

Living record of decisions and removed context for the engine, newest first. Read before editing. From v0.21.0 the kernel and the mesher have their own logs, `kernel/HASHOUT_LOG.md` and `mesher/HASHOUT_LOG.md`.

## WK1 v0.21.0 the session key goes to one window, environments are checked with SHA-256, a wide host warns (app/core/guard.py, app/main.py, formcad_engine.py, app/render/environments.py, app/data/render/environments.json, app/services/agent_programs.py)

The key. `Frontend.get_response` wrote `guard.TOKEN` into every `index.html`, so `curl http://127.0.0.1:<port>/` from any process of any user of the computer returned a working key. The owner left the choice between a window key like formvis's and an accepted exception to the assistant; the key was chosen, in the smallest form that fits this engine. formvis has a second secret beside its guard; formCAD already has one token that every route asks for, so the same token is simply delivered differently and no route, header or client changed:

- `FORMCAD_WINDOW_KEY_STDOUT=1` (the shell sets it): `formcad_engine.py` prints `formcad-window-key: <token>` once before the server starts and `guard.page_token()` answers `None` for every page, so no page the engine serves carries the key. The shell hands it to its window. The line is printed with `print`, not through `logging`, and the file handler in `core/runtime.py` hangs on loggers only, so the key is not in `formcad-engine.log` (checked).
- otherwise (`./formCAD serve`, a browser): `page_token()` answers the token for the first `index.html` it is asked for and `None` from then on. `guard.PAGE` counts the withheld loads.
- `session.token` in the data folder is unchanged (mode 0600): the command line, the stdio MCP bridge, `setup/agents.py` and Vite's dev server read it. A process that can read that file is the user.

Decided against: a `POST /api/window/key` claim route as in formvis. With the first-load rule the key never travels in an answer a script could ask for later, and there is no route to race.

What this does not stop: a process of the same user reading `session.token`. That is the documented way in for agents.

Environments. `environments.json` had `md5` per file; it has `sha256` in its place, and `download` hashes with SHA-256. The nine values were computed from the files: the three shipped ones from `app/data/render/env/`, the six others fetched from the catalogue's addresses, each only after its size and old MD5 matched the catalogue. Run: a download of `studio_small_08` 2k was kept; with the last character of its catalogue value changed it was refused and nothing was left in the folder.

Host. `FORMCAD_HOST` outside `guard.LOOPBACK` prints one line to stderr. The guard's Host check is unchanged, so such an engine still answers only requests addressed to 127.0.0.1 or localhost.

Dock contract. `agent_programs.CONTRACT` is "2.2.1", the module version the interface now carries (clause 16 of `frontend/src/dock/CONTRACT.md`).

## AD1 v0.20.0 the engine side of the shared agent dock 2.2.0 (app/routers/terminal.py, app/services/terminal.py, app/services/agent_programs.py, app/services/agent_lookup.py, app/main.py, setup/agents.py, setup/freeze_backend)

The owner asked for "the actual claude code, cursor, codex, antigravity" inside the app, as the family's newest public apps have it. formCAD had no terminal and no agent tabs, so this is a full port, written from formtxt's (Apache-2.0) in this engine's names. Nothing was removed: there was no older dock.

Routes, all under the existing guard (loopback Host, Origin allow list, `X-FormCAD-Token`; the websocket takes `?token=`): `GET /api/terminal` (tabs, `contract`, `available`), `GET /api/terminal/contract`, `POST /api/terminal` (shell), `POST /api/terminal/agent` (`kind` claude, codex, cursor or antigravity, optional `prompt` and `resume`), `POST /api/terminal/{id}/restart`, `DELETE /api/terminal/{id}`, `WS /api/terminal/{id}/stream`, `GET /api/agents/status`. One line in `ROUTERS` mounts them at `/api`; the lifespan binds the terminal service to the loop and closes every tab at shutdown.

Clause 11, the per-tab token. The HTTP route takes it cleanly: `guard.issue_run_token(("/mcp",))` is the scoped token macros already use, so a tab's token passes the guard for `/mcp` and nothing else, and `guard.revoke_run_token` in the session's `on_close` ends it. The MCP server then runs the call as `agent`, the lower trust class, with no granted folders. Claude Code gets `--mcp-config` with the header written as `${FORMCAD_TAB_TOKEN}`; Codex gets `-c mcp_servers.formcad_tab.env_http_headers` naming that variable; Cursor gets a `formcad_tab` entry in `.cursor/mcp.json` of its working folder that reads `${env:...}`; Antigravity gets the token itself in `.agents/mcp_config.json` of its own folder (`<data>/agent-tabs/antigravity`), mode 0600, every other entry kept, taken out on close only when it still holds that tab's token. The token is never on a command line. The stdio server was not needed.

The child's environment drops `FORMCAD_TOKEN`, `FORMCAD_GRANTS`, `FORMCAD_MACRO_NAME` and every key of `backend/.env`; the window's session token is never put in it. Tabs start in the projects folder, so no file of the repository is written by a tab (`.cursor/mcp.json` in the checkout is tracked and stays as it is).

Clause 10: `agent_lookup.py` 1.0.1 is the family's shared finder, copied byte for byte (SHA-256 62667f9b...6d51, checked by `agents check`). Override variables `FORMCAD_CLAUDE_BIN`, `FORMCAD_CODEX_BIN`, `FORMCAD_CURSOR_BIN`, `FORMCAD_ANTIGRAVITY_BIN`; no path setting. `signedIn` is asked of Claude Code only and is null for the others. The answer is cached 60 s; `?fresh=true` skips the cache. A program that is not found carries `note`, the finder's sentence of where it looked.

Clause 13: codes, not words. 424 is "not installed" (the finder's sentence), 409 is busy (16 tabs at most, or no program to restart), 503 is no pseudo-terminal library, 401 and 403 come from the guard and are refusals. Clause 14: `tabs()` lists shell and the four programs; a missing PTY library answers 503 on open. Clause 16: `contract` "2.2.0" in `GET /api/terminal`, `GET /api/terminal/contract` and `GET /api/agents/status`.

Clause 1, refused not unknown (core/guard.py `is_run_token`, `Guard.__call__`). A tab's token is not the session token and passes the guard only at `/mcp`. Presented anywhere else, in the header or as a websocket's `?token=`, it was at first answered 401 like a wrong token; the review of another app's port asked for 403, so a program in a tab is told plainly that it may not open, restart, close or attach to a terminal, read the agent status or use any window route. The guard now answers 403 (websocket close 4403) to any known scoped token outside its routes, which also covers a macro's token outside `/api/docs` and `/api/commands` (401 before; nothing in the tree read that code). An unknown or revoked token stays 401. Checked live with an Antigravity stand-in tab open: its token answered 403 on `POST /api/terminal`, `POST /api/terminal/agent`, `GET /api/terminal`, restart, `DELETE`, `GET /api/agents/status`, `GET /api/capabilities` and `POST /api/docs`, the websocket attach was rejected with 403, `tools/list` on `/mcp` answered 200; after the tab closed, `/mcp` and `POST /api/terminal` answered 401.

Left out: clause 12 (Chat; formCAD has no chat pane and no stream-json engine for Claude, and porting one is its own change), the Activity tab (no feed of outside agents' commands), clause 15 (consent; formCAD gates outside agents by the token and the trust class, not by a question in the window). Clause 1 holds for agents: a tab token or a macro token is refused on the terminal routes. A local process that reads `<data>/session.token` is treated as the window, as everywhere else in this engine.

Freezing: `setup/freeze_backend` adds `--hidden-import ptyprocess` (macOS, Linux) or `--hidden-import winpty --collect-binaries winpty` (Windows); `freeze_backend.bat` adds the Windows pair. Not run here: a freeze.

Checked on Linux with a fresh data folder: a shell tab echoed `dock-ok` back through the websocket and closed; the status answered four programs, Claude Code found at `/opt/node22/bin/claude` through the login shell's PATH (this machine has it) and the other three not installed with their install lines; with a stand-in `agy` on PATH the status said installed, the Antigravity tab opened, `.agents/mcp_config.json` held `formcad_tab` with the tab's token at mode 0600 beside a kept entry, `tools/list` on `/mcp` answered 200 with that token and `/api/terminal` answered 401, and after close the entry was gone, the other entry kept and the token answered 401. Not checked: macOS, Windows, the real Codex, Cursor and Antigravity programs.

## MR1 v0.19.6 the app carries the record of the published mesher 6.2.2608 and can be asked to install it (app/data/mesher-licence.json, release/formCAD-binaries/records/mesher-licence.json, app/routers/sim.py)

formcompiler's `binaries` workflow released the mesher on 2026-11-20: `mesher-6.2.2608` in `nodemode-dev/formai-binaries`, published, four archives (Netgen 6.2.2608 with the OpenCascade 7.8.1 build of netgen-occt). As with the kernel in KR1, the two records of that release are in the tree byte for byte: `app-mesher-licence.json` as `backend/app/data/mesher-licence.json` (1,723 file rows) and `mesher-licence.json` as `release/formCAD-binaries/records/mesher-licence.json`, whose SHA-256 dfcc3141...60c7 is the one in the release's `SHA256SUMS`. Both workers are released now, so `preflight.py --app-data backend/app/data --published` passes without `--kind`. The old record's `netgen` and `occt` fields are gone with the copy; nothing reads them (the versions shown come from the worker's hello).

The record alone would not have been enough. `mesher.start_install()` existed and nothing called it: no route and no control. `POST /api/docs/{doc_id}/sim/mesher/install` now starts it, beside `GET /api/docs/{doc_id}/sim/status`, which already reported the mesher with `installing` and `progress`. The route needs the session token and an open document like its neighbours. The interface's side is in `frontend/HASHOUT_LOG.md` SM28.

The staging folder names in `mesher.py` stay `.partial-<random>/unpacked/`: the longest path in the mesher archives is 89 characters, so the Windows limit KR1 dealt with is not near (a user name would need 59 characters).

Verified on Linux against the public release: an engine with a fresh data folder reported "No mesher is installed", took the install request, downloaded the archive, checked every file and reported the mesher available, version 6.2.2608, source `installed`. The installed frozen worker meshed a 10 by 20 by 30 box from a BREP file into 1,234 nodes and 5,025 tetrahedra with a volume of 6,000.0. Not verified on macOS or Windows.

Still open: each record's `licence_gates.needs_review` list.

## KR1 v0.19.5 the app carries the record of the published kernel 8.0.1 (app/data/kernel-licence.json, release/formCAD-binaries/records/occt-licence.json, app/services/kernel.py)

formcompiler's `binaries` workflow built, signed, notarized and released the kernel on 2026-11-19: release `occt-8.0.1` in `nodemode-dev/formai-binaries`, published, four archives. The app installs a worker only when every file of the download matches the record it shipped with, and until now that record said `release: null` with no files, so the app answered "No kernel build is published yet". The two records of that release are now in the tree, byte for byte as the release holds them: `app-occt-licence.json` as `backend/app/data/kernel-licence.json` (27,147 file rows for four platforms, 6.2 MB) and `occt-licence.json` as `release/formCAD-binaries/records/occt-licence.json`. Both files are machine-written by the release tools and were copied, not edited; their SHA-256 values are 34ebf698...a427 and 78817041...b621, the second being the one in the release's `SHA256SUMS`. `preflight.py --app-data backend/app/data --published --kind kernel` passes. The old record's `occt` field is gone with the copy; nothing reads it (the OpenCascade version shown in the app comes from the worker's own hello).

The engine reads the record only when an install starts, not on each status request, so its size costs about 40 ms once per install.

One code change, in `run_install`: the staging folder is `.p-<random>/u/` where it was `.partial-<random>/unpacked/`. The longest path in every platform's archive is 126 characters (a type stub that jedi carries; jedi comes with IPython, which build123d depends on). On Windows the install unpacks under `%APPDATA%\FormCAD\kernel\<staging>\<archive folder>\`, and with the old names a user name of 27 characters or more pushed that past the 260-character limit, so the unpack would fail on those machines. The shorter names move that to 40 characters. Folders that start with a dot are still skipped when the engine looks for an installed release. `mesher.py` has the same staging names and was left alone: no mesher release exists to measure.

Verified on Linux, end to end against the public release: an engine with a fresh data folder took `POST /api/kernel/install`, downloaded the Linux archive, checked all 7,061 files, installed it under `kernel/8.0.1`, and left no staging folder; the installed frozen worker answers hello (OpenCascade 8.0.1, OCP 8.0.1.1, build123d 0.13.0, 134 methods) and makes a 10 by 20 by 30 box that reports one valid closed solid with six faces. Run twice, before and after the staging rename. Not verified: the same on macOS and Windows, where the download is a different archive and the signed program runs under the system's own checks; the first packaged build of 0.19.5 on each is that test.

Still open: the mesher has no release, so `mesher-licence.json` stays empty and meshing stays unavailable; the record's `licence_gates.needs_review` list has six entries the release document says to settle.

## WB5 v0.19.4 the worker build moved to formcompiler (.github/workflows/binaries.yml removed, docs/RELEASE.md, .docs/wiki/Building-and-Releasing.md, release/formCAD-binaries/README.md)

WB4 made this repository's `binaries` workflow ask for the family's signing values as repository secrets. That was the wrong direction: the family's rule, written in formcompiler's own rules file, is that signing identities and keys are formcompiler secrets only and no app repository is ever asked for one. The owner said so the same evening ("update formcompiler to include the binaries ci (it should be there anyways)"). formcompiler v0.19.0 carries the workflow now, and this repository's copy is deleted so there is one.

What moved and what stayed. formcompiler owns the runners, the values, the keychain and notary key, Windows signing with its own tool, and the draft release in formai-binaries with its release credential. Everything that knows what a worker is stayed here and is called by path from there: `setup/install_<kind>` with `FORMCAD_LOCKED=1`, `setup/freeze_<kind>`, `release/sign/macos_sign.sh` (`sign`, `notarize-folder`), the tools in `release/formCAD-binaries/tools/`, `release/sbom/formcad_sbom.py`, the two lock files and the records. docs/RELEASE.md lists them so a rename here is known to be a change there. Everything WB3 and WB4 fixed in those scripts still applies; only the WB4 items that lived in the workflow file itself (the `credentials` job, the secret names, the Azure action, the signature step, the handshake step) are gone from here, and formcompiler's workflow has its own form of each.

Two differences from WB4 on the formcompiler side, by its house practice: on Windows only the worker's own `.exe` is signed, not every library beside it; and `BINARIES_REPO_TOKEN` is not used, the release credential is formcompiler's GitHub App installed on formai-binaries.

`release/sign/windows_sign.ps1` is no longer called by any workflow. It stays as the certificate-file route for a person signing by hand.

A second gain: formcompiler is a public repository, so worker builds there do not draw on the private repository's Actions minutes.

Not verified: formcompiler's workflow has not run. Until its first run completes, no worker has been signed, notarized or released by anything.

## WB4 v0.19.3 the publishing run checks its credentials first, and the signing path is made to match what exists (.github/workflows/binaries.yml, setup/lib.sh, setup/freeze_kernel, setup/freeze_mesher, release/sign/macos_sign.sh, release/sign/windows_sign.ps1, docs/RELEASE.md)

Run 3 of the `binaries` workflow (kernel 8.0.1, `publish` checked for the first time, 2026-11-19 about 21:32 EST) lost three of its four build jobs in 2 to 6 seconds at the "Refuse to publish without signing credentials" steps; Linux built. Nothing was wrong with the builds: the formCAD repository has no signing secrets. The family's Apple and Azure values live in formcompiler's `signing` environment, and a secret of one repository cannot be read from another. They have to be set again here.

That run was the first time anyone exercised the publishing path, so the rest of it was read line by line before the owner pays for another. Changes, each for a stop that had not been reached yet:

1. A first job, `credentials`, on Linux. With `publish` checked it lists every missing name in one message, checks that the tag is free, and proves `BINARIES_REPO_TOKEN` can write by creating a draft release `probe-<run>-<attempt>` in formai-binaries and deleting it. `build` needs it. Before, the token was first used in the last step of the last job, after every macOS and Windows minute had been spent, and the reply to "missing secrets" came one platform at a time. Reading the repository's `permissions.push` was not used as the write test: it reports the token owner's role, not what a fine-grained token may do.

2. The names and formats are formcompiler's. The notary key was `APPLE_API_KEY_B64` here and is `APPLE_API_PRIVATE_KEY` there (same content: base64 of the `.p8` on one line). The three `ARTIFACT_SIGNING` values are repository variables there and were read only as secrets here; they are read from either now. The per-job refusal steps stay, with the new names.

3. Windows signing through Azure called `artifact-signing-cli`, a program that Microsoft's page of signing integrations does not list (it lists SignTool with a plug-in library, the GitHub action, the Azure DevOps task, PowerShell and the SDK). The step would have failed after the Windows build. The Azure route is now Microsoft's own action, `azure/artifact-signing-action@v2` (inputs taken from its README: tenant, client and secret, `endpoint`, `signing-account-name`, `certificate-profile-name`, `files-folder` with filter `exe,dll,pyd`, recursive, SHA256, timestamp `http://timestamp.acs.microsoft.com`). `windows_sign.ps1` keeps only the certificate-file route. A step after either route fails the job when any `.exe`, `.dll` or `.pyd` is `NotSigned` or `HashMismatch`; other states (an untrusted root on the runner, for one) are printed as notices and do not fail, because a false alarm there would cost a whole run.

4. macOS: the worker folder is flattened before it is signed, as the app's engine has been since 0.18.1 (FZ1). PyInstaller leaves the Python library inside `_internal/Python.framework` behind links; the engine built that way, with its links turned into plain copies by the app bundler, was refused by Apple's notary (whether the linked shape would pass when signed file by file is not known), and the flattened shape (one regular file at `_internal/Python`, no framework, no links, every Mach-O signed on its own) is the one that is known to pass. `fc_flatten_frozen` in `setup/lib.sh` does it, called by `freeze_kernel` and `freeze_mesher` on macOS before their handshake, so the handshake proves the flattened program starts. It differs from `flatten_engine` in one way: where the engine's version stops the build if a file links `Python.framework`, this one looks first and, if any file does, changes nothing and says so. An unflattened worker still runs; a stopped freeze would cost a run. `flatten_engine` in `setup/freeze_backend` was left exactly as it is, since it is the proven path of the app build; the two functions overlap on purpose until the next time that file is opened.

5. `macos_sign.sh submit`: the pipe from `notarytool` into `tee` now ends in `|| true`. The workflow's shell runs with pipefail, so a non-zero exit from `notarytool` ended the script before it could fetch and print the notary log, and the reason for a refusal would have needed a second paid run to see. The status test on the JSON that follows is what decides.

6. A step "The staged program still answers" runs the hello handshake on the staged folder after signing, on all four platforms. Until now the only handshake was in the freeze, before the Developer ID signature and the hardened runtime were applied.

Verified here: the workflow parses and its three jobs chain credentials, build, assemble; the credentials script names all 13 values when none is set and only the token when only the token is missing; the handshake step passes for a stand-in program and fails for one that exits; `fc_flatten_frozen` on a stand-in folder with stub `file`, `otool` and `codesign` leaves no link and no framework, and leaves everything alone when a file links the framework; `bash -n` on the four shell files. Not verified: anything on macOS or Windows, `windows_sign.ps1` (no PowerShell here), the Azure action, the Apple signing and notary calls, and the draft release step. The first publishing run is still the first real test of all of them.

Costs worth knowing: every `.exe`, `.dll` and `.pyd` of the Windows folder is one Azure signature per run; files that arrive already signed by their makers (the Python and Visual C++ runtime libraries) get our signature in place of theirs.

## WB3 v0.19.2 the four worker builds agree on their documents and their lock (kernel/requirements_kernel.lock new, mesher/requirements_mesher.lock new, setup/install_kernel, setup/install_mesher, .github/workflows/binaries.yml, release/formCAD-binaries/tools/stage_notices.py, release/formCAD-binaries/tools/preflight.py, release/sbom/make_lock.py)

The second run of the `binaries` workflow (kernel 8.0.1, `publish` off, 2026-11-19 evening EST) built all four programs and stopped in the assemble job: "parts/x86_64-pc-windows-msvc.record.json: documents differs from the first record". `merge_records.py` requires the four per-platform records to agree on everything but their own `targets` entry, and the `documents` block holds the hash of `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt`. `stage_notices.py` wrote that file with `Path.write_text`, which on Windows turns every line end into CRLF, so the Windows hash differed. Both writes in that tool now pass `newline="\n"`.

Reading the rest of the assemble job against what the four runners really produce found three more stops behind the first one. None had been reached yet; each would have ended the next run.

1. The lock. Each runner made `requirements.lock` from its own venv with `make_lock.py`, and the merge requires one lock. The four could not agree: the Windows resolution has `colorama` and lacks `pexpect` and `ptyprocess`; the file was CRLF on Windows; and `fc_python` prefers `python3.14`, which the Mac runners have from Homebrew, so the Mac programs were being built on 3.14 while the record says 3.12. The lock is now a committed file per worker, `kernel/requirements_kernel.lock` (60 pins) and `mesher/requirements_mesher.lock` (3 pins). The workflow makes the venv itself with the Python 3.12 that `setup-python` provides, then runs the install script with `FORMCAD_LOCKED=1`, which installs exactly the lock with `--no-deps` and runs `pip check`; the release's `requirements.lock` is a copy of the committed file. Without `FORMCAD_LOCKED` the install scripts behave as before, so a developer's `./formCAD install` is unchanged. The kernel lock is the union of four `uv pip compile --python-version 3.12 --python-platform <triple>` resolutions of `requirements_kernel.txt`; the four agree on every version, and the union differs from each only by the three platform-only packages above, which are pure Python and install anywhere. `packaging` is in the lock although `make_lock.py` skips it as a build tool: `marshmallow`, which the kernel's packages pull in, needs it at run time. `make_lock.py` writes LF now too, for the `sbom` workflow and `release/dry_run`.

2. `preflight.py --published` required both records to be filled in, so the first release of either worker could never pass: publishing the kernel fails on "mesher-licence.json: not filled in". `--kind kernel|mesher` limits that one rule to the program being released. Without `--kind` the rule is as it was.

3. `preflight.py` required a `sha256` on every upstream source of a released record. The kernel record's `cadquery-ocp-novtk` entry points at a PyPI project page, which `pack_release.py finalize --fetch-sources` rightly never downloads or pins (it only fetches `.tar.gz` URLs). The rule now applies to `.tar.gz` URLs, the same test the fetch uses.

To change a pin: edit the requirements file, remake the lock for the four targets (`aarch64-apple-darwin`, `x86_64-apple-darwin`, `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`, `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`) and commit the union. The lock is not hash-pinned; the release record's own file hashes are what the app checks.

Verified here, on Linux only: the kernel lock installs into a fresh Python 3.12 venv with `--no-deps`, `pip check` reports nothing, and `OCP`, `build123d` 0.13.0 and `rhino3dm` import. The whole chain (notices, record, pack, merge, finalize with a local `.tar.gz` standing in for the upstream source, check, app record, preflight with `--published --kind kernel`) passes for four stand-in programs, and the same preflight without `--kind` still answers "mesher-licence.json: not filled in". Not verified: any of it on a GitHub runner. That the lock installs on macOS and Windows rests on the per-platform resolutions, not on an install. Signing, notarising and the draft release step have never run.

## WB2 v0.19.1 the worker build asks for a Mac runner that still exists (.github/workflows/binaries.yml)

The first run ever of the `binaries` workflow (kernel 8.0.1, `publish` off, 2026-11-19 about 20:25 EST) lost its Apple silicon job before it started: "The configuration 'macos-14-arm64-us-xl' is not supported". GitHub retired the macOS 14 runner image on 2026-11-02, and the matrix asked for `macos-14`. The other three jobs (macos-15-intel, ubuntu-22.04, windows-2022) were accepted and started.

The Apple silicon entry now asks for `macos-15`, the oldest Apple silicon image GitHub still runs, and records `macOS 15` as the worker's minimum, the same floor the Intel entry already had. `macos-latest` was not used: it is macOS 26, and the label moves when GitHub moves it, which would raise the floor without a commit. The minimum is recorded in the release record and notes and is not enforced by the app.

Left as it is and worth knowing: the app's own entry and `tauri.conf.json` still say macOS 14 for the application, while the worker says 15. Nothing was checked on a real macOS 14 or 15 machine.

## FZ1 v0.18.1 the macOS engine leaves the freeze with no links and no framework (setup/freeze_backend)

The first two Mac builds of 0.18.0 through formcompiler (Apple silicon and Intel, 2026-11-19) built, signed and reached Apple's notary, and both were refused with status Invalid for the same two files and nothing else: `Contents/Resources/engine/_internal/Python` and `Contents/Resources/engine/_internal/Python.framework/Python`, "The signature of the binary is invalid". The other 200 or so Mach-O files of the engine passed.

Cause. PyInstaller 6 keeps a framework Python as `_internal/Python.framework/Versions/3.14/Python` and adds links to it: `_internal/Python`, `Python.framework/Python`, `Python.framework/Resources` and `Versions/Current`. `sign_engine` signs regular files only, so it signed the library once, inside the framework, where codesign binds the signature to that framework (its Info.plist and resource seal). Tauri copies resources one file at a time: a linked file arrives in the app as a plain copy and a linked folder is left out. The two copies of the library therefore sat outside the version folder their signature names, and Apple refused them.

Fix, ported from formvis (`flatten_engine` in its `setup/freeze_backend`, Apache-2.0, in use there since 1.99.5 with the same PyInstaller pin, 6.22.3). On macOS, after the engine is copied to `src-tauri/engine` and before it is signed: the library is copied to `_internal/Python` as a regular file and signed ad hoc to drop the framework binding, `Python.framework` is removed, and every remaining link is replaced by a copy of what it points at. The step stops the build if a link is left, if any other `.framework` is in the engine, or if a Mach-O file still names `Python.framework` in its load commands. `sign_engine` then signs the library like any other file, and `--selfcheck` runs the flattened and signed engine, so a library the bootloader cannot find fails the build here and not on a user's machine. The bootloader is given the base name `Python` (PyInstaller `building/api.py`, `python_lib_name=os.path.basename(...)`), which is why `_internal/Python` is the one place it must be.

Linux and Windows are untouched: they have no framework and no notary, and the Linux bundles were built with the links as they are.

Not a fault: the Intel log's `Hidden import 'mujoco' not found` and `"mujoco": "missing"`. `requirements.txt` leaves mujoco out on Intel Macs by a marker, because it has no wheel there.

Checked here, on Linux only: `bash -n`, and the function run against a hand-made copy of PyInstaller's framework layout with stand-in `codesign` and `otool` (no link left, the framework gone, `_internal/Python` a regular copy, linked files and folders kept as copies, a second framework and a dangling link each stop it). Real `codesign`, `otool` and the notary run for the first time in the next Mac build.

## DM1 v0.17.0 demo documents and their routes (demos/router.py new, data/demos/*.fcad new, main.py ROUTERS)

Three demo documents ship in `app/data/demos`, so the frozen engine carries them through the existing `--add-data backend/app/data` and `router.py` finds the folder the way `services/kernel.py` finds `kernel-licence.json`, relative to the module file. `GET /api/demos` lists `{id, title, line, kind}`. `POST /api/demos/{id}/open` goes through `service.new(name, path)`, the call `POST /api/docs` makes with a template, and answers `doc.summary()`: the document is an untitled copy with an empty path, so Save asks for one and the shipped file is never written. An unknown id is 404 `{"detail": "Unknown demo <id>."}`; the token guard applies as everywhere. The files are mode 0644 because the engine's own save writes 0600.

The documents. `drafting` is new: an invented 14 by 8 m single-storey plan (three rooms, hatched walls, door and window blocks, furniture, eight dimensions at DIMSCALE 50, seven layers) drawn through `app.automation.ops.execute` on a copy of `fixtures/sheet.fcad`, which gives the A3 layout, the `TITLEBLOCK` block and a 1:50 viewport; no fixture had a believable dimensioned drawing with a filled title block that needs no kernel. `part` is `fixtures/part-core.fcad` (13 features) rebuilt with the dev kernel and saved after one `/scene` fetch, so the container holds the solid and its cached display mesh. `graph` is `fixtures/graph/plate-holes.fcad` saved as kind `graph` (the fixture's manifest said `drawing`) with the baked body's mesh cached. The fixture kept every node at 0, 0, so the editor drew the eight nodes on top of each other; a left-to-right layout by wire depth (columns 300 apart) was written through `PUT /api/docs/{id}/graphs/G1` and the engine's own save. That save also set the manifest title to the file name, `graph`; the card and the opened document take their names from `router.py`, so nothing shows it.

Without a kernel worker (none is published yet): the part opens shaded from the cached mesh and its tree reads all `ok`, and the graph shows its baked plate and section curves; the first edit or rebuild of the part turns features F2 to F13 to `error` and empties the 3D view until undo, and solving the graph fails its box and hole nodes with the kernel message. The drafting demo needs no kernel. A gusseted bracket to match the README hero was not built for time; part-core stands in.

Checked: `python -m compileall -q app formcad_engine.py`; `formcad_engine.py --selfcheck` (app ok, 71 routers); both routes with the token (200), an unknown id (404) and no token (401); each demo opened once in the window.

## KB1 v0.17.0 worker downloads come from formai-binaries (data/kernel-licence.json, data/mesher-licence.json, services/kernel.py, services/mesher.py, requirements.txt, formcad_engine.py)

`nodemode-dev/formCAD-binaries` never existed. The owner keeps one binaries repository for the family, `nodemode-dev/formai-binaries` (formcut-binaries, renamed), and formCAD's records, licences, templates and tools live in its `formcad/` folder; the worker releases keep their tags `occt-<release>` and `mesher-<release>`, which cannot collide with `ffmpeg-<release>`. The two shipped records name the new `source` and `url`, the two "no build is published yet" messages name the new repository, and `release/formCAD-binaries/` in this repository keeps its folder name (so `binaries.yml`, `release/dry_run` and the tools still resolve) and mirrors that `formcad/` folder. Record contents did not change beyond `source` and `url`; `tools/preflight.py --records ... --app-data backend/app/data` passed before and after. Until the rename happens and a first release is recorded, the app answers that no kernel build is published, as it did before.

`watch_parent` in `formcad_engine.py` now watches the process id the shell passes in `FORMCAD_SHELL_PID` when there is one (POSIX only, `os.kill(pid, 0)`), and the parent process otherwise, so the server exits when the shell is gone even if the frozen launcher between them was force-killed. Same code as formvis's engine.

## I15 v0.14.0 integration and audit fixes (ecadout, schem, pcb, cmd/commands/ecadchecks.py)

The enclosure link reads a part file only if that document is already open or its real path (links followed) lies inside the board's folder; macro and agent callers get the open-document case only, and the confinement runs before the existence check so a missing file and a refused one read alike. Schematic devices carry at most 500 pins (128 for macro and agent callers), checked on the raw list before it is cleaned. Export options go through the same plus or minus 1 mm margin check as the settings route, in `board_view`, so every export route and command shares it. KiCad import: the edge chain indexes segment ends on a grid of the match tolerance (linear instead of quadratic), edge segments are capped at 4000 (the board's own point cap), malformed nodes answer as a file error instead of a 500, and quoted names use a plain backslash table instead of `unicode_escape`. The slow part left after that was the self-intersection test of the board outline (pairwise, pure Python, 55 s at 3000 points); it now filters pairs by bounding box in numpy first (1 s at 4000 points, same answer on a square, a bowtie and a star). Autorouter passes are clamped to 20. Two typed commands, `ecaderc` and `pcbdrc` (read only, no file, so also MCP tools through the catalogue), report the schematic rule check and the board design rule check. `POST /ecad/link/sync` accepts an empty body object. The KiCad user strings no longer name the product, and `NOTICE.md` gains a short section that the Gerber, Excellon, IDF and `.kicad_pcb` formats are written from published specifications. Left open: CSV cells starting with an equals sign, plus, minus or at sign are written raw by the four routing CSV writers (the older exports share it; one helper for all writers is the right fix), relative export names resolve against the engine folder, writes are not atomic and follow an existing symlink, stored headers are not checked against the record caps on open, and the Gerber header carries the app version, which is 0.13.0 until the release commit. The generative safety factor is from the hexahedral re-analysis of the thresholded grid, not a re-meshed tetrahedral model; staircase corners raise the peak, so the stated factor errs low.

## I16 v0.15.0 integration and audit fixes (weld, mold, costing, toolbox, release)

Audit A15 fixes. One shared CSV guard `core/csvsafe.py` (leading = + - @ tab CR get an apostrophe, plain numbers stay) now serves the weld cut list and the cost currency cell; the inspection writer keeps its own copy of the same rule. The cost currency is letters or one currency symbol. The costing profile counts as the user's only when a rate, price, material or process value differs from the example values; setting currency, batch or volume alone keeps the example label (the label is data, not a flag the first key clears). CSV readers (library, results, emissions) turn `csv.Error` into their own error; a library import that fails removes the family it created. The user library is capped at 16 MB on save. Report writers refuse a relative file name and write through a `.part` file and `os.replace`; the toolbox part `stem` is reduced to its base name. DFM stops after `MAX_SECOND` and says which parts were not checked. Mold margins above five times the part diagonal are refused and the split volume check is relative to the smaller of the block and ten times the part (a huge block can no longer hide a part sized residual). Weld cut list rows no longer split on member notes; the notes of a row are joined in its description. New route `GET /{id}/weld/library-profile?family&row` turns a row of the user toolbox library (rect_tube, round_tube, flat_bar, angle) into an inline weld profile so the document keeps its own sizes (the library file is never read at rebuild); channel and I sections have different parameters and are refused. The toolbox proportion defaults (head 1.5 d, hex across flats 1.6 d, nut 0.8 d, washer 2 d outside and 0.15 d thick, the 10 x 26 x 8 bearing) are FormCAD decisions for a generic shape, not a standard; user sizes override them. Release: `dry_run` resolves `--out`, refuses the repo, its parents, the home folder and `/`, refuses a non empty folder without its sentinel file and defaults to `mktemp -d`; the build job of `binaries.yml` holds no secret at job level (each secret sits in the one signing step) and fails a publish run without signing and notary credentials; the x86 macOS runner is `macos-15-intel`; `sbom.yml` passes the version through the environment, validates it and fetches the schema from the 1.6 tag; release numbers are validated in `fcbin`. Not done: actions pinned by tag, not commit (same as ci.yml, decide once); notarytool `--password` and `security import -P` still put a secret in the argv on an ephemeral runner (the API key route does not).

## WM1 weldments, Phase 40, v0.15.0 (weld/*, routers/weld.py, cmd/commands/weld.py, kernel/kernel_weld.py)

One feature `weld_frame` builds every member as its own solid in one compound (members stay separate so the cut list is exact); gussets and end caps are lists inside it. The skeleton is a list of 3D lines in the params, not a 3D sketch entity (none exists). Profiles: a sketch id (listed in refs `profiles`) or inline generators with user sizes (no section table ships). Member frame: w along the line, u = reference x w (reference Z, or X for a vertical member), rotation about w, `locate` shifts the profile. Treatments are recomputed each rebuild: miter (two members, plane perpendicular to u1 minus u2 through the node), butt (top priority member runs through and is extended by the lower member's profile support; lower members end on the top member's nearest profile face plane), cope (lower end overshoots then is cut by the filled envelope of every higher member), none, and a per-end `trim_to` plane for free ends. Priority: members that pass through the node first, then longest, ties by creation order, or an explicit `order` in a junction override. A member end on the interior of another member is a tee and always butts or copes. Cut angle: angle between the member axis and the cut plane normal (0 is square, 90 degree miters give 45); rotation is the tilt direction in the profile frame; rows group by profile, length within the tolerance (0.5 mm default), both angles and the rotation difference between the ends (invariant to turning the member about its axis). Length is long point to long point from the solid's vertices. Gusset: triangle plate in the plane of the two members, cut by their filled envelopes. End cap: only on square ends; match, rectangle or circle. Records in drawing table `weld` (added to `TABLE_NAMES`). Caps: 200 members, 600 parts, 40 members per command for macros and agents. No command reads or writes a file or changes settings, so nothing new in barred or guard. Drawing table: `weldcuttable` copies the BOM table path (`extra.weldcut` keeps tolerance and columns, `weldcutupdate` refreshes). Not done: planar butt of skewed round sections is a tangent plane, three-member butts trim only against the top member, mitre does not correct for different profile sizes, trim to a face or body, end caps on cut ends, offset on a matching cap, interface.

## TB1 toolbox, inspection and MBD, Phase 41, v0.15.0 (toolbox/*, routers/{toolbox,inspection}.py, cmd/commands/toolbox.py, main.py, cmd/barred.py, core/guard.py, fixtures/toolbox/*)

No size table of any standard ships (R27): twelve parametric generators (socket and hex screws, hex nut, washer, pin, bearing by boundary dimensions, I, channel, angle, rectangular and round tube, flat bar) take user sizes; omitted proportions default to labelled multiples of the nominal size. Closed-form volumes are checked against the kernel (all within 1e-4). Parts are written as `.fcad` files named by designation plus a parameter hash (reused when present) through `core/outputs.checked`. Local frame of fasteners: axis +Z, seat plane z=0 with outward normal -Z, shank towards -Z, so one rule places screw, washer and nut: concentric to the hole axis, coincident (or distance when an offset is given) between the seat plane and the target face; the kernel face normal is outward, so no flip was needed. The user library is `<data dir>/toolbox/library.json` (200 families, 5000 rows); the CSV importer reads a path (.csv .tsv .txt, 2 MB, text only, 500 rows and the granted-folder check for macro and agent callers), maps columns by name or by an explicit mapping, converts inch to mm, reports bad rows (first 50) without failing the file. Inspection is header `INSPECTION`: characteristics collected from PMI (dimensions with value plus tolerance band, tolerance frames as an upper zone) and drawing dimensions (measurement times DIMLFAC is not applied; tolerance only from the dimension overrides DIMTP and DIMTM, style-level tolerances are not read); numbers are stable and reorderable, repeated characteristic keeps one number with qty actual cells; verdict pass, fail, partial, pending. Drawing balloons use the DR2 `balloon` entity tagged `insp:K<n>` in `part_key` and are regenerated, not duplicated. The report is FormCAD's own three-section layout (HTML, CSV; HTML escaped, CSV cells that start with a formula character are quoted). MBD views are header `MBD` (camera, PMI id list or all, types, balloons); `assign facing` keeps items whose planar reference faces look at the camera (dot above 0.2) and items on non-planar features; the MBD report lists lost items, missing datums, unused datums, dimensions without tolerance and items in no view. Guard: `toolbox` in `MACRO_DENIED_WRITE`; `toolboxinsert`, `toolboximport`, `inspectionexport` in `MACRO_BARRED`; an outside agent needs `folder` for `toolboxinsert`. Export and results import use route segments `export` and `import`, so macros are already refused.

## MD1 mold tools, Phase 40, v0.15.0 (mold/*, routers/mold.py, cmd/commands/mold.py, kernel/kernel_mold.py, fixtures/mold/part.fcad)

- Mold work is commands and routes that create plain bodies on layer `Mold` (source `{op: mold, role}`), not part-tree features: a split yields several bodies and must be re-runnable; each route runs in one transaction so undo removes them. Settings live in header `MOLDSETTINGS`, the last split record in `MOLDRESULT`.
- Draft: per sample, draft = asin(n . pull); side cavity (n.pull > 0), core (< 0), vertical (|s| < sin 0.01 deg), straddle for mixed faces. Undercut = a ray from the sample (offset out along the normal) along its own side is blocked; vertical samples need both directions blocked. Steep = |draft| below the minimum. Caps: 1500 faces, 24000 samples, grid at most 5 for macro and agent callers.
- Parting line = edges between faces of different sides (silhouette along the pull), chained into wires. Only a flat parting plane is built: its height is the median silhouette edge height (or given, or the middle of the part); a non-planar silhouette sets `planar` false and `wrong_side` lists faces left on the wrong side. Straddle faces are not split; the plane splits the block. Non-planar and stepped parting surfaces are a gap.
- Shut-offs: faces of (plane section of block minus part) that do not touch the block boundary, i.e. discs inside through holes at the parting plane. Side holes are reported as undercuts, no slide is built.
- Split: block minus part, cut by two half boxes at the parting height; invariant block = core + cavity + part and zero core/cavity overlap checked with adaptive volume integration (eps 1e-9; default Gauss volume was off by 0.2 percent on NURBS from non-uniform scale).
- Shrinkage: percent per axis (0 to 30), convention `multiply` (scale 1 + s) or `divide` (1 / (1 - s)), about the part bbox centre unless an origin is given, applied before parting; the check uses the scaled part. No material table ships. Source: R27 section 2, DERIVED.

## TB2 costing, DFM, design checker, sustainability, Phase 41, v0.15.0 (costing/*, routers/costing.py, main.py router line, core/guard.py MACRO_DENIED_WRITE, fixtures/toolbox/assembly-costed.fcad)

Routes only, no commands, so `cmd/barred.py` is unchanged; `costing` joins `MACRO_DENIED_WRITE` (macros read, never write) and the report route carries `export`. Settings are header values `COSTING`, `DFMRULE`, `DESIGNRULES`, `EMISSIONS`. One finding schema for DFM and the design check. Cost template per R27 (material + rate x time + setup/batch + tool wear (machining) + tooling/volume, then defect, overhead, margin); every shipped rate and price is an example placeholder and the estimate says so until the user saves a profile. Times: CAM op summaries and slicer plate times are reused when present, otherwise removal volume over a user rate, sheet cut length and bends, or volume over a build rate. Sheet material uses blank area over a user utilisation (no nester). DFM thresholds cite Hubs and Protolabs (single sources flagged, never called a standard); hole depth and internal corner radius come from `pl_inspect` cylinders, wall from `pl_wall` sphere samples kept 1 mm from face edges (a FormCAD measurement setting), sheet holes from the flat pattern (hole-to-bend measured to the bend line minus half the allowance). Not measured and listed as skipped: thread length, cavity depth to width, flange minimum. Molding and additive reuse PL1 and AM1 checks on the open part only. Toolbox parts (note starts "Toolbox part") count as purchased. No emission factor table ships: the UK conversion factor material rows were not read, so the table is user-entered or imported from a user CSV; caveats print in every report. Caps: 300 distinct parts (60 for macro and agent callers), 100 rules, 500 factors, 1 MB import, 8 MB report.

## GD1 generative design, Phase 37, v0.14.0 (gen/*, routers/gen.py, cmd/commands/gen.py, topo/simp.py build_chain, sim/registry.py MODULES, core/guard.py, main.py, fixtures/gen/bracket.fcad)

A generative study is an FE1 study of type `generative` (registered next to topology) whose `options.generative` holds the study matrix; regions, grid and symmetry keys are TO1's and resolved by `topo.study.resolve` unchanged (the `generative` key is stripped first). One edit in `topo/simp.py` `build_chain`: a filter item with `last: true` goes after the projection and before the passive stage and receives `cls=` (the cell classes); everything else in TO1 is untouched. Manufacturing filters are plug-ins in `gen/filters.py`, registered in `topo.filters.FILTERS`: `overhang` (layer-by-layer smooth min and max with the 3 by 3 support stencil, about 45 degrees; P=30, Q=P+log10(n) as the published smooth approximation, written from the idea, exact adjoint checked by finite differences to 8 digits) and `access` (cumulative smooth maximum from each tool or draw side, minimum over the directions; one direction is a height field from the stock, two opposite directions forbid any void trapped between two solids on a line). Casting is `access` along both senses of the draw axis (single draw, no undercut) and milling is `access` over the user's directions, so they differ by the directions given; draft angle is not modelled. Cutting is TO1's extrusion tying.

Soft filters steer the optimiser; before every verification the thresholded design goes through a hard repair (`gen/rules.py repair`: remove unsupported cells layer by layer for additive, fill the line hull for milling and casting but never into obstacles, majority vote per line for cutting) so the stated rule holds exactly, and the safety factor includes the repair. Safety factor is yield over the peak von Mises of the design cells (preserve cells excluded) from the re-analysis of the thresholded outcome on the design grid (hexahedra, staircase corners overstate the peak; p99 stored beside it). Mass is the closed marching-tets mesh volume times density. The tetrahedral re-mesh and FEA of the outcome solid is NOT done (it would need the load selectors re-resolved on the new body); GENAPPLY with `to_solid` and `unite_preserve` produces the exact united solid for a later study. Minimum member size: filter radius equals the requested cells, below 2 cells refused; the check is a 2-cell cube opening on the result, reported as `thin_share`, not part of `manufacturable` (diagonal staircases fail it on coarse grids).

min_mass objective: per method a bracketing search on the volume fraction, up to `passes` full SIMP runs (later passes warm-started with half the iterations), picking the lightest pass within the stress limit; with too few passes the pick can be heavier than the true minimum (measured spread between runs: see GD1.md). Methods run in sequence inside one cancellable job (the research suggested worker processes; the engine box has two cores shared with other agents, so no gain was measured). Jobs reuse `topo.jobs` (one job per document, `topojob` events with kind `generative`). Caps: runs 24 / 8, passes 6 / 3, seconds 3600 / 600, grid caps TO1's.

## I14b v0.13.0 audit fixes (services/mesher.py, mesher/formcad_mesher.py, sim/{jobs,study,meshio,npz,report}.py, topo/{study,outcome,problem}.py, flow/{stokes,su2,register}.py, simnl/register.py, cmd/commands/sim.py)

Mesher: the size estimate now adds the cells a finer face control brings (4 tetrahedra per boundary triangle of the controlled face at its size, measured 3600 against 3700 estimated at 0.5 mm on a 10 by 10 face) so a 0.05 mm control is refused in a second under the agent cap instead of meshing until the 300 s timeout. `mesher.call` serialises calls with its own lock and waits for it at most the call timeout; the state lock is held only for the lookup, so `status` answers at once (0.02 s against 84 s). A job names itself as the call owner and `jobs.cancel` kills the worker only when that job owns the running call; a cancelled job stays cancelled when the killed worker raises. The mesher exits quietly on a closed pipe. Stored arrays: `sim/npz.checked` reads the zip directory before `np.load` and refuses a member over 300 MB or a total over 400 MB (a 522 KB file that expands to 512 MB is refused in 1 ms); used for results, stored meshes and topology outcomes (`gen/run.py` has the same pattern, not touched). Memory: the flow solve and the topology study compare their estimate with free memory (70 percent rule of `fem.check_size`). Flow jobs pass the caller class to the Stokes and SU2 caps; explicit `max_steps` is 400000 for macro and agent jobs. SU2 settings take only a full path to an existing file, a non-object option list and an `ITER` above the cap are refused, and the program runs with a short environment. Report files need a full path. The mesher download stops at 600 MB. `problem.build` of topo (dead) removed. Commands `simcontact` and `simcontrol` (the second can attach the control to a study with `study`), both plain record writes like `simload`.

## EL3 board outputs and links, Phase 38, v0.14.0 (ecadout/*, routers/ecadout.py, cmd/commands/ecadout.py, kernel/kernel_board.py, formcad_kernel.py, core/outputs.py, core/guard.py, cmd/barred.py, main.py, fixtures/ecad/{board-linked,enclosure}.fcad)

Writers are pure functions over one neutral board description (`ecadout/view.py` `clean`) built from the `ECAD_PCB` records (`fromboard.py`), so the formats never read the board model directly and share the size caps (400k tracks, 60k pads, 3M fill points, 600 mm on a side interactive; a tenth of that for macros and agents, which are refused on every export route anyway). Gerber: metric, 3.6 coordinates, origin at the outline bounding box minimum for every file, one aperture dictionary per file numbered from D10, `G75` multi-quadrant arcs, X2 file attributes (`FileFunction`, `FilePolarity`, `SameCoordinates`, generation software, creation date, project id), aperture function per aperture, `TO.N`, `TO.P`, `TO.C` on copper objects, zone fills as regions (holes joined by a zero-width cut-in, falling back to a clear-polarity region only if the bridge would cross another contour), mask drawn dark with negative polarity, vias tented by default, a separate `Profile` file with draws on a 0.1 mm aperture, rounded rectangles, rotated rectangles and rotated obrounds as macro apertures. Not done: silkscreen text (only footprint lines, arcs and circles are drawn), IPC-2581 (schema terms unread), ODB++ (confidential, R26), block apertures and step-and-repeat. Whether Ucamco's terms need written permission is still the owner item from R26; the exporter does not extend the format. Excellon: one file per plating and layer span, tool list in the header, decimal coordinates, `METRIC,TZ`, slots as `G85`, the X2 function as a `; #@!` comment; the slot syntax and the comment attributes come from memory of common output and the R26 gap, not a read specification. IDF 3.0 (single mirrored source, R26): board outline loops, drilled holes, placement and a library of box outlines; the arc angle is written on the end point of the segment (unverified against the spec text), outer loop counter-clockwise and cutouts clockwise. The 3D board is built in the worker (`kernel_board.py`): the outline extruded by the stackup thickness minus through holes, plus one compound of component boxes or cylinders from the footprint body placeholders; blind and buried vias do not cut the body. The enclosure link keeps a reference (part document by id or by a path relative to the board file, body, face index or direction, offset, tolerance, hole size limit) in the header value `ECAD_LINK`; the worker returns the chosen planar face loops with arcs as bulges and circles flagged, the engine flattens arcs within the tolerance (the board model stores polygons), circular inner loops under `hole_max` become mounting holes (skipped when a board hole of the same size already sits there), and the part counts as changed when its body blob hash changes (`stale`). Sending the outline back writes a closed polyline sketch `BoardOutline` into the part; when the board outline is still exactly the pulled one the original arcs are written, otherwise the polygon. KiCad: a small tolerant s-expression reader and a writer for outline, tracks, vias and footprint pads, switched by `ECAD_LINK.kicad.enabled` (default off, per document), reader capped at 8 MB, 600k tokens and 64 levels, Y flipped; footprints are not imported. No new dependency; the independent Gerber reader used to check the files lives only in the scratch folder. `.zip` was added to the expected-content table of `core/outputs.checked` so an existing zip can be replaced.

## EL2 PCB engine, DRC and autorouter, Phase 38, v0.14.0 (pcb/*, routers/pcb.py, main.py router line, fixtures/ecad/board.fcad)

The board is header value `ECAD_PCB` (schema 1) of an ecad document: outline and cutouts, stackup, rules, netlist copy, placed parts with their pads copied in from the library (so a board survives a library edit), track segments, vias, zones, keepouts, mounting holes. Header values keep undo, save and reopen free; every route is one `doc.transaction`. No new dependency: numpy for the router grid, pyclipper (already shipped) for zone fill and offsets. No commands were added (routes only), so `cmd/barred.py` and `core/guard.py` are unchanged: no route writes a file or starts a process; the long jobs (route, DRC) take the lower caps from `jobs.restricted` for macro and agent callers (grid 1.5M cells, 120 nets, 1500 pads, 600k expansions, 40 rips, 120 s) and counts of records are a quarter of the window limits.
Rules: four named profiles (generic, JLCPCB, PCBWay, OSH Park), each value carries its source and the date read (2026-11-03), values the pages did not give fall back to generic and say so. Deviations from R26's derived default: clearance and track width 0.16 (not 0.15) so the profile is looser than OSH Park's 0.1524, via pad 0.7 (not 0.6) because 0.6 over a 0.3 drill leaves a 0.15 ring below the profile's own 0.20 ring. JLCPCB via pad is left to the generic value: its page asks for 0.10 mm over the drill, below the 0.20 ring used here. Overrides and per-net classes (width, clearance) are editable and validated; the effective value is always recomputed from profile plus overrides. Clearance between two nets uses the larger class clearance.
Zone fill (computed, not stored): zone polygon clipped to the outline inset by the edge clearance, minus cutouts, pour keepouts, every other-net item grown by the clearance and earlier higher-priority zones; opening by the minimum width removes slivers; same-net pads follow the zone connection (or the pad override): thermal leaves a gap ring and joins by spokes (default 4 at 45 degrees, width 0.3, gap 0.3), solid merges, none treats the pad as another net; islands under the minimum area or touching no same-net copper are dropped. Connectivity of a pad, track end or via to a pour is a point-in-fill test on its centre or end points.
DRC: clearance and short (exact capsule and polygon distances, not the grid), width, annular ring, drill, hole to hole, edge clearance, outside board, keepouts, pour clearance (offset and intersect), courtyard overlap (warning), unplaced parts (warning), unconnected (ratsnest by union-find over touching copper and pours, minimum spanning tree between groups). Roundrect pads are checked as rectangles (slightly conservative); zone fill is checked against other-net items but pours are not checked against tracks of the same net.
Autorouter (two copper layers only, refused otherwise): 8-direction A* on a grid of 0.1 mm inside the outline box; obstacles are rasterised distance fields of every item inflated by the clearance plus half the widest track plus a quarter pitch (tracks) or the via radius (vias, plus hole to hole), edge and keepouts; one conservative half width for the whole job (the widest net class), wider nets are drawn at their class width; own-net pads and copper are subtracted while a net routes. Nets in shortest half-perimeter order, multi-pin nets grow a tree to the nearest unconnected group, layer 1 prefers horizontal and layer 2 vertical (cost 1.45 the other way), via cost 2.5 mm, pad stubs run from the pad centre to the first free node inside the pad. Rip-up: a failed net is searched again with other routed copper made passable at a 40 mm penalty; the nets that path crosses are ripped, the failed net routes, the ripped nets are queued again; a net gets at most `passes` (6) tries, the job at most `max_rips` (60, cap 200 or 40) rips, then the net is reported failed with the reason. Only unconnected nets are routed (`mode: unrouted`), `all` clears the chosen nets first; user tracks are obstacles for other nets, never ripped and never reused as sources. Commit checks that the board did not change during the job and is one undo step. Post-processing is only collinear merging: staircase smoothing was not built, paths are octilinear with small jogs. Pad-escape stubs are not individually DRC checked by construction; the DRC pass after routing is what proves the result.
Auto placement is a first version: shelf packing of courtyard boxes inside the outline inset 2 mm, connected parts adjacent by a breadth-first order, connectors first, holes and footprint keepouts avoided; no orientation search beyond a quarter turn for parts wider than the board.
Known limits: no arcs, no blind or buried vias in the router, no differential pairs or length tuning, no push and shove, no per-layer rules, no zone to zone clearance between different nets on different layers (not needed), pour thermal spokes are not individually validated against the minimum width after the opening step (they are added after it).

## RT2 pipes, tubing and electrical schematics, Phase 39, v0.14.0 (pipe/*, schem/*, routers/{pipes,schem}.py, cmd/commands/routes_pipes.py, doc/tables.py, main.py, cmd/barred.py, core/guard.py, fixtures/route/{piping,schematic}.fcad)

Two new tables `pipes` and `schem` (`TABLE_NAMES`); every record keys on `name` = its id, so user names live in `label` (runs, specs) and `wire` (schematic wires; the API still says `name` and maps it). Own numpy code, no new dependency, no kernel change: fittings and straights use the existing `primitive`, `boolean` and `transform` calls. Run geometry is the centreline model of R26 3.7: each interior vertex of a tube becomes an arc of the centreline radius, setback R tan(theta/2) trims both neighbours, developed length is straights plus arcs (or, when a spec carries a bend table, the interpolated 90 degree developed length scaled by angle over 90, linear extrapolation flagged `table_extrapolated`); it agrees with RT1's `routing.path.fillet_polyline` to 1e-3 on the fixture polyline. LRA rows: L is the straight from the previous bend end (from the tube end for the first), rotation is the signed dihedral between consecutive bend planes about the straight, 0 to 360, first bend 0 (planar S 180, planar C 0, 3D corner 90, checked). Springback is a spec angle added in `machine_angle`; setback of the bender mark is the tangent length, not a vendor formula. Bends below the spec minimum radius or straights below the grip length are flagged, not blocked; a segment too short for its two setbacks is an error and refuses the bender table. Pipe style runs use elbows with centre-to-end R tan(theta/2) from a spec factor (1.5 times OD, an example), tees and reducers by distance along a segment, flanges as end fittings that shorten the adjacent piece by their thickness; the fitting proportions are generic examples, no standard or catalogue table ships (R26 gap), the label says so. Route points are 3D points or `{component, local}` anchors resolved through the assembly transform at solve time, so moving a component moves the route. Fittings: elbow is a torus ring cut by a wedge prism, tee two cylinders, reducer a cone, flange a disk with bore and bolt holes, each placed by a 12 number matrix; elbow, flange and reducer volumes equal their closed forms. The solve view also returns `segments` in the RT1 route segment shape (kind `pipe`, points, fillet path pieces, millimetres).
Schematics: device symbols are FormCAD's own outlines on a 5 unit grid (16 kinds), wires join pins, nodes are union-find over pins. Wire numbers: one per node, default template `{prefix}{sheet}-{seq:02}`, tokens prefix, sheet, seq, class, from, to, net with optional widths; manual numbers are kept and other nodes skip them; wire names default to the number with A, B suffixes when a node holds several wires; names are unique per document. The wire list compares to RT1's harness by wire name (from and to as unordered pairs, gauge, colour), reading another open document with `harness_doc`; junction and earth ends compare as the bare reference. `draw` regenerates the 2D entities tagged by xdata (undoable), one sheet per 500 unit block. Limits: 2000 points window and 400 for macros and agents, 500 runs, 5000 devices, 20000 wires, 100000 entities (agent values lower). Guard: `pipes` and `schem` in `MACRO_DENIED_WRITE`; `pipeexport` and `schemexport` in `MACRO_BARRED`. IEC 60445 is paywalled and was not read; the scheme is FormCAD's own.

## EL1 ecad document kind, libraries, schematic engine, ERC, netlist, Phase 38, v0.14.0 (ecad/*, routers/ecad.py, doc/templates.py KINDS and the `ecad` template, main.py router line, fixtures/ecad/board-schematic.fcad)

The design lives in the header value `ECAD` (schema 1: parts, sheets, user libraries, ERC settings); the board is header `ECAD_PCB` (EL2) and the link is `ECAD_LINK` (EL3). Header values keep undo, redo, save and reopen free, and every edit is one `doc.transaction`, so a failing batch rolls back whole. No new table name, because `TABLE_NAMES` is shared and a header needs no migration. `Document.kind == "ecad"`; `templates.KINDS` and the `ecad` template are the only shared-file edits besides the router line.
Decisions where R26 is silent: Y up, millimetres, rotation counter-clockwise in 90 degree steps, mirror flips X before rotation. A pin joins a wire anywhere along it (end or middle); a wire end that lands on the middle of another wire joins only through a junction (R26), otherwise ERC reports `wire_end_on_wire`. Local labels join per sheet instance; global labels and power symbols (the net is the part value) join everywhere; hidden `power_in` or `power_out` pins join the net named by the pin. Net name priority: power symbol, then global label, then local or hierarchical label (shallowest sheet first, then alphabetical); several names of equal rank are reported by `multiple_names`. Unnamed nets are `Net-(REF-PIN)` after their first node. Hierarchy is flattened per instance path (`/H1/H4`); a child sheet may be instantiated several times and `instances` on a part override its reference per path. A bus is a drawn object plus a label `NAME[a..b]`; members are real nets only through their own labels, the bus checks membership (`bus_member_mismatch`) and a bus sheet pin joins `NAME<i>` local labels across the sheet boundary.
Footprints are FormCAD's own parametric land patterns (families chip, dual, quad, sot, header, dip, terminal, radial). The pad rule is the part's terminal or lead dimensions plus an editable toe, heel and side fillet plus a tolerance (fab plus placement) split between toe, heel and side; there are no IPC tables and no claim of IPC compliance. The chip size code maps to the body by the metric code (0603 inch = 1608 metric = 1.6 x 0.8 mm), a naming equivalence, not a land table. Ids are readable (`CHIP-0603`, `DUAL-8_P1.27_S6`); the preset list is generated on demand, user footprints and symbols are stored in the header. The default fillets, tolerance and the lead and body sizes of the presets are our own illustrative defaults, to be checked against a datasheet by the user.
ERC pin-conflict matrix and rule list are derived from common practice (R26 says no standard was fetched): output with output or power output is an error, tri-state or open-collector against an output a warning; severities are per rule (`error`, `warning`, `ignore`). Passive pins count as drivers, so a floating input behind a resistor is not caught (known weakness). Power inputs need a power output, an output pin or a power symbol on the net.
Security and bounds: all routes need the session token; the only file writes are the netlist (`.json`, `.net`, `.txt`) and BOM (`.csv`) exports, forced through `core/outputs.checked`; the path segment `export` already puts them in `MACRO_DENIED_LATER`, so macros cannot write files. Caps: 5000 parts, 50000 drawn items, 999 sheets, 2000 sheet instances, 32 hierarchy levels, 2000 pins per symbol, 1000 pads per footprint, batches of 2000 operations (200 and 5000 items for macro callers). No commands were added, so `cmd/barred.py` is unchanged. KiCad interop belongs to EL3 per the revised ownership and is not built here.

## RT1 wire harness, Phase 39, v0.14.0 (routing/path.py, harness/*, routers/harness.py, cmd/commands/harness.py, main.py, fixtures/route/*)

Harness lives in header value `HARNESS` (schema 1), so undo, save and reopen come with the document; one command `harness` with an `action` argument (programmatic) and `harnessexport` (typed, asks for a file name). Group `harness` is outside `MACRO_GROUPS`, so macros are barred by default; agents get lower caps (300 nodes, 600 segments, 1000 wires, 100 cables, 128 pins) and every path goes through `args_refusal` and `core/outputs.checked` (`.csv` forced, 8 MB bound). Routes under `/api/docs/{id}/harness`, token required.
Two single-source rules are flagged in every report (`flags`): the bundle diameter packing factors (Glenair table, one manufacturer source, linear interpolation between rows, mixed sizes by average diameter, 0.025 in for a braid shield, plus twice the covering thickness, refused above 300 conductors unless the segment gives a diameter) and the minimum bend radius of 10 x bundle diameter (one secondary source). Both are editable (settings `min_bend_factor`, segment `diameter` and `min_radius`, cable and wire `min_radius`). Wire insulation thickness default 0.25 mm per side is an unsourced editable example; conductor diameter from the AWG formula, not a table.
Decisions: a bend tighter than the minimum is flagged, never blocked; the path of a segment is a fillet polyline (exact length, default) or a centripetal spline through the clips (length from sampling, curvature from three-point circles); a fillet that does not fit between neighbours is reduced and reported. Wire routes use the shortest chord path through the segment graph (chord lengths, because true lengths depend on the diameter which depends on the routes); cut length = path + both allowances unless a length override is given. Clips and waypoints define the path; no obstacle avoidance and no interference check of the swept tube in this version. A cable counts as one conductor of its jacket diameter in a bundle; its cores show in the wire list with the same route.
Flattening keeps lengths: trunk is the longest path by length (or a given segment chain), branches leave at the branch angle (default 90 degrees, alternating sides) with a greedy search over other angles when a branch comes closer than the clearance to a drawn segment; cycles are cut (the segments not in the shortest-path forest are listed in `cut`) and islands stack below. A branch is turned with `rotate` about its parent, which preserves every length. The drawing is rebuilt on its layout (generated entity handles are stored and erased first, one undo step): sheet chosen from A3 to A0 by fit, else A0 with a reduced board scale (labels keep true lengths), tables for wire list, from-to, connector pins and segments.

## TO1 topology and shape optimisation, Phase 35, v0.13.0 (topo/*, sim/registry via topo/register.py, routers/topo.py, cmd/commands/topo.py, core/guard.py, main.py, fixtures/sim/topo-bracket.fcad)

SIMP written from Sigmund 2001 and Andreassen 2011 (no DTU code read). Voxel hexahedra of one size (own 24 by 24 element stiffness, own assembly with a hoisted sparsity pattern, void cells dropped from the system: 5 times faster), modified SIMP with Emin 1e-9, density filter (kd-tree weights), Heaviside projection with beta doubling, OC with bisection on the volume of the filtered field, move limit 0.2 divided by beta over 2 (the limit cycle at beta 8 otherwise never converged), plateau stop. Filters are plug-ins (`topo/filters.py`: `register(name)`, forward and backward, chain with passive regions last); symmetry (orbit averaging) and extrusion are two of them; the next release adds overhang filters there. No new dependency: marching tetrahedra (own, Kuhn split, watertight, outward) replaced scikit-image; surface level is bisected so the smoothed mesh volume matches the verified solid within 1 percent (a fixed level 0.5 lost 36 percent on thin members).
Stress loop (R25: 60 percent reduction gave 1.63 times the stress): after each SIMP pass the design is thresholded at the level that matches the target volume, cut to the part connected to the supports, and re-solved with true material and no SIMP interpolation. The checked stress is the maximum element average von Mises over design cells (preserved cells holding loads excluded), the same metric on the full solid ("before"). Over the limit: optional stress relief (densify cells over 95 percent of the limit, off by default: on the bracket it moved the peak elsewhere and did not help), then more material by interpolation on log stress between the infeasible and feasible volume fractions. A pass that loses the load path counts as infeasible instead of raising. Reported honestly when the target mass cannot be kept (`met_target` false).
Grid size is fitted so the body bounding box is near a whole number of cells. Voxel stress at re-entrant corners is mesh dependent (stair steps); the number is a verification screen on a coarse grid, not a refined stress. Direct SuperLU up to 14000 unknowns, above that a conjugate gradient with a recycled incomplete factorisation (refreshed every 8 iterations); caps in `topo/study.py CAPS`: 30000 or 12000 unknowns, 12000 or 3500 cells, 300 or 100 iterations, 8 or 3 passes, 1800 or 300 s (window or macro and agent).
Studies are FE1 `study` rows of type topology or shape; the general run (which meshes) is not used, so `topo` has its own jobs and routes; the `topo` segment is in `MACRO_DENIED_WRITE`. Commands take no paths and start no process except TOPOAPPLY with `to_solid` (same kernel path as MESHTOBREP), so `barred.py` needs no entry; macro and agent calls get the lower caps through `jobs.restricted`. Shape study: Nelder-Mead on document parameters, each candidate rebuilt with `force=True` inside a transaction that is rolled back (without the force the part cache returned the previous body after a rollback), mass exact from the rebuilt body, stress and compliance from the voxel solver; the best values are applied by TOPOSHAPE (one undo step). Selectors with `faces` indices may move when a parameter changes; `point` and `plane` are safer.
"A load is not attached to the remaining material" (U16, 200 by 10 by 10 beam): not a bug in the answer but a bad refusal. A 50 percent cut of a 5 by 5 cell section can sever the beam, and the first version raised instead of treating that pass as infeasible. Now it is recorded in `outer_attempts[].failure`, the loop adds material, and the messages say what to change (less reduction, finer grid, preserve region on the load path).

## CF1 flow, cooling, plastics, Phase 36, v0.13.0 (flow/*, routers/flow.py, sim/records.py, main.py, NOTICE.md, fixtures/sim/pipe.fcad)

Studies `flow`, `cooling`, `fill` register through `flow/register.py` (loaded by `sim/registry.MODULES`); three load types added to `sim/records.py` (`flow_inlet`, `flow_outlet`, `injection_gate`). Stokes: Taylor-Hood on the mesher's curved P2 tets (SU2 or straight tets read 5 to 11 percent high in R25), developed inlet profile from a Poisson solve on the flat inlet face scaled to the flux through the P2 inlet, direct solve, unknown cap 90000 window and 30000 for macros and agents, 24 KB per unknown memory estimate. Pipe fixture: 1.5996 Pa against 1.6002 Hagen-Poiseuille (-0.04 percent) on 1701 tets. SU2 link (`flow/su2.py`, same pattern as `interchange/dwg.py`: configured path, off by default, argument lists, timeout, 256 MB log cap, config keys allow-listed): measured with SU2 8.5.0 (conda-forge, scratch install) on the same pipe with a prescribed parabolic inlet file (8 columns: x y z T speed dir): pressure gradient 4.2 times too high at 1.5 mm cells, 1.9 times at 1.0 mm (25k cells); `INC_NONDIM= INITIAL_VALUES` is needed (DIMENSIONAL gave 140 times at 3 mm); so SU2 does not reach 5 percent on laptop meshes of straight tets and the exit rests on the fallback. Fill: Hele-Shaw P1 triangles, control volume front, power law by Picard in log space; peak pressure is reported at 98 percent fill because the last closing step spikes with the mesh (plate: 8.68 MPa vs 9.0 formula Newtonian, 10.51 vs 10.70 power law); midplane = skin facing the pull direction offset half the ray-measured thickness. Pack and warp are formulas only (slab freeze time, free-plate curvature), no PVT.

## FE2 nonlinear, contact, events, fatigue, laminates, Phase 35, v0.13.0 (simnl/*, routers/simnl.py, main.py, fixtures/sim/nonlinear.fcad)

Own numpy and scipy code, no new dependency. P2 tetrahedra (4 point rule) for statics, P1 one point with row-sum mass for explicit (P2 row-sum mass is negative, R25). Newton with load stepping, step halving, SuperLU. J2 radial return with isotropic hardening (linear or piecewise curve); formulas of R25 checked against the Simo and Hughes radial return as remembered and, stronger, the consistent tangent matches a central difference of the stress update (2e-8) and uniaxial results are exact to 1e-15. Neo-Hookean total Lagrangian from the R25 energy; the hand tangent matches finite differences (6e-11). Contact acts on all face nodes (corner and midside) with equal tributary areas against a rigid plane or, for two faces of the part, closest-point projection on the corner triangles with an interpolated vertex normal (per-triangle normals made nodes on diagonals flip and Newton stalled at 1e-5). Tangent keeps projection weights frozen, so convergence is linear with deformable pairs. Augmented Lagrangian multipliers advance only between rounds; the force residual criterion is relative (1e-4). Friction is Coulomb with penalty regularisation on rigid planes only. Convergence floor 1e-10 E V^(2/3) so a rigid translation does not look unconverged. Loading pseudo time `loads_first` ramps forces in the first half and prescribed displacements in the second. Adapter module `simnl/register.py` is what `registry.MODULES` imports. Rainflow is the ASTM E1049 three point method, checked on the standard nine point example. Caps: 20000 elements and 45000 unknowns (4000 and 9000 for macros and agents). The routes sit under the existing `sim` path segment, so macro tokens are refused writes. Slip: I used pkill by pattern once on a scratch process of my own.

## FE1 linear simulation, Phase 34, v0.13.0 (sim/*, services/mesher.py, routers/sim.py, cmd/commands/sim.py, data/{sim-materials,mesher-licence}.json, solid/blobs.py, doc/tables.py, mesher/*, setup/{install,freeze}_mesher*)
