Checks. `check.run` reads the last built body of the part, one `pl_inspect` call (draft per face about the pull, concave edges between planes, concave cylinders under 180 degrees of span, face adjacency) and one `pl_wall`, then compares with the rule: wall thin (error), thick, variation and adjacent ratio (warnings, info), draft below the minimum plus texture allowance, rib thickness ratio (info between the minimal and slight sink ratios, warning above), rib height and draft, boss outside diameter 2 to 3 x screw, boss height against 5 x outside diameter, boss wall ratio, snap strain against the permissible (error), root fillet, sharp inside edges and small inside fillets, lip and welded wall minimums. Draft uses the absolute angle (the pull side is not told apart). A snap hook is exempt from the draft check with an info note.

Bounds and trust. Samples 20 to 4000 (sphere 4000), 800 for a macro or agent caller (`guard.CALLER`); display vertices at most 60000 for ray and 6000 for sphere (6000 and 1500 for a macro or agent, defaults 20000 and 4000), checked before any work; the worker caps the same numbers and the face count of the body at 4000. The routes and commands read and write only document data and use no file argument (the worker files for the colour map are private temp files the engine writes), so `cmd/barred.py` and `core/guard.py` need no entry. Check and colour map calls are synchronous inside the document lock, bounded to about 10 s at the defaults; they are not cancellable jobs.

## X2 3D interchange, Phase 25, v0.10.0 (kernel/kernel_xde.py, interchange/stepio.py, fileunits.py, files.py, meshes/threemf.py, gltf.py, ply.py, exchange.py, pmi/*, routers/pmi.py, cmd/commands/pmi.py)

STEP and IGES go through XDE in one new worker module. `xde_read` and `xde_write` replace `open_step`, `save_step`, `open_iges` for the engine (the old methods stay for callers that exist). `STEPControl_Controller.Init_s()` and `IGESControl_Controller.Init_s()` run before any `Interface_Static` call; without it the schema request is silently ignored (R22). Schema default AP214IS, AP242DIS whenever the document carries PMI (the engine switches an ap214 or ap203 request to ap242 and says so), AP203 on request; the file header is read back and returned so the dialog can show the string actually written. Both `Transfer` and `Write` statuses are checked. Validation properties (area, volume, centroid) are computed with GProp and attached to every part before writing.

Location handling. A body shape of the worker carries a `TopLoc_Location` (loaded or moved shapes). XDE cannot attach sub-shape labels, colours or layers to a located shape, so the writer stores the unlocated shape as the definition, keeps the location on the component, and builds the face maps from the unlocated shape; face index order is the same, so persistent ids map by index. Found and fixed during the F1 run: before, every PMI item of an imported body was dropped and the colours were lost.

Assembly structure. A part document keeps the structure of an imported STEP in each body: `source.path` (the names of the enclosing assemblies) and `source.def`. Export rebuilds the tree from the paths. An assembly document (AS1) is walked with `assembly.walk.instances`: each instance is a group node, each body of its part a leaf with the instance world matrix (translation scaled to millimetres); parts that appear several times share one definition. Shared definitions of an imported file become separate bodies in a part document (decision: the part document has no instance concept), so a round trip of a file with repeated parts writes them as separate definitions. IGES keeps no assembly names; compounds read back as `Name 1`, `Name 2`.

Units. The file unit is read by the engine (`fileunits.py`): STEP through the unit entities named by the first `GLOBAL_UNIT_ASSIGNED_CONTEXT` (a conversion-based unit contains an SI millimetre as its reference, so the first match is wrong), IGES through the global section (parameter 14 and 15), 3MF through the `unit` attribute, glTF is metres. A mismatch with the document unit is a warning plus a choice: `convert` (default) reads the numbers in millimetres and the document shows them in its unit, `keep` reads the file in its own unit (`xstep.cascade.unit`) and scales the shapes by the document millimetres per unit, so the numbers stay. A new document opened from a file takes the file unit. PMI values are in document units in the engine and in millimetres in the worker; on write the worker scales dimension values and tolerances by the chosen file unit (OCCT does not scale PMI itself: a value of 20 in an inch file reads as 508 mm).

PMI. Stored in the header value `PMI` (not a table, so no change to `TABLE_NAMES` and the DXF writers). Records reference features by persistent id; `state` is `lost` when an id no longer resolves, the item stays and is not written. What OCCT 8.0.1 did in the probe and the F1 run: dimensions (distance, diameter, radius, angle), tolerances (all 14 FormCAD characteristics with diameter or sphere zone and M or L modifier) and datums are written and read back with the same values; a datum reference on a tolerance is written only when the datum object has a position (`SetPosition`), which the writer sets from the first frame that names the datum; a size dimension keeps only its first feature, so a thickness is written as a linear distance between its two faces and returns as a distance (the report notes it); a dimension of the two-face kinds with one face is refused by `Write`, so it is dropped with a reason. Not written: presentation PMI, saved views, datum targets, dimension modifiers, tolerance modifiers other than M and L, per-face colours, materials. Not recovered on read: dimension types outside the FormCAD five (toroidal sizes, curve length, oriented, with path), tolerance types that have no FormCAD kind; each is listed with a reason in the read report. After a write that carries PMI the engine reads the file again in the worker and reports written against recovered counts (`read_back`).

Mesh formats. 3MF, GLB and PLY are our own writers and readers; no new dependency, so no NOTICE row. 3MF: zip of three parts, `unit` attribute, `basematerials` per colour, one object per body; the reader refuses entity declarations, limits the archive (entries, total and single part size), flattens components and build items with transforms, reads `basematerials` and `colorgroup` colours, warns for required extensions it does not implement (production extension components are skipped). glTF: GLB with one buffer, POSITION accessors with min and max, uint32 indices, a metallic-roughness material per body (metal factor 1 when the material name holds a metal word, roughness 0.4 or 0.8; colour from the body, linear sRGB), a root node turning Z up to Y up, metres; no NORMAL attribute so viewers shade flat; a `.gltf` name writes the GLB form. The reader takes GLB and `.gltf` (buffers as data URIs or files in the same folder only), triangle lists, strips and fans, scales by 1000 and turns Y up to Z up; it refuses Draco, meshopt and quantization. PLY: binary little endian or ascii writer with a `comment units: mm` line, reader for ascii and both binary byte orders, polygons fan-triangulated, vertex colours (uniform becomes the body colour, otherwise the mean with a note), unit read from a `units:` comment. Solids are tessellated for these three at linear deflection 0.01 mm and angular 0.1 rad (the F1 values), raised by a factor 4 up to three times when a body passes 4 million triangles.

Warnings. STL, OBJ and PLY warn that the format carries no unit (previously a note for STL and OBJ) and check the part size: a body outside 0.1 mm to 10 m after the chosen unit gets the unit that would bring it between 1 mm and 2 m as a suggestion, never applied silently. `POST /api/interchange/inspect` returns unit, schema, mismatch and the mesh suggestion before the import dialog.

Caps and trust. Bodies per STEP/IGES file 5000 (200 for a macro or agent caller), PMI items 5000 (500 for macro or agent), 16 features per item, 1000 items drawn or listed per call, mesh and PLY element counts and sizes checked from the header before allocation (20 million triangles, 512 MB file, 64 MB glTF JSON), 3MF archive 512 MB with no single part above 256 MB. Every new route needs the token; `/api/interchange/*` lies outside the document prefixes a macro token is scoped to, and the document file routes `import`, `export`, `saveas` stay in `MACRO_DENIED_LATER`, so a macro cannot read or write interchange files; the PMI commands are in group `solids`, take no file argument and are available to macros and agents (they only change document data, with the lower item cap). `.3mf .glb .gltf .ply` joined `FILE_EXT` of `cmd/barred.py` for the typed-prompt path check and `EXPECT` of `core/outputs.py` for overwrite checks (zip, binary, text or binary).

Left. Lib3mf is not a dependency, so the 3MF manifold check is our own naked and non-manifold edge count plus the orientation test, not `IsManifoldAndOriented`; datum targets and presentation PMI are not written; face and edge colours are not carried; a document opened from a file with repeated parts has no instances.

## I10, v0.9.0 audit fixes (graph/model.py, solver.py, sdk.py, runner.py, values.py, optimize.py, geo.py, nodes/*, routers/graph.py, core/guard.py, services/kernel.py, sheetmetal/bend.py, docs/GRAPH_FORMAT.md, fixtures/graph)

Finding 1 (graph load quadratic). `Graph.load` no longer tests each wire with a fresh `TopologicalSorter`: it adds every wire, then `settle_loops` runs one Kahn pass over the node graph. Acyclic input (the normal case) is done there. Only when nodes remain stuck does it re-add the wires between stuck nodes in `order` and disable each one that closes a loop, using a depth first walk restricted to those nodes, so the set of disabled wires equals the old rule. `connect` keeps a per-wire refusal through `cycle_through(src_node, dst_node)` (a walk from the destination back to the source, giving the loop's node names). `edges` and `successors` use dicts (the old `a not in preds[b]` list test was also quadratic on fan in), `order` and `downstream` look positions up in a dict instead of `list(self.nodes).index`, and `apply_definition` expires downstream nodes with one multi source walk instead of one `downstream` call per expired node. Caps: `model.MAX_NODES = 5000` and `MAX_WIRES = 20000`, checked in `Graph.from_definition` before any node is built, so import, PUT, the run route, the MCP tool and the CLI share them; `add_node` and `connect` refuse past them (code `limit`); `runner.MAX_NODES` now reads the model value. Measured: 4990 node chain loads in 0.35 s (was 85 s), 2000 nodes 0.08 s (was 12 s). Written in `docs/GRAPH_FORMAT.md` under Limits. The load happens before the run clock, which is fine now that it is linear.

Finding 2 (no memory bound). `sdk.MAX_NODE_ITEMS = 2,000,000` and `MAX_TOTAL_ITEMS = 5,000,000`. `SolveContext.account(outs)` runs after every node: one node's output items above the first, or the running total of all cached output trees above the second, fails that node with `TOO_MANY_ITEMS` (the kept results of other nodes survive; `ctx.total` starts from the nodes already computed). `spend` counts calls and the items of multi nodes during matching, so a cross match stops at two million results instead of running to the timeout. `reserve(n)` is called by series, range and random with the requested count before they allocate, so 400 series nodes now stop after five (2.5 s, was 35 s and 3.9 GB). `geo.counted` bounds Divide Curve By Count (100,000) and Divide Surface (1000 per direction); linear and polar arrays were already at 10,000. `runner.solve_private` caps `timeout_s` at `AGENT_TIMEOUT = 60` when `guard.CALLER` is set (MCP agents and macros); the window keeps the 3600 s ceiling. A memory rlimit for the runner was not added: the item budget bounds it and an rlimit in a thread of the engine process would bound the whole engine.

Finding 5. `kernel.probe` retries the spawn and hello once at once when a worker that was running has died, before it arms the 30 s back-off. SIGKILL during a facade solve now loses the one node in flight and the next solve runs 48 of 48 on a new worker after 1.6 s (it failed for 30 s). Not done: re-running the interrupted batch inside the same solve.

Finding 6. `Graph.from_definition` turns TypeError, ValueError, AttributeError, KeyError, OverflowError and RecursionError from raw JSON into a `GraphError`, a node position beyond 1e12 is refused, `nodes` and `wires` must be lists. `runner.load_graph` also catches those (the `deepcopy` of a deeply nested list happens there) and `runner.run` turns TreeError and the same set raised while reading inputs and stored trees into a `RunError` (schema). `FAILURES` in `routers/graph.py` gained TypeError, OverflowError and RecursionError so routes answer 400. The 250 case fuzz of the audit, four seeds: no unhandled exception.

Finding 7. `values.decode` and the handle branch of `from_wire` refuse a stored Shape ("a file cannot carry kernel shapes"): shapes live only in solve output and never in a file, so a forged id cannot reach the worker from file or run input. Side effect: `tree.from_json` of a tree containing a Shape now raises (GC1's scratch round trip check of one Shape item no longer applies; nothing in the engine does that).

Finding 8. `core/guard._macro_route_ok` allows a macro write only on the exact shape `/api/docs/{id}/graphs/{gid}/solve|cancel` (six segments), not on any route whose last segment is solve or cancel. The optimizer job cancel is no longer reachable by a macro; a macro cannot start an optimizer anyway.

Finding 9. `optimize.MAX_POPULATION = 2000`, `MAX_GENERATIONS = 10000`, applied where the job reads its spec.

Finding 10. Fixtures `graph/geometry-nodes.fcad` and `.graph` renamed `plate-holes` (README row and heading edited, files moved with plain mv); `kernel/README.md` says "the geometry family of the graph". Node display names changed (type ids and guids unchanged, so no alias is needed and saved graphs open; the stored `type_name` is only a fallback): Mass Addition to Sum Of Numbers, Mass Multiplication to Product Of Numbers, Graft Tree to Branch Per Item (nick PerItem), Flip Matrix to Transpose Tree (Transp), Path Mapper to Rewrite Paths (Rewrite), Entwine to Interleave Trees (Interl). Kept as ordinary English or maths words that name what the node does: Cull Index, Cull Pattern, Weave, Dispatch, Shift Paths, Member Index, Explode Tree, Construct Point, Number Slider, Value List, Larger Than, Curve Closest Point, Divide Curve By Count, Surface Boundary. The stored modifier key `graft` stays (file format key, stable); its label is the interface's.

Finding 11. The one docstring of `sheetmetal/bend.py` (`resolve_bend`) removed; the six hex group colours are replaced in the interface log below.

## I9 integration, v0.9.0 (graph/solver.py, setup/freeze_backend)

Stale kernel shapes: `solver.solve` calls `shapes.refresh(g)` under the graph lock before it orders the nodes, so a node holding a shape id from before a worker restart is expired and re-run with the changed nodes (GR2's discovery-only refresh left cached upstream geometry stale until a preview or bake met it). A graph that nobody touches after the restart is still refreshed only when something solves, previews or bakes it. Checked by killing the worker with SIGKILL, editing a slider and solving: 11 nodes ran, none failed.

`setup/freeze_backend` joined `scipy.optimize` and `--collect-submodules fontTools` into one argument (`scipy.optimize--collect-submodules`), so the frozen engine took scipy only by static analysis and the font flag was lost. Split into two flags. Two functions nothing called were removed from the new worker modules (`seg_end` in `kernel_unfold.py`, `uv_bounds` in `kernel_graph.py`); the kernel was frozen again after it. No other engine change this round.

## GR3 loops, optimizer, headless runner, node plugins, Phase 24 exit, v0.9.0 (graph/loops.py, optimize.py, runner.py, plugins.py, nodes/flow.py, routers/graphrun.py, automation/cli.py, mcp.py, plugins.py, graph/solver.py, setup/freeze_backend*, fixtures/graph/facade*)

Loops. `flow.loop_begin` and `flow.loop_end` pair by `settings.scope` (the End takes the nearest upstream Begin with the same scope text, so nested scopes resolve to the innermost). Members are the nodes both reachable from the Begin and reaching the End. The solver skips members (two lines in `solver.solve`: `held, order = loops.plan(g, order)` and a `continue`), and the End node runs them: each pass sets the Begin outputs to the carried state, runs the members in topological order with the solver's own `run_node`, reads the End inputs again, tests `exit` after the pass (do-while only; a pre-test would need the exit value before the first pass and R21 allows Anemone style as the default). Limit is the End input `max_iterations` (default 100, ceiling 100000), a stop at the limit with an exit wire connected is Warning LOOP_MAX, a stop on the node time budget is Warning LOOP_TIMEOUT, cancel aborts through `ctx.check`. Three carried slots `a`, `b`, `c` (tree access) instead of a variable number of ports, because ports come from the type. R21's `iteration_count_limit` output is dropped (the limit lives on the End). Seeds: member nodes see `ctx.seed` combined with the pass number. The Begin outputs are put back to the pass 0 state after the loop so any later expire that touches only the End restarts from the start; members keep the last pass. Wires from a member to a node outside the scope are not refused at connect time (wiring order would make that refusal depend on which wire is drawn first); such a node and everything below it is moved to the end of the solve order and reads the last pass. `history` collects the `a` items of every pass as branch `{pass}` when the End setting `collect` is on.

Optimizer. SciPy `differential_evolution` in deferred, vectorised mode (`polish=False`, `rng` seed, `integrality` for integer sliders, the current slider values as `x0`) is the default; `dual_annealing` (`no_local_search=True`) is the annealing option; both BSD-3, NLopt is not used (R21 licence conflict). Genes are `input.slider` nodes (bounds from their min and max), the fitness is the first number of one node output. A run solves a private pruned copy of the graph that keeps only the ancestors of the fitness node, so previews, bakes and geometry that do not feed the fitness are never computed; changing a gene expires only its downstream. A candidate whose fitness node fails or is not a number counts as a failure and scores worse than any number. Stops: generations, evaluation budget, time limit, stagnation, cancel (checked between candidates; a halt inside a generation returns infinity for the rest of it). The best genome is written back after the run, also after cancel, with `routers.graph._edit` and `store.update`, so it is one `GRAPHOPTIMIZE` undo step; `commit: false` leaves the sliders alone. One run per graph at a time. Events: type `graph`, reason `optimize`, with the job, state and progress. Routes under `/api/docs/{id}/graphs/{gid}/optimize` fall under the `graphs` segment already classified for macros by GC2 (POST refused for a macro).

Runner. Inputs are the input widgets that have a nickname (slider, toggle, panel, value list; the nickname is the public name, letters, digits and underscore); outputs are `io.output` nodes with `settings.name`. Overrides go into a private copy of the graph, never the open document. Tree JSON is `formcad.graph-io/1` of R21 6.2 (path keys, item arrays, tagged type); geometry comes back as `{handle, type, bbox}`. `--bake` runs `bake.bake` inside a transaction on a private document (the `--doc` file or a new one, never registered with the service, so no tab and no recent entry) and saves it through `outputs.checked` with `.fcad` forced; the path is checked before solving. Limits: 5000 nodes, default 60 s timeout (cap 3600 s), at most two runs at once over HTTP (429 after that). Exit codes 0 ok, 1 errors on a path to an output, 2 usage or file, 3 missing node type, 4 timeout, 5 cancelled. The MCP tool `formcad_graph_run` checks `graph_path`, `doc_path` and `bake_path` with `barred.args_refusal` and not the inline graph, because a node record has a port list named `out`, which the path-name list would take for a file argument.

Plugins. Node plugins use the existing loader: `Api.node` and `Api.port` (so a plugin written for commands can also add nodes), entry point group `formcad.nodes` read next to `formcad.plugins` (ids `epn:<name>` and `ep:<name>`), the hashed `plugin.json` folder, off until enabled. The type id is `<plugin-id>/<name>`; the namespace is the plugin id lower-cased with other characters turned into a dash. Unload removes the types, and a graph that names one opens with placeholders (GC2's `registry.placeholder`) keeping ports, values and wires; the runner stops with exit 3. Plugin code runs in the engine process like command plugins (decision of A2), so enabling is the trust decision.

Dependencies. scipy 1.18.1 added to `requirements.txt` and `requirements_macos.txt` with a NOTICE row; both freeze scripts pass `--hidden-import scipy.optimize`; `--selfcheck` imports it. The frozen size is in the GR3 handoff.

## SM1 sheet metal rule, features and bend list, Phase 22, v0.9.0 (sheetmetal/rule.py, records.py, tables.py, features.py, check.py, routers/sheetfeat.py, cmd/commands/sheetmetal.py, kernel/kernel_sheet.py, part/rebuild.py, part/tree.py, doc/tables.py, data/sheetmetal/bend_tables.json)

The rule is the params of the first sheet metal feature (base flange or convert), not a separate record, so a rule edit changes that feature's hash and every later feature rebuilds through the existing hash chain. Later features read the rule with `rule.for_step` and override per bend with their own radius, K, method, BA, BD and table params. Dimensionless values (K, relief ratios) use the `length` kind with an empty unit because the registry has no number kind; U10 should show them without a unit. The default K is 0.44 (R20), the default radius is the thickness, the minimum gap is T/10 (a placeholder, R20 found no source) and the relief defaults are width 0.5 T and 0.5 mm past the bend tangent (Ponoko, R20 section 3).

Geometry is built by construction. A feature is a cross section path (flats and arcs along the reference skin, thickness to one side) swept by a prism along the width; the same path gives the bend list, so the flat pattern can be replayed from it (`sections` on the feature row). Edge flanges, hems and miter flanges fuse the section to the body after cutting a strip from the parent when the bend starts inside the footprint (`material_inside` puts the outer virtual sharp on the end face corner, `material_outside` the inner virtual sharp on the top corner, `bend_outside` starts the bend at the end face; all three use tan(theta/2) setbacks so they hold for any angle). Jog and sketched bend cut a slab of flat sheet of the length the bends consume (BA each plus the web), move the far piece rigidly and fuse the section in its place; they refuse a strip that is not plain flat sheet. Reliefs are cut beside a partial width flange at both ends when the bend starts inside the footprint (rect, obround, tear), remnant strips narrower than the rule value are removed; bend relief and corner relief are also features on their own. Two flanges on adjacent edges that both start inside the footprint are separated by the earlier flange's depth plus the minimum gap.

Reference face. One per part (`ref_key` on the root feature row): the skin that a base flange sketch lies on, the sketch plane side of a plate, or the fixed face of a convert. Bend direction is up when that skin is inside the bend. An edge flange folds toward the face the picked edge lies on; `flip` folds the other way. The reference skin set is found by walking tangent edges, so it survives bends and jogs.

Records live in the new drawing table `sheetmetal` (rows `F3` feature, `F3.1` bend) written by the feature `finish`; `step.entry` is set in `rebuild.build` just before `finish` so a feature can turn result face indices into persistent names. Rows of features that are rolled back, suppressed or failed are filtered at read, rows of deleted features are pruned at the next write. Hems and rolled bends of 180 degrees or more are outside `resolve_bend`; their allowance is `bend_allowance` with the rule K and they carry `bd: null`.

Tables. The research found no licensable K, BA or BD table (R20 section 7), so `bend_tables.json` ships empty with the cited air forming formula as the only built-in (`air-forming-ratio`, exact as a three point ratio table, cap at R/T = 3). Document tables live in the header value `SHEETTABLES` and a change rebuilds the features that name the table.

Classification. SMRULE, SMTABLE and the feature commands are in group `solids`, change only document data and take no file arguments, so they are not in the barred lists; the routes sit under `sheetmetal` and need no denial. `kernel/formcad_kernel.py` gets one load block.

Typed prompts: the P1F flows that ask for a distance and test `kind == "value"` never complete when the answer is typed (the input kind is `number`); SM1 prompts use `ctx.number` and test `number`. P1F's FEATEXTRUDE, FEATEDIT typed paths still have the old test.

Left: miter flange faces are cut by a plane that is not square to the bent skins; bend and corner relief work only where the bend zone has parent flat beside it; the corner relief is a through cut of the folded corner, not the flat-state shape of a two-bend corner; forming tools and rips are not built.

## GR2 graph geometry, preview, bake and document nodes, Phase 24, v0.9.0 (graph/geo.py, shapes.py, preview.py, bake.py, docvalues.py, nodes/curve.py, surface.py, brep.py, intersect.py, transform.py, docref.py, attributes.py, cmd/commands/graphbake.py, kernel/kernel_graph.py)

Space and units. Graph geometry is millimetres everywhere. The document boundary converts: docref nodes multiply by `mm_per_unit`, preview and bake divide by it. A graph therefore gives the same numbers in an inch and a metric document.

Batching. A geometry node is a `run` node (no per item solve call). `geo.run_batched` takes the matcher's calls (`match.iterate`, so access flags, modes and the principal rule hold), sends every item of the node in one `graph_batch` worker call, and collects with `match.collect`. 1000 circles to 1000 capped extrusions is one call per node; a failing item becomes a null plus one aggregated warning (`ITEM_FAILED`), never a failed node. The solver already applies input and output modifiers for `run` nodes, so `run_batched` passes only the principal flag to the matcher. Nodes with several results per input are `multi`; `rows` outputs (surface divide) write one branch per grid row.

Light against kernel. Points, lines, polylines, circles, arcs, rectangles and every vector, plane and transform stay engine values. Interpolated and control point curves, and all surfaces and solids, are `Shape(kind, sid, bbox)` with kinds curve, surface, brep. Operations on light curves send a light spec (`{t: line|poly|arc|circle}`) in the same batch, so no shape is made until a result needs one. Transforms of light geometry are engine math; only `Shape` goes to the worker (`xf`, non uniform scales through a general transform).

Shape lifetime. The engine wraps each worker id as `<kernel serial>.<worker id>` (`shapes.wrap`), so an id from before a worker restart can never alias a new one. `shapes.track` keeps a ledger; a sweeper thread (5 s) and every 200 new shapes free ledger ids that no live graph output holds (grace 120 s so a long running node is never robbed). Held means referenced by `node.outputs` of any graph in `session.SESSIONS`; with no session, everything older than the grace is freed. After a restart a node that meets a stale id calls `shapes.recover`: it expires every node holding stale shapes and its dependents, asks the session for a new solve and raises `Aborted`, so the solve restarts clean; preview does the same when it meets a stale shape, and bake refreshes and solves synchronously first. Docref bodies are copied (`copy`) into graph owned ids; the document body cache (`work._loaded`) is never shared with the graph.

Preview. `preview.scene(doc, graph)` writes the existing scene format (header, 128 byte records, strings JSON) from nodes that are enabled, preview on and computed. Brep and surface items are meshes from `graph_preview` (written to a temp folder, parsed with `solid.scene.parse`), curves and points are zero triangle bodies whose edge table holds the polyline (a point is a one point edge). Extra flag bits: 8 selected, 16 graph item, 32 point, 64 curve. The strings carry `node`, `port`, `path`, `index`, `kind`, `selected`, `name`, `layer`. Selection comes from `graph.view["selected"]`, node `extra["selected"]` or `preview.select(gid, ids)`. Meshes are cached per wrapped id (6000 entries, 400 MB).

Bake. `bake.bake(doc, graph, {options, nodes})` runs inside the caller's transaction (the route and the `GRAPHBAKE` command both are), so one undo step removes everything and redo restores it. Bodies and surfaces are copied worker shapes saved as blobs through `work.new_body`; curve shapes become `nurbscurve` through the `record` op; light geometry becomes entities: point, line, `polyline3d`, and with `drafting` true (option, or node setting `bake_drafting`) a flat XY polyline, circle or arc become `lwpolyline`, `circle`, `arc`. Without drafting a circle or arc is a nurbs curve (an arc or circle falls back to the 2D entity when no kernel is installed). Attributes: layer (made when missing), colour, name, user properties from `Attributed` values (`attr.set`, `attr.user_property`), else the option defaults, else the current layer. Provenance `G/<node>/<port>/<path>/<index>` is `xdata["FORMCAD_GRAPH"]`, user properties `xdata["FORMCAD_PROPS"]` (`key=value`, and `name=` for entities that have no name field). A rebake (option `replace`, default true) erases the objects of the baked nodes with the same prefix first. Options: `layer`, `colour`, `name`, `drafting`, `replace`, `ports`, `selected`.

Attributes travel. `Attributed(geometry, name, layer, colour, props)` is a frozen value that only passes through `geometry` ports; transform nodes carry it. `values.encode` got one hook (`to_stored()`), so the solver digest and state summaries can hold it; it is not decoded.

Document nodes. `doc.objects` (layer pattern with trailing `*` for sublayers, kind, name pattern, optional handles), `doc.object_by_id`, `attr.read`. They register a `docvalues.Sub` per node; the commit hook (priority 90) and the undo and redo event expire the node (and dependents) when a watched handle changes, when an entity of a matching layer and kind is added, changed or removed, or when the layer table changes, then ask the session for a solve in 60 ms. Bulged polylines are read as chords with a warning; only point, line, circle, arc, lwpolyline, polyline3d, nurbscurve and solidbody are readable.

Guids are `uuid5(namespace, type id)` so a node keeps one guid forever without a table; the SDK only checks that it parses.

Not done: free form `Shape` vertex and mesh outputs, Python script node, live bake, interface.

## GR1 graph core node families, Phase 24, v0.9.0 (graph/nodes/common.py, params.py, inputs.py, maths.py, lists.py, sets.py, trees.py, vector.py)

Loading. `registry.ensure_loaded` imports every module of `graph/nodes` through `pkgutil`, so a family needs no import list; `nodes/__init__.py` is empty. Type ids are `param.*`, `input.*`, `math.*`, `list.*`, `set.*`, `tree.*`, `vector.*`, `xform.*` (transform values, so they cannot meet the geometry `transform.*` ids of GR2) and `colour.*`; every guid is a literal uuid4.

Shapes of nodes. Scalar nodes are item access and go through the matcher. Nodes that return a list per matched call set `multi`, so every output of such a node is a list. Nodes that mix scalar and list outputs, build branches or pass whole trees (params, widgets, mass addition, partition, every tree structure node, explode, statistics) use `run` with tree access ports and no matching. Params are tree access and pass the incoming tree unchanged (paths kept) after type conversion.

List nodes. Parameters of a list node that returns a list (sub list start and end, shift offset, insert index, split index, cull pattern, dispatch pattern, weave pattern) are list access and take the first item of the paired branch, so the output path equals the input path and no extra integer is added; only nodes with item access parameters next to a multi output (range, series, random) get the extra path integer of D9. List item takes its index as an item port, so one list with several indices gives several items. Cull pattern: true removes. Shift: positive moves items toward the end, wrap is on by default, off leaves nulls. Sub list indices are inclusive and negative counts from the end. Sort ranks nulls, booleans, numbers, text and is stable; sort outputs the permutation too. Weave rule 0 stops at the first exhausted source, 1 pads with nulls, 2 skips exhausted sources.

Widgets as data. Slider settings are `min`, `max`, `step` (0 is free), `decimals` and `integer`; the position is the persistent data of input `value` (the optimizer sets that). It is clamped (remark CLAMPED), snapped to the step from the minimum and rounded. Panel passes its input tree, or its saved lines when nothing is wired. Toggle and button read persistent booleans. Value list keeps `items` (name and value pairs) and `selected` (index or list of indices) in settings.

Maths. Angles of the trig nodes and of vectors, rotation and arcs are radians (graph values use radians); the Expression node runs on `app.calc`, whose sin, cos and tan take degrees, with variables x, y, z, w; no code runs, a calculator error is an Error on the node. Division, modulo by zero, domain errors of sqrt, ln, asin and acos are Warnings with null results. Random uses only `random.Random(seed).random()`. Round is half away from zero. Port defaults that are not JSON (domains, points, planes) are left empty and filled in the node, so the catalogue stays plain JSON.

Trees. Entwine puts a single branch input at `{k}` and a multi branch input below `{k}`. Tree item and branch take a path text like `{0;1}` or the branch number. Path mapper warnings become node Warnings with their code.

Not done: Relative Item, Split Tree mask grammar, Tree Paths and Path From Elements, Sort by descending flag, Closest Point and Grid nodes (GR2 owns geometry).

## GC2 graph solver and document, Phase 23, v0.9.0 (graph/sdk.py, registry.py, model.py, solver.py, session.py, store.py, edits.py, routers/graph.py, cmd/commands/graph.py, docs/GRAPH_FORMAT.md)

Storage. A graph is one record of table `graphs` (`TABLE_NAMES` gained the name; header `GRAPHS` counts ids), so undo, autosave, save and reopen cost nothing. The runtime graph (node state, output trees) lives in a `Session` per (document, graph), never in the document; `Session.sync` compares a digest of the stored definition with the last synced one and applies the difference in place (`Graph.apply_definition`): a node whose configuration signature or incoming wires changed is expired with everything downstream, all other nodes keep their cached outputs. The same path serves PUT, PATCH, undo and redo (a subscriber on the `doc` events re-syncs and requests a solve), so an undo recomputes only what the undone edit touched.

Solver. Order is `graphlib.TopologicalSorter` over enabled wires with the ready set sorted by node creation order. States blank, collecting, computing, computed, failed, skipped; a failed node is expired at the start of the next solve (R21 retry rule). Errors empty the node's outputs and the next nodes report NO_DATA from the matcher, so one root error shows. Cooperative abort is a per graph event checked between nodes, between matched calls (`ctx.check`) and by nodes through `ctx.cancelled()`; a node that returns after an abort is not committed (state back to blank). A result is committed only if the node's `epoch` did not move while it computed, so an edit during a solve never lets stale data in. Time budget per node is `settings.time_budget` seconds (60), enforced as `cancelled()` turning true and a TIMEOUT error after the call. Matching, modifiers and null rules are GC1's `match.run`; the solver wraps `solve` with kind coercion and turns `NodeWarning` into a skipped call with a warning.

Scheduling. One thread per session. A request replaces the pending one with the shorter deadline (the documented shorter-delay-wins rule), a request during a solve sets the abort flag and the loop starts the next solve straight after. Delay is chosen by the caller (the interface sends 0 for structure, 250 for typing, 50 coalesced for drags); the engine defaults to 0. Live edits (`live: true` on PUT and PATCH) merge into one undo step when they arrive within 3 s of each other (the merge edits the undo stack: it keeps the older step's before-image and moves its `state_after`).

Cycles. Connecting trial-inserts the wire into a graphlib sorter and refuses with the node names of the loop (HTTP 409, code `cycle`). A stored definition with a loop (hand edited file) loads with the closing wire disabled and Error CYCLE on its nodes. PUT refuses a definition with a disabled wire.

Files. `.graph` is the definition plus `format`; unknown fields are kept at every level, placeholders keep the saved port list so wires and persistent data survive, a file with a newer major or `min_reader` is stored verbatim, solvable, exportable and refused for edits (409 `newer`). Export forces the extension through `core/outputs.checked`; reading a file refuses over 32 MB and anything that is not a JSON object with the right `format`.

Trust. Macro route rules: `graphs` joins `MACRO_DENIED_WRITE` with `solve` and `cancel` exempted, so a macro may read and solve but edits go through commands; the `import` and `export` segments were already denied. `graph` (opens the editor) is barred for macros and agents; `graphrun` is open to both; `graphimport` and `graphexport` take `path`, covered by `args_refusal`.

Not done here: the headless `/api/graph/run` and CLI (GR3), bake and preview bodies (GR2 supplies `graph.bake.bake(doc, graph, body)` and `graph.preview.scene(doc, graph)`; the routes answer 501 until they exist), parallel node evaluation, early cutoff by output hash.

## GC1 graph data, Phase 23, v0.9.0 (graph/tree.py, match.py, values.py, modifiers.py, fixtures/graph/*_corpus.json)

Trees. `Path` is a tuple subclass, so Python's own tuple order is the R21 order (numeric, proper prefix first); `DataTree` keeps a dict and a cached sorted key list, mutation drops the cache. Items are plain Python values, null is `None`, and bare tokens of the notation become `Symbol` (a str subclass equal to the same str) so a round trip keeps quoted text and symbols apart. Equality is type aware (`values_equal`): a bool never equals a number, numbers compare within 1e-9 in fixtures and exactly in `==`. The notation grammar is numbers (int when no dot or exponent), double or single quoted text with escapes, true, false, null, and bare symbols (`a~x`, `sym_1`); `format` quotes what a symbol cannot carry and writes a geometry value as quoted text, so the notation is for display and fixtures while `to_json` (tagged `{"$": "Point3"}` values) is the lossless form. `to_map` and `from_map` give the `{"{0}": [...]}` shape of persistent data and graph-io, typed by port kind when a kind is given.

Operations. Flatten of a tree with no branches is empty, otherwise one branch even if every branch was empty. Simplify removes the longest common leading run but never the last integer of the shortest path (T09, T10); a trailing alignment option for unequal path lengths is not built. Flip matrix pads with null and refuses variable depth; flip tree appends collisions in traversal order. Trim culls the last N integers of each path, clamped to keep one, and merges equal paths in sorted order (T16 reading). Shift paths has no wrap rule: a negative result is `PATH_NEGATIVE` (T18); position is `last`, `first` or an index (negative counts from the end, a path too short for it is left unchanged). Path mapper follows R21 1.7; `path_count` and `path_index` count every branch of the input tree, including ones dropped by the source pattern; source slots may also be a whole number (matches only that value) or `_` (matches any), a superset of the grammar; `/` is floor division; presets are strings in the same grammar.

Matching. `iterate` applies the port modifiers, then D1 to D9 exactly as the reference algorithm of R21 2.4. The two risky decisions are named settings: `cross_scope` (D3, only `items` is implemented, any other value raises) and `principal_rule` (D2, `extreme` default or `first`). The port flag `principal` is the override of 1.6; an override with fewer branches than the pair count repeats its last path, so several pairs write into one branch (stated in R21's algorithm, kept). An optional input with no data supplies its default as one item at `{0}`; a null item on an optional item input is replaced by the default. Whole-branch calls (no item-access input) yield `index` None; item calls yield the position inside the pair. A multi output writes at `path.child(index)` for item calls and at the pair path for whole-branch calls; a skipped null call writes one null there; an empty branch (D4) on a multi output is written at `path.child(0)` so depth stays the same. `run` is the whole pipeline for the solver (null rule D5, NULL_INPUT once per skipped call, NO_DATA, output modifiers, a `cancelled` poll before each call).

Modifiers. Order and skips as R21 1.5: expression, null replacement, invert, remove null, remove empty, flatten, sort, randomise (skipped under sort), reverse, trim and renumber (skipped under flatten), graft, simplify (skipped under flatten or renumber). Expression goes through `app.calc.engine.evaluate(expr, {"x": value})`, numbers only (booleans and text raise `EXPRESSION_TYPE`, null passes through), results are 14 significant digits floats, a 2-D result becomes `Point3(x, y, 0)`. Sort orders null, bool, number, text, other. Randomise shuffles each branch with `random.Random("seed:path")`, so the same seed gives the same order. Unknown keys of a modifier dict are ignored.

Fixtures. `matching_corpus.json` (47 cases) and `tree_ops_corpus.json` (37 cases) transcribe R21 2.3 and 1.8; a scratch driver also parses the R21 text and compares every id, mode, input, access, modifier, expected tree and warning against the JSON, and runs each case: 170 checks, no case of R21 needed a corrected expectation. 76 extra checks cover the parser errors, values, transforms, JSON round trips, settings, multi outputs, cancellation and 20000 items. `--selfcheck` passes. No rule slip: every file is new and was written whole with Write; the three existing files (README, this log) were edited with Edit.

## SM2 flat pattern, bend maths, cut list, flat DXF, flat drawing views, v0.9.0 (sheetmetal/bend.py, flat.py, flatdxf.py, kernel_unfold.py, routers/sheetflat.py, cmd/commands/sheetflat.py, views/flatview.py)

Maths: `bend.py` keeps the deflection angle in radians everywhere (the included angle is a view), K from BA or BD, `resolve_bend` as the one call. A constant BA or BD in a rule is the value for 90 degrees: another angle scales BA linearly (BA) or reuses the K the 90 degree value implies (BD), so the three methods agree at 90 degrees and stay smooth elsewhere. Tables are `{mode k|ba|bd, axis angle|ratio, sets[{thickness?, radius?, points}]}`; linear interpolation, clamp with a warning, or the backup K when no set matches thickness and radius (R20 found no vendor rule for interpolation, so this is our choice). Angles of 180 degrees and more are refused by `resolve_bend`; the flat pattern then uses the rule K with a warning.
Unfold: the flat pattern is always computed from the B-rep of the part body (`unfold_solid`, face graph walk of R20 section 6), never stored. The bend list of SM1 is used by matching each found bend (axis line, radius, angle) to a record: the record gives the bend id and its K (so per-bend overrides and table K reach the flat pattern); a bend without a record gets `resolve_bend` from the rule. A recorded BA that differs from the flat pattern BA by more than 1e-6 mm is a bend warning. Reasons for B-rep first: holes, cuts, reliefs and irregular outlines are already in the solid, and one code path covers authored, converted and imported sheets. Skins are the faces that have an opposing face one thickness away (planes antiparallel at T, coaxial cylinders differing by T, overlap tested by sampled points and the face classifier); a convex cylinder of radius T next to a skin plane is a skin without a partner (inner radius 0). The reference skin is the skin of the fixed face (`rule.ref_key` when present, else the largest flat skin face, lowest index on ties); `flip` takes the opposite skin. Bend direction is Up when the reference skin is on the inside. Cylinders are unrolled at the neutral radius (R + K T, R from the skin that is the reference), lines stay lines, end arcs become straight edges, anything else on a bend is sampled to a polyline (`poly`, at most 400 points). A sharp (zero inner radius) planar fold on the reference skin is a bend of radius 0 with a strip K T times the angle; a sharp outside corner on the reference skin is refused with advice to flip. Outline loops are chained by vertex identity, collinear lines merged, outer counter-clockwise and holes clockwise; the sheet is rotated to the minimum area rectangle with the long side along x and the lower left corner at the origin (`align: none` keeps the fixed face at the identity). Refused with a message: cones, spheres, tori and freeform faces joined smoothly to the sheet, full tubes, a second path round a loop of faces (rip needed). Checked in the worker: flat area equals developed plane areas plus cylinder areas times Rn over R (`area_check`), outline self crossing, side of each placed face.
State: the toggle and options are the document header `SHEETFLAT` (`on`, `flip`, `fixed_face`, `align`, `rule` override used when the part has no sheet metal rule), undo safe through the transaction. The flat data is derived and cached on the drawing object by body blob, rule and bend list.
DXF: `flatdxf.py` through ezdxf, R2018 default (R2000 to R2018 accepted, R12 not offered because it has no LWPOLYLINE), `$INSUNITS` 4 and `$MEASUREMENT` 1, loops as closed LWPOLYLINE with bulges (true arcs), full circles as CIRCLE. Profile `cutting` is one layer `CUT` for outline and inner cuts, nothing else; profile `bending` is `OUTER`, `INNER`, `BEND_UP`, `BEND_DOWN` (linetype CENTER) and optional `BEND_NOTE` text (direction, angle, radius, K). Bend lines run straight across cutouts. Path goes through `outputs.checked(path, (".dxf",), ".dxf")`; the argument is `path`, so `barred.args_refusal` covers FLATEXPORT; the typed prompt says file. FLATEXPORT and FLATPATTERN are in group solids (macro and agent allowed with granted folders only, like BOMEXPORT), so `barred.py` needed no edit; the route `sheetmetal/flat/export` falls under the existing `export` segment denial for macro run tokens.
Cut list: `flat.cutlist` (thickness, material, bbox length and width, blank area, cutting length outside and inside, number of bends and cutouts, per bend rows) is returned by `GET /sheetmetal/flat/props` and as `sheet` on the body row of `POST /bodies/massprops` for a one body query of a sheet metal part.
Drawing views: `DrawView.flat` (viewbase `flat: true`, viewedit `changes.flat`) makes `build.compute` ask `flatview.compute` instead of the hidden line worker; the outline and cuts go to class `sharp_v`, bend lines to cache `bends` drawn on the new layer `VIEW_BEND` (CENTER) with a note `UP 90.0 DEG R3` shown when the view label option is on, in the display builder and the DXF writer. View local origin is the middle of the flat bounding box. The view goes stale with the body; a rule override held only in `SHEETFLAT` needs VIEWUPDATE.

## F9 open minors of the v0.8.0 audit, v0.9.0 (cmd/commands/fileio.py, cmd/commands/plot.py, display/drawview_d.py, drawann/titleblock.py)

R3 (Enter at a file prompt). Pressing Enter on a prompt with a default does not arrive as an `enter` input: `resolve` turns it into a text input with `from_default` set, so the earlier idea of checking the empty token never fired. `fileio.ask` (OPEN, SAVEAS, EXPORT, IMPORT) and `plot.ask_path` (PLOT) now treat `enter` or `from_default` as the default answer and send the default through `barred.args_refusal` (key `path`), raising CommandError when the caller is a macro or agent and the default is outside the granted folders. A path typed by hand still goes through `prompt_refusal`. The window session has no caller and is unchanged. Template prompts (default is the app's own template) and the `Last` macro prompt are not file writes and were left. 28: the title block description no longer names a standard. Detail view boundary (DR1 left note): a detail view now draws its own boundary circle (view position, radius times scale) on the visible layer; `show.boundary` false hides it; the DXF export of views is unchanged. Left with reason: mate picks on parts inside sub-assemblies (AS2) need the solver to carry nested component poses, not a contained change; 24 and 25 (README, version sites) belong to the orchestrator. Checked on a scratch engine: MCP `EXPORT`, `SAVEAS`, `PLOT` with Enter refused and nothing written under /root; typed `EXPORT /tmp/..`, `OPEN`, `INFO3DM`, `SCRIPT`, `IMPORT /etc/passwd` refused; LISP `(command "EXPORT" "")` refused; a zero-grant macro sending the same lines is refused at the group gate; window Enter at EXPORT still writes; detail circle at the view position with radius 10 in the display list; `--selfcheck` passes.

## Re-audit follow-up, v0.8.0 (cmd/barred.py, lisp/session.py)

The v0.8.0 re-audit's remaining major: an outside agent could type `(command "EXPORT" "/tmp/x.stl")` over MCP and the LISP routine, which runs on its own thread, lost the caller context, so the typed-path gate saw the window and let the write through. Two changes: `barred.lisp_refusal` now refuses LISP for outside agents as it already did for macros (LISP is a full programming language with file functions, so it belongs to the window), and the routine thread starts inside `contextvars.copy_context().run`, so any future path from a routine keeps its caller. Checked: the auditor's five MCP LISP probes (`scratch_A8/mcp_cmds.txt` lines 4 to 9) are refused and no file was written; `(+ 1 2)` typed in the window session still prints 3; `--selfcheck` passes. Left open (minor): an agent pressing Enter at a file prompt writes the default file name in the document folder.

## I8 round 2, v0.8.0 audit fixes (cmd/runner.py, cmd/barred.py, cmd/commands/meshes.py, cmd/commands/drawann.py, core/guard.py, meshes/formats.py, assembly/refs.py, assembly/walk.py, interchange/*, kernel/kernel_3dm.py)

Typed commands (audit 1). `Runner._refuse_prompt` wraps `barred.prompt_refusal` and is called from `_feed_text` and from `_feed` before `resolve`, so `EXPORT /path` typed inline gets the same refusal as a separate answer. `prompt_refusal` now takes the token: for a free-text prompt that asks for a file, folder or path, or when the token looks like a path (leading slash, tilde, dot slash, drive letter, or a known file extension), the token is checked like `args_refusal` against the granted folders and refused when outside them; a token inside a grant passes. The wording alone no longer decides, and the window session (no caller) is untouched. INFO3DM's `run_info` calls `args_refusal`. Routes (audit 2, 9): `3dm` joins `MACRO_DENIED_FIRST`, `docprops` joins `MACRO_DENIED_LATER`, and `MACRO_DENIED_WRITE` (`drawann`) blocks PUT, POST, PATCH and DELETE on those routes for macro tokens only (the method is now passed to `_macro_route_ok`); the other new route segments (`assembly`, `matesettings`, `saveas`, `export`, `import`) were already classified. Smaller: OBJ read refuses a face line with more than 1000 vertices and non-finite vertices (7); the drawing BOM CSV writes cells through `assembly.bom.cell` (8); a refused BOMEXPORT raises CommandError so it reports ok false (10); `mates/__init__.py` is empty (22); the title block description reads "data fields following the ISO 7200 list (single source)" (28); unused imports and four dead functions removed from `kernel_views.py`, `drawview` and `attribs` imports removed from `drawann.py` (29); spacing in `MACRO_DENIED_LATER` restored (31). Identifier rename (26): `RHINO_EXT` is `THREEDM_EXT`, `export_rhino_mesh` is `export_threedm_mesh`, the worker methods are `threedm_*` with `lib3dm()` and `threedm_unavailable`, the report key is `threedm`; each site was an Edit. Assembly references (6): a component file that is itself a symlink is accepted only when its real path lies inside the assembly folder or a search folder, and a missing, unreadable or wrong-kind file all answer with one generic message and no `resolved` path. The DR2 heredoc slip is named in the DR2 entry below and was re-checked here; no further slip this round.

## I8 integration, turn 8, v0.8.0 (kernel/kernel_views.py, core/outputs.py, core/guard.py, routers/interchange.py, fixtures/mechanism*)

Section faces are now cut per solid: `section_faces` walks the solids of the compound and merges the faces from `section_faces_one`. Cutting the whole compound at once joined edges that coincide between parts (pins in holes, plane through the cylinder seams) into one wire set, so a full section through the middle of the mechanism gave zero faces at y 0 and one wrong face at y 5; it now gives 15 and 6. `EXPECT` in `outputs.checked` gains `.stl` and `.3dm` (text or binary): a binary STL header holds zero bytes, so overwriting an STL written earlier was refused as a different kind of file. The `saveas` branch for 3DM, STL and OBJ goes through `outputs.checked` and `saveas` joins `MACRO_DENIED_LATER` (the window writes copies, macros and agents use the commands). The mechanism parts carry part number MECH-001 to 010, description, material (Aluminium 6061 for base and slider, Stainless steel 304 for the rest) and revision A in `DOCPROPS`, and the body material; `fixtures/mechanism.fcad` was reloaded, and the drawing, its PDF and its DXF were rebuilt so the parts list shows all columns. View commands that point at another file: `viewbase` and `viewedit` refuse any `source.doc` for macros and agents (stricter than a granted folder), `bomtable`, `autoballoon` and `bomexport` take their path through `args_refusal` (key `path`, nested in `source` too) and `bomexport` writes through `outputs.checked`; checked, no barred-list change. Dead code removed from `kernel_views.py` (an unused curve location, `region_slabs`, an unused angle). Read in full as scripted-edit files: `kernel_analysis.py` (Z1 slip), `kernel_views.py` (DR1 slip), `cmd/commands/drawann.py` (DR2 slip); all three are named in their own entries and are coherent. No K-series file was touched this turn.

## AS2 mates, solver, motion, exploded views, interference, Phase 20, v0.8.0 (app/mates/*, cmd/commands/mates.py, routers/mates.py, kernel/kernel_asm.py, fixtures/mechanism*)

Each free component is a rigid body with 6 DOF (left-multiplied pose updates, rotation columns scaled by the model length L). A mate is a residual between frames taken from picked faces, edges, axes and points (geometry from the worker, stored on the mate record). Solve is Levenberg-Marquardt with a weighted minimum-norm step; components already in other mates weigh 1000 so the newly mated one moves. Status comes from the SVD of the row-normalised central-difference Jacobian (rank tolerance 1e-6): the null space is the DOF per component with its free twists, a mate is redundant only if it adds no rank, partial overlap is reported apart. States are none, under, fully, over, conflict. A conflict is shrunk by greedy removal (8 s budget, `minimal` flag says whether it finished) and written in the explainer style with the mates whose removal fixes it. Gear, rack and screw mates keep an unwrapped turn count in the mate state so the ratio holds over many turns. Limit mates are one-sided rows, active only past the bound. Lock stores the relative pose at creation.

Drag: a tangent step. The hard rows give the null space N; the soft target row is solved in the least squares sense inside N, the step is projected back with a hard solve, and accepted with step halving only if the gap shrinks. A first version with a weak soft row inside LM stalled (the hard residual cost balanced the pull); the null-space method reaches the target exactly. When the pose is stuck (dead centre, a limit), small random nudges are tried at three scales. Drive steps a mate value in sub-steps (5 degrees or 4 percent of L) for continuity and returns per-sample poses and solve times; times are recorded per kind (`GET /mates/timing`).

Exploded views live in the `MATES` header: named steps of a component set, a direction and a distance, with per-step progress for animation. The auto-explode is a suggestion from `asm_blocking` and is saved only when `save_as` is given. Interference follows the briefed pipeline in `kernel_asm.py`; the fuzzy value is explicit (document tolerance by default). Mates ride the AS1 container and document transactions, so undo and redo need no extra code. The command for exploded views is `explodeview` because `explode` already breaks compound objects. `matesettings` is on both barred lists and in the macro-denied route set. No command reads or writes a file.

Left: no mate preview during pick, no gear mate across a non-circular pair, the minimal conflict set is a greedy result bounded by time, and auto-explode only tries axis directions.

## DR1 drawing views, Phase 21, v0.8.0 (kernel/kernel_views.py, app/views/*, doc/kinds/drawview.py, display/drawview_d.py, cmd/commands/drawviews.py, routers/drawviews.py, interchange/dxf_write.py w_drawview)

A view is a `drawview` entity on a layout; the built geometry is cached on the entity in view-local millimetres (x right, y up, unscaled), so scale and move never rebuild. The worker op `view_build` runs OCCT hidden line removal (exact `HLRBRep_Algo`, draft `HLRBRep_PolyAlgo`), keeps the eight edge classes (sharp, smooth, seam and silhouette, each visible or hidden) and attributes each 2D curve to the nearest original edge, face and vertex so DR2 dimensions can follow the model. Sections cut the model with a removal box, intersect with a plane, and build cut faces (full, half, offset, aligned by rotating one region about the elbow axis); detail and crop clip edges and faces with booleans on rebuilt clean edges (raw HLR 2D edges have no 3D curve and crashed `BRepAlgoAPI_Common`). The cut-over from exact to draft is 600 faces: exact costs 1.0 s at 528 faces and 4.2 s at 1188, mostly attribution, while draft costs 0.46 s and 0.98 s but returns about 14 times more segments and fails past about 100k pieces. The default mode is `auto`.

The projection angle is a layout record key (`third` default); `placement()` is pure and only the position mirrors in first angle. Views are routed under `/api/docs/{id}/drawviews` because `/views` already serves named views. A commit hook at priority 90 compares source signatures (same document bodies, AS1 instances, or an external part) and marks views stale or rebuilds them when `auto` is set; a rebuild of eight views took 0.45 s to 1.0 s on top of the 0.5 s part rebuild. A view command that points `source.doc` at another file is refused for macros and agents. A detail view reports its clip circle as its bounds so it keeps its size on sparse content. DXF export writes plain entities on the VIEW_* layers through `app.views.export.entities`.

Left: no overlap avoidance for several views on one side of a parent; no boundary circle on detail views; draft keeps no persistent ids; hatch fixed to ANSI31. One edit to `kernel_views.py` was made with a replace script instead of the Edit tool, against SEDO; checked afterwards.

## Z2 3DM read and write, mesh objects and mesh tools, Phase 19 exit, v0.8.0 (kernel/kernel_3dm.py, interchange/threedm.py, meshes/*, doc/kinds/meshbody.py, cmd/commands/meshes.py, routers/meshes.py)

rhino3dm 8.35.0 (MIT) lives only in the kernel worker; the engine never imports it and the worker answers `rhino_unavailable` without it. Its wheel carries openNURBS, whose licence text has no SPDX name, so NOTICE rows say written confirmation is still recommended. Knots follow the flat-vector rule: OCCT to 3DM drops the first and last knot, 3DM to OCCT adds 1 to the first and last multiplicity; 3DM control points are homogeneous (x*w, y*w, z*w, w) and are divided by w on the way in. The archive version is checked on read (2 and up; newer than 80 reads with a note) and on write (0 is the latest, else 2 to 80, anything else refused).

Brep trims are only partly exposed in 8.35.0, so import rebuilds faces from the surface plus the boundary curves of each loop (rectangle path when the loop is the surface boundary, else MakeFace on the surface and wire, FixAddPCurve, ShapeFix_Face, then ShapeFix_Shape), sews, and makes a solid only when every face rebuilt. Every face that fails is listed with object index, face index and reason, and the object is marked partial. Export cannot write trimmed Breps: natural-boundary faces go out as untrimmed surfaces, trimmed faces as the untrimmed patch plus separate trim curves, each with a lossy entry and a STEP recommendation; multi-face bodies go out as separate face Breps (lossy `unjoined_faces`). Written files are read back (`rhino_verify`) and the sampled distance of every object is reported against the model tolerance; a trimmed disc therefore shows its real deviation (4.14 mm for a cap of radius 10) instead of passing. Periodic curves and surfaces read back as clamped closed non-periodic, same geometry. SubD, block instances, text dots, lights and hatches are skipped with a reason. Mesh vertices are 32-bit in the file and the lossy list says so.

SAVEAS to .3dm writes a copy and leaves the document as .fcad with its path; opening a 3DM, STL or OBJ creates an unsaved drawing. Layer paths use `$` for `::`; pure black and white layer colours become ACI 7. STL and OBJ carry no unit: they follow the document unit and take an optional `unit` argument on IMPORT and EXPORT. Export uses the selection when there is one, else all solids, surfaces, meshes and curves.

Mesh objects (`meshbody`) hold a float64 FMESH blob under `meshes/<hash>.fmesh` in the file. Pure numpy, no trimesh. Weld is a grid hash with union-find; orientation is a parity BFS per component then an outward test by signed volume; naked edges are edges used once, shown as edge rows of curve kind 8 in the scene; hole fill triangulates each boundary loop by dynamic programming (largest dihedral first, then area, then shape), not a fan, and skips loops that touch themselves or exceed the edge limit, with the reason. Scenes are flat shaded up to 200000 triangles, vertex normals above. The report's `inconsistent_edges` is the count of neighbouring triangle pairs that traverse their shared edge the same way. STL binary is detected by size equal to 84 + 50 n, so a binary file whose header starts with "solid" still reads; OBJ negative indices and polygons are handled by fan triangulation. File paths go through `outputs.checked`; the path arguments are named `path` so `barred.args_refusal` covers them and the INFO3DM prompt says file.

Fixed on the way: a face carrying a display triangulation crashed `UVBounds` in the worker after the scene had been fetched; faces are copied without the mesh before conversion.

Left: no primitive shortcut (box, cylinder, sphere through the library factories) so cylinders and spheres export as lossy trimmed faces; no joined multi-face Brep on export; no SubD or instance read; no mesh read-back check in the verify block; no mesh bodies inside assemblies.

## AS1 assembly document model, references, configurations, BOM, instanced scene, v0.8.0 (assembly/*, routers/assembly.py, cmd/commands/assembly.py, docs/ASSEMBLY_SCHEMA.md)

Storage follows the part tree: header value `ASSEMBLY` (schema 1, counters, search settings, find numbers) and drawing tables `components`, `patterns`, `asmconfigs`, so undo, autosave, save and reopen need nothing special; the manifest kind is `assembly` (the template already existed). Embedded parts are ordinary solid bodies in a space `part:C<n>`; the plain scene route of an assembly document is served by `assembly/scene.py` (routers/scene.py dispatches on `doc.kind`), which also draws loose bodies in the model space. Schema, search order and status words are in `docs/ASSEMBLY_SCHEMA.md`, which is the contract; unknown fields are kept, a newer schema is readable but refused for edits.

References. Status is computed on every read, never stored: `loaded` (found and equal to the signature recorded when the file was accepted), `outofdate` (found but the signature differs), `missing` (not found, unreadable, wrong type, or a cycle; `reason` says which). A changed file is not picked up silently: the assembly keeps drawing the accepted copy (flagged `stale`, source `cache`) until Reload, because mates and patterns were made against that version. The copy is the part's tessellation (`cache/<blob>-d1.mesh`) written into the assembly file for each part component (setting `embed_cache`, 64 MB cap per component); `blobs.kept_copies` lets `container_entries` keep those meshes. Sub-assembly components keep no copy (nested content is not cached), so a missing sub-assembly draws nothing. Signature is size, mtime in ns and sha256 (files over 64 MB: size and mtime); a touched file with equal sha256 stays `loaded`. Search order is a recorded list of steps (relative, absolute, same folder, search folders, deep); every place tried is stored in the component with the order used. Deep search is off by default and bounded. Paths from another operating system, URLs and other machines' shares are never followed, only `.fcad` files are opened. Cycles are refused at Insert, Replace and Reload by walking the candidate's own sub-assemblies, and reported as `missing/cycle` when a hand-edited file contains one.

Transforms are rigid 4 by 4 (SVD-cleaned, scale, shear and mirror refused), row-major in the file, column vectors, translation in assembly document units; part geometry is millimetres, so mixed-unit parts keep their size. The first component is fixed (grounded). Pattern copies are derived (`C3#2`), never records; a copy is `P_k * M_seed` in the holding assembly's frame. Feature patterns find sites from the cylinder faces whose radius and axis match the cylinders the feature (and its pattern or mirror children) created, grouped by axis (kernel `geometry` per face), because pattern features name their faces by neighbours, so names alone cannot give hole axes. Sites are cached in the pattern (mm) so the pattern still evaluates when the target part is missing. Part-configuration choice builds a variant through P4F's `configs.activate` on a private copy of the part inside a transient Document registered in `service.docs` for the call (the rebuild hook finds its document there); variants are memoised on the loaded part. Assembly configurations copy P4F's rule: `Default` captures the live value of every key any configuration uses; edits made while a configuration is active are stored in it.

BOM: items are keyed by part number, else file and configuration, else embedded id; find numbers are stored once per item in the header and stay put (MIL-STD-100G: a cross-reference number, not an identifying number); GET never writes, export assigns. Part number falls back to the `idno` property, which DR2's title block uses. Modes top, indented (quantity per level plus total) and flat. CSV through `outputs.checked` with leading-apostrophe protection for formula characters. Document properties (`DOCPROPS`, `assembly/props.py`) are shared with DR2.

Security: every `/assembly` route is in `MACRO_DENIED_LATER` (macros use the commands); path arguments are named `path` or `folder` so `barred.args_refusal` covers them; the typed prompts say file and re-check the answer; `asmsearch` (search folders and order) is in both barred sets. The scene is built with mesh bytes only; nothing a component file contains is executed.

Left: no lightweight or large-assembly open modes, no in-assembly sub-assembly grouping (form from selection), no instanced draw format (each instance writes its own transformed copy, 13 MB for 100 plates, 40 ms), no mesh bodies from Z2 in assemblies, sub-assembly cache.

## Z1 gumball, 3D snaps and analysis, v0.8.0 (analysis/*, gumball/*, routers/analysis.py, routers/gumball.py, cmd/commands/analysis3d.py, cmd/commands/gumball.py, kernel/kernel_analysis.py)

All analysis is computed in the engine and worker; the interface only colours vertices. Results are per-vertex f32 arrays aligned with the G1 scene buffers (NaN is undefined), sent as `application/vnd.formcad.analysis` v1 (64-byte header, 32-byte body records, JSON metadata, crc32). They are held in a small in-memory cache and never written to disk. Worker data files (`.adat`) are scratch in a temp folder.

Zebra uses the true reflection r = 2(n.v)n/|n|^2 - v, not the quoted (2/|n|^2)((n.v)n - v), which vanishes at normal incidence; r is projected off the stripe axis and the stripe coordinate is its angle about that axis over the band. Isophotes use the angle between the normal and the light direction. Exact normals and curvature come from BRepLProp_SLProps per display vertex (display normals are wrong at seams); the vertex order is reproduced by re-meshing with the same deflection and compared by position, a mismatch is refused. OCCT reports a sphere's principal curvatures negative for the outward normal, so mean and principal values are negated by the face orientation (K is unchanged); at sphere poles the parameter is nudged toward the face centre until curvature is defined.

Draft is asin(n.d) in degrees against the pull direction; its ramp is "zones": the engine expands pass, warn and fail colours with limits (default -3 and 3) into hard-step stops. Deviation measures against a compound of the target's faces, so a solid is not a volume (distance to a closed solid's surface, not 0 inside); a single untrimmed face uses GeomAPI_ProjectPointOnSurf with a face classifier, everything else a reused BRepExtrema_DistShapeShape. Signed distance follows the target normal. Deviation values are f64 in the worker and f32 on the wire. Curvature combs put tips on the convex side (p - n k factor), the factor is max height (10 percent of the bounding diagonal times scale) over max curvature; planar curves carry signed curvature and inflection indices, others unsigned.

Ramps are the engine's own and user-editable; stops and limits live in the document header key `ANALYSISRAMPS` (kinds gradient, bands, zones) and follow undo, save and reopen. ANALYSISRAMP is on both barred lists.

3D snap kinds are numbered vertex 0, mid 1, centre 2, quadrant 3, end 4, intersection 5, perpendicular 6, nearest 7, surface 8, curve 9. A candidate must lie within the aperture of the pick ray, so a centre only snaps when the ray passes near the centre. Cost grows with edge count (bodies capped at 64, curves at 400).

Gumball state is runner memory (`gumball`), not document data. Affines come from `gumball/frame.py`; solids move and rotate through `multibody`, scale through the worker (`transform` for uniform, `an_affine` for non-uniform). A tree-owned part body refuses scale. 2D entities (circle, arc, ellipse, lwpolyline) take move, Z-parallel rotation and uniform scale; other cases are counted as skipped. The command clears the selection after an undoable step, so the route `POST /gumball/apply` keeps it and takes `copy` and `preview`. Relocate sets a sticky manual pivot that moves with the selection; reset clears it. Aligning to a 2D curve class uses the world frame.

Process slip: `kernel/kernel_analysis.py` was rewritten once with a python script instead of Edit; it was parse-checked and every later change was an Edit.

## DR2 drawing annotation, v0.8.0 (drawann/*, doc/kinds/drawann.py, doc/kinds/drawannlink.py, display/drawann_d.py, cmd/commands/drawann.py, routers/drawann.py, dim/assoc.py, dim/features.py, interchange/dxf*.py)

Views are reached only through `drawann/viewsrc.py` providers (`kinds`, `edges`, optional `section_loops`, `scale_text`, `project`, `direction`, `role`, `model_drawing`); `drawann/drawview.py` is the provider for DR1's `drawview` entity. Edges are given in sheet units, ids are the persistent edge id (`p<pid>`) or class and index. A dimension point links to a view edge as `{h, e, t, a}` (start, end, mid, center, ang); `dim.assoc.follow` resolves it, so a rebuilt view moves the dimension.

Geometric tolerance frame: own stroke glyphs built from lines, arcs and polygons (14 characteristics, 5 categories), compartments characteristic, tolerance (diameter sign, M or L), up to three datum references. Compartment order is the commonly documented one and is stored per frame and as a document default in `FC_DRAWANN.gdt_order`; the interface calls it a geometric tolerance frame and makes no conformance claim. The source of the order is the encyclopedia's common practice, not a standard text; this is a known gap.

Surface finish is a value-only mark (roughness number and optional text), no weld symbols; recorded as a gap. Balloons default to 9.525 mm diameter (Navy source), size is the style value `balloon_diameter`; auto-balloon numbers follow the BOM find number and are placed on a ring round the view box in FormCAD's own order (each balloon sits where the ray from the view centre through its anchor meets the ring, then sorted counter-clockwise from the bottom left and spaced apart). A balloon tip links to an edge in a view and follows it.

BOM table comes from the assembly walk (leaf parts grouped by reference and configuration, qty counted) and is rebuilt by `bomupdate`. Title block is the K1 `TITLEBLOCK` block with the 16 ISO 7200 field tags filled from the AS1 `DOCPROPS` header, single source; scale comes from the main view, sheet n of m from the layouts.

`gdtstyle` and `docprops` are on both barred lists. Section hatches use the HATCH engine on DR1's face loops and are regenerated by the commit hook (priority 81) when a view changes. DXF: each annotation is written as an exploded anonymous block `*U` plus the FORMCAD snapshot, so other readers see plain geometry and FormCAD restores the kind. Process slip: one append to `cmd/commands/drawann.py` was done with a shell heredoc instead of Edit; the file was parse-checked and every later change was an Edit.

## v0.7.0 audit minors and tree split and copy, turn 8 (cmd/commands/surfaces.py, curves3d.py, part/*, bodies/multibody.py, routers/curves.py, routers/bodies.py, automation/plugins.py, kernel/kernel_match.py)

Bounds: SEAMCHECK samples 2 to 2000, SRFPATCH and SRFNETWORK degree 1 to 8, samples or points 2 to 2000, iterations 1 to 10, SRFPATCH segments 1 to 64 (now passed to the worker); out of range is refused with the value named. Messages: a lost reference reads "An edge in ..." (article by noun); extrude depth over 1,000,000 mm is refused by the engine before the worker. CRVCONT prints gaps with %.3e. The silent import guard in `routers/bodies.py` is a plain import of `planesource` (it registers the refgeo plane source at import).

Curve drag preview: `POST /docs/{id}/curves/{handle}/preview {grip, to, per_span}` runs `grips.stretch` on a copy and answers poles and samples; the document is untouched. It takes control and edit grips and periodic curves. The interface's de Boor evaluator is deleted; a drag asks at most one request in flight with 50 ms between requests, the last pointer position always sent.

Entry-point plugins load from verified bytes: `read_verified_dist` reads every file the discovery digest covers once, recomputes the same digest and refuses a mismatch, then `VerifiedImporter` serves the package under its real name from memory. importlib allows this because the finder sits first on `sys.meta_path`. Refused with a message: compiled files in the module, a module already imported before verification (restart), a package with no source. Package data files read at run time and other distributions it imports are not covered.

Split and copy as tree features: `split_body` (planes, tool bodies, `keep` piece) and `copy_body` (translate, rotate, count). The feature's own body stays the part's; the other pieces and copies are side bodies in the document, recorded in the tree as `sides` (key `F<n>:<piece>` to handle), updated in place by every rebuild, removed when the feature is suppressed, rolled back, failed or deleted. The rebuilder takes `pieces` from the worker and `passthrough` plus `extras` from a plan; entries own the extra shapes and free them. Side bodies get fresh face ids on a rebuild that changes them; editing a side body by hand lasts until its feature next rebuilds. Piece order is the worker's order. Tool bodies of combine and split are part of the feature hash (version and offset) and watched by the follow hook, so editing a tool rebuilds without Force Full Rebuild.

SEAMCHECK reports `edge_offset` (worker, three points of the first edge against the second edge) and warns when the second edge is farther than ten times the length tolerance. Seam reports carry the bodies they involve; the interface clears a seam badge only when one of those bodies changes. README lists the three build and dev variables.

## Audit follow-up, v0.7.0 (cmd/commands/bodies.py, part/features_shapes.py, NOTICE.md, CHANGELOG.md, README.md, fixtures/README.md)

The v0.7.0 audit's one major: the typed DESIGNTABLE flow asked "Enter the table name", so the file prompt guard (it looks for file, folder, path or directory in the prompt) never fired, and a macro's `send` or an MCP command line could export to or import from any folder. The prompt now reads "Enter the table file name" and the typed answer is passed through `barred.args_refusal` with the command's catalogue entry before anything is read or written, the same check the programmatic form already had. Rerun of the auditor's probes (`scratch_A7/mac/new3.py` through a trusted zero-grant macro on a scratch engine, `scratch_A7/mcp3.py` over MCP stdio): export, import and the command-line form are all refused, the victim file is unchanged and no file is created; the window session is unaffected (no caller, no refusal). Minors closed: the feature is labelled Hole builder (the earlier label is a commercial product's feature name), NOTICE.md lists the seed data, CHANGELOG states the hole table's real content, fixtures README says chamfer 0.3, README lists `FORMCAD_HOST` and `FORMCAD_DIST` and the new folders.
