Left open for turn 8: unbounded samples, degree and segments in SEAMCHECK and SRFPATCH; "A edge" wording and a raw kernel message at absurd depths; the interface's de Boor evaluator for drag preview (`view3d/curve-layer.ts`); CRVCONT's four-decimal output; the silent import guard in `routers/bodies.py`; the entry-point plugin race; the command words FILLETEDGE, CHAMFEREDGE and BLENDEDGE (common words, owner's call).

## Integration fixes, v0.7.0 (kernel/kernel_feat.py, part/profile.py, part/features_bodies.py, part/tree.py, bodies/multibody.py, part/preview.py, routers/part_preview.py, automation/plugins.py)

Profile bugs found by P3F. `profile.nest` took the circle centre as the representative point, so two concentric circles each lay inside the other and no loop was outer; the representative point of a circle is now a point on it (`cx + r, cy`), so only the bigger circle contains the smaller. `build_wire` returned `Edge.Reversed()`, a plain shape, for a circle hole, which `MakeWire.Add` refuses; it is now `TopoDS.Edge(...)`. `edge_roles_for` took the nearest label midpoint over all loops, which mixed the roles of concentric loops; circle labels now carry centre and radius and match an edge by radial distance (within 1e-6 of the radius), so each ring keeps its own `h<handle>` role. An annulus sketch extrudes to the exact volume (pi(R^2-r^2)h to 1e-9) with roles `side:h256` and `side:h257` kept apart.

Combine, Move, Split, Copy against the tree. Two feature types register in `part/features_bodies.py`: `move_body` (translate and rotate of the whole part body, run through the worker `transform`) and `combine_body` (op add, subtract or common with other solid bodies listed by handle in the `tools` parameter, run through the worker `combine` with the worker ids of those bodies). `bodies/multibody.py` routes by caller: when the body being combined or moved is the tree body (`PARTTREE.body`), the command adds the feature instead of editing the entity, so a rebuild, a rollback and undo all keep it. Combine needs the tree body first and keeps the tool bodies (a feature that reads a body cannot erase it). Split on the tree body is refused with a message (its pieces would be dropped by the next rebuild); Copy of the tree body makes an independent snapshot body that a rebuild does not touch. Known: a tool body edited later is not noticed by the hash chain; use Force Full Rebuild.

Plugin load race. Folder plugins no longer import from the path. `read_verified` reads `plugin.json` once, compares its sha256 with the value recorded when the plugin was enabled, reads every listed file once and compares each with the manifest digest; `VerifiedImporter` (a meta path finder and loader over those bytes) serves the entry module and its sibling modules under `formcad_plugin_<id>` from memory, so a file swapped on disk after the hash has no effect, and a swap before the read is refused with the changed file named. Unload and failure remove the finder and the modules. Entry-point plugins still import through the installed distribution (hash of files at discovery).

Held scene. `GET /part/scene-at?to=<fid>` answers the binary scene as the part stands with the rollback at that feature, built in a rolled-back transaction like the preview, so the real rollback and the cache are untouched. `POST /part/preview` takes `hold`, the same rollback applied before the scene is built.

## Feature preview route and sketch exit attach, v0.7.0 (part/preview.py, routers/part_preview.py, part/tree.py, main.py)

`POST /api/docs/{id}/part/preview {type?, id?, params, refs, after?}` answers the binary scene of the model as it would look after adding or editing the feature, with the outcome in the `X-FormCAD-Preview` header (URL quoted JSON: id, status, message, warnings, failed). It runs a rebuild inside `doc.begin('PREVIEW')` against a shadow `Cache` whose trash list drops appends, builds the scene, then `doc.rollback()` and restores the real cache, freeing only worker shapes the preview created. The document, undo stack, tree and cache are unchanged afterwards. A command running at the time gives 409; a worker failure 503; bad input 400.

`sketch_exit` now attaches the entities drawn in `sketch:<name>` to the sketch feature before returning to model space and answers `{space, attached}`. Without it a sketch drawn in the 2D canvas stayed outside the feature and the rebuild saw an empty profile.

## Interface read routes for surface reports, turn 7 (routers/curves.py, cmd/commands/surfaces.py)

`GET /api/docs/{id}/surface-last` returns the runner memory the surface commands fill (`surface_report`, `surface_naked`, a `surface_seq` counter bumped by `keep_report` and `do_naked`, and `mm_per_unit`), and `GET /api/docs/{id}/model-tolerance` returns the MODELTOL values without running a command (a command run would cancel a running one). Both are read only and sit behind the session token like the rest of the router.

## Surfaces, booleans with diagnostics, edge treatments, seam continuity, v0.7.0 (kernel/kernel_surf.py, kernel_match.py, kernel_diag.py, app/solid/surfwork.py, cmd/commands/surfaces.py)

Surfaces are `solidbody` entities whose shape is a shell or face (no new entity kind), so persistent ids, undo, save, mesh and the scene work unchanged; `source` holds `op`, `surface: true` and a compacted `report` (naked-edge polylines thinned to 24 lines of 120 points, long lists to 400). Commands use the prefix `srf` (SRFLOFT, SRFSWEEP, SRFNETWORK, SRFPATCH, SRFEXTRUDE, SRFREVOLVE, SRFPLANAR, SRFEDGE, SRFBLEND, SRFMATCH, SRFEXTEND, SRFOFFSET, SRFSPLIT, SRFTRIM, SRFJOIN) plus BOOLOP, BOOLCHECK, FILLETEDGE, CHAMFEREDGE, BLENDEDGE, SEAMCHECK, NAKEDEDGES, SURFREPORT, MODELTOL; FILLET and CHAMFER stay the 2D commands.

Tolerance policy: every worker call carries `tol` (mm) and `angtol` (rad) from the drawing (`tolerances.absolute` times `mm_per_unit`, `tolerances.angular`). A stored value below 1e-6 mm (the untouched 1e-9 default) is replaced by 1e-3 mm and 1e-3 rad and reported as source `default`; MODELTOL sets 1e-6 mm to 1 mm and 1e-7 to 0.1 rad, is not undoable (tolerances are outside the undo keys), and sits in MACRO_BARRED and AGENT_BARRED. The worker refuses `tol` below 1e-7. Each result carries an audit of the stored vertex, edge and face tolerances against the limit.

Coons style is invalid in this binding (`GeomFill_CoonsStyle` raises), so network uses MakeFilling with boundary curves, two-curve edge surface is a ruled surface, three or four curves use the curved style. ocp_gordon is not used. Two-rail sweep uses an own frame from the rail pair with a Law_Interpol scaling law and reports the distance of the result from each rail; the one-rail sweep does not report one (the profile is not on the rail).

Blend (`method` auto): MakeFilling with G2 constraints first, judged by LocalAnalysis_SurfaceContinuity at both seams; if either seam is below the request, an own Hermite surface (cubic in u, quintic across, end position, tangent and normal curvature taken from each side, `influence` scales the tangent length) is built and judged the same way. The report keeps the filling attempt. Match moves the edge row and the next two rows of a non-rational, untrimmed, non-periodic B-spline surface to meet G0, G1 or G2 and refines the knots until the seam is crisp; rational, trimmed and periodic surfaces are refused with a message. Extend is the exact polynomial continuation of the end span (every derivative continues), because ExtendSurfByLength is broken in this binding; a periodic direction across the edge is refused with the R17 message; a periodic along-edge direction is unpacked first. Offset refuses a face that is only C0 inside. Seam analysis samples the edge, projects to both surfaces, and reports the maximum position gap, normal angle and curvature gap with G0, G1, G2 verdicts against the tolerances (curvature tolerance is 1 percent of the local curvature, `curvature_relative`); G3 is not measured and is returned as null.

Booleans: `bool_diag` runs BOPAlgo_ArgumentAnalyzer in every mode before the operation, then BOPAlgo_BOP with a ladder (as given, ShapeFix of the inputs, a ten times larger fuzzy value capped by `max_fuzzy`), reads HasErrors, HasWarnings and the dumped alert names, unifies same-domain faces and runs BRepCheck_Analyzer on the result. A failed or invalid boolean changes nothing and returns `{ok: false, message, report}` with invalid shapes and status names, small edges, self-intersections, free and naked edges as polylines and the fuzzy value used. History is returned only for the first ladder step.

Fillet and chamfer use BRepFilletAPI_MakeFillet (G1 or G2 continuity, constant, two-radius or radius schedule) and MakeChamfer (distance, two distances, distance and angle) with the history of the faces; a result that fails BRepCheck is refused. Join sews with BRepBuilderAPI_Sewing (OCP gives no history, so a small SewHistory adapter maps faces and edges) and closes into a solid when no free edge is left. Split pieces other than the first get fresh persistent ids.

## Sweep, loft, boundary solid, rib, hole wizard, v0.7.0 (kernel/kernel_shapes.py, app/part/features_shapes.py, app/solid/holes.py, app/data/hole-sizes.json)

Own worker module `kernel_shapes.py`, loaded by one guarded import in `load_operations`; the engine registers feature types `sweep`, `sweep_cut`, `loft`, `loft_cut`, `boundary_solid`, `rib`, `hole` through `features_shapes.py` (imported once from `part/tree.py`). Standalone methods (`sweep`, `loft`, ...) and the `feat_*` versions share one body; the `feat_*` ones go through `kernel_feat.combine` and return the part-tree result layout.

Sweep is `BRepOffsetAPI_MakePipeShell` with fixed, Frenet, corrected Frenet and binormal modes. Twist is an auxiliary spine built as a rotation-minimising helix around the path; scale is a linear law; both together are verified against closed forms. `CurvilinearEquivalence` is false because true gave wrong volumes. A guide curve uses contact mode and so is C0 only; this is a warning on the result, not a promise. Loft is `ThruSections`; it cannot honour guide curves, so they are recorded and a warning is returned (`guides_applied` false). Start and end conditions are approximated by an inserted offset section, not an exact tangent constraint. Inner loops are lofted separately and cut.

Boundary solid is `MakeFilling` per curve group, sewn, planar loops capped, then made a solid; a closed shell of zero volume is reported as not closed. Fill accuracy on curved tops is about 7e-4 of volume (planar is exact). N2 never published surfaces, so nothing from N2 is used. Other fill styles were tried and removed: they failed or gave 7 percent error.

Rib: the open profile is extruded along the growth direction and thickened both ways, the piece touching the profile is kept, and a rib that the body does not close in on the growth side is refused. Draft needs a straight profile square to the growth direction.

Hole wizard sizes: no free primary source gave legible clearance, counterbore, countersink or tap drill tables, so those sections ship empty. `hole-sizes.json` carries only coarse thread pitches traced to one public standard copy, each row with a status (read twice, read once, or illegible with no value), and the notice says it is an unverified seed. A tapped size with no pitch is refused; a missing tap drill is derived as nominal minus pitch with a visible warning. Every number can be typed over in the feature. The user copy at `<data dir>/hole-sizes.json` replaces rows by (standard, id). Cosmetic thread data (position, axis, nominal, pitch, depth, derived minor diameter, tap drill) is kept on the part cache per feature, not as geometry.

## NURBS curves, blend, match, continuity report, v0.7.0 (app/nurbs3d/*, doc/kinds/nurbscurve.py, cmd/commands/curves3d.py, routers/curves.py, kernel/kernel_curves.py)

Entity kind `nurbscurve` (degree, poles, weights, knots as distinct values, mults, periodic, source, audit), poles in document units, z kept. The worker module `kernel_curves.py` is separate from the other worker modules and is loaded by one guarded import in `load_operations`. The engine package `app/nurbs3d` never imports OCP: `NurbsForm` evaluates rational curves to order 3 itself (periodic ones through an unwrapped knot sequence checked against OCCT), so blend, match, grips and the continuity measures work without a kernel; commands that need OCCT (interpolation, fit, rebuild, simplify, offset, extend) answer that no kernel is installed.

Tolerances: OCCT has no global tolerance, so every worker call carries the document absolute and angular tolerance (millimetres in the worker, scaled by `mm_per_unit`) and every result carries an audit of stored vertex, edge and face tolerances against the limit. Approximation floors at 1e-7 in the worker; fit, simplify and offset use max(document absolute, 1e-6) unless the command gives one, because the 1e-9 default is below what an approximation can promise. Continuity checks use the raw document values. Curvature agreement is relative, 1e-3 unless `tolerances["curvature"]` is set.

Blend: OCCT has no G2 or G3 blend, so a Bezier of degree cA+cB+1 is built from end derivatives (tangent, curvature vector, third derivative with torsion) scaled by shape factor times chord per end; exact to G3 including 3D. Match: moves the poles of the start end (a reversed copy for the end) so the derivatives up to the order meet the target, refining knots when poles are too few, and refusing an order above the degree. G0 to G2 are decided by LocalAnalysis_CurveContinuity (with a manual G2 when a curvature is zero), G3 by FormCAD.

Offset is planar; a positive distance lies left of travel about the normal; a periodic input gives a non-periodic result. Smooth extend is a G2 parabola (quadratic Bezier), since the OCCT point extension cannot be called from Python. Rebuild of a periodic curve is a degree 3 periodic interpolation of equally spaced points; its deviation is reported, not promised. Edit commands change the curve in place with one undo step; the `copy` option makes a new entity. Edit-point grips use a minimum norm pole shift. Skipped from R03 2.1 and 2.2: conics, catenary, spirals, tween, handle, curve on surface, fillet and chamfer between curves (the drafting FILLET and BLEND exist), curve boolean, offset multiple, normal, on surface, dynamic extend, fair, make uniform, non-uniform rebuild, seam, projection and section curves.

## Part feature tree, topological naming, incremental rebuild, sketch features, Extrude and Revolve, turn 7 (app/part/*, routers/part.py, cmd/commands/part.py, kernel/kernel_feat.py)

Storage: one drawing table `features` (record per feature: id, type, label, params, refs, suppressed, state) so undo, save and reopen need nothing extra, plus the header value `PARTTREE` (order, rollback, next id, body handle, `names` key to persistent id, auto flag, workspace, built hashes). The workspace is a flag in that value; drafting and part live in one .fcad. Sketch features reuse S2: entities sit in space `sketch:<name>` and the sketch feature stores plane (XY, XZ, YZ, face, or a registered plane source such as `refgeo`), offset, and for face attach an origin and x direction taken from the face frame.

Naming: every face and edge gets a key when a feature builds. Keys are `<feature>:<role>` for faces the feature creates (`side:h<handle>[.s<i>]`, `cap:start`, `cap:end`, `cap:end2`, thin `:l`/`:r`/`:end`), history rows keep the old key (splits become `key#n`), faces nobody named are named by their neighbours (`F5:~a|b`), and edges by the two faces they join, without a feature prefix (`e(a|b)`), so an edge keeps its key when a later feature only reshapes it. A selection record is `{kind, key, ctx, sig, pid}`; `sig` is a geometric signature used only when the key is gone. Fallback accepts a unique best candidate within score 0.35 with a margin of 1.5 over the runner-up; anything else raises `RefLost`, which becomes that feature's error and never drops the tree. Chosen over index-based references because indexes move on every edit; chosen over signature-only because equal-sized neighbours (hole patterns) are ambiguous.

Rebuild: per feature hash chain (previous hash, type, evaluated params, ref keys, hashes of referenced entries). The first feature whose hash differs is rebuilt and everything after it; the cache is keyed by document and kernel serial, so a cold open rebuilds once. A failed feature passes the previous shape through; children that reference it fail with a pointer to it. Suppressed features pass through; features after the rollback bar get status `rolled`. A commit hook at priority 80 rebuilds when sketch entities, parameters, sketches or constraints tables changed, guarded by `tx.part_done`; `auto` false only marks stale. Forced rebuild (`force`) ignores every hash.

Kernel: `feat_extrude` and `feat_revolve` build a tool solid, then fuse or cut with `SetToFillHistory`; up-to-face and up-to-next use `BRepFeat_MakePrism`. The tool solid id is kept so pattern and mirror use it as the seed. Results use the same layout as P2F (`history.inputs`, `created`, `adjacency`). P2F draft leaves out edge history rows, so edges around a drafted face get new keys from their face pair; the fixture references the boss rim after the draft by those keys.

Limits found while building `fixtures/part-core.fcad`: shell followed by a through-hole pattern gives an invalid solid in this OCCT build, so the fixture shells before the pocket and holes; chamfer on a shell rim fails when the distance exceeds the wall (0.4 on a 1 mm wall is used). Commands are not file readers or writers, so `cmd/barred.py` is unchanged. Not done: multibody with merge off (P4F), draft with a second direction, selected contours, ellipse and spline profiles are faceted, no up-to-face fallback when the prism feature fails.

## Multibody, reference geometry, configurations, design tables, materials, mass properties, turn 7 (kernel/kernel_bodies.py, bodies/*, routers/bodies.py, cmd/commands/bodies.py)

Booleans (R16 section 8): worker `combine` and `split` run `BRepAlgoAPI_Fuse/Cut/Common` and `BRepAlgoAPI_Splitter` with `SetToFillHistory`, `SetNonDestructive` and the fuzzy value; before a boolean `BOPAlgo_ArgumentAnalyzer` checks each argument (type, self-intersection, small edges, face rebuild, continuity, curve on surface; tangency per pair) and a finding refuses the operation unless `force`; after it `BRepCheck_Analyzer` rejects an invalid result so no bad shape is stored. The fuzzy value is the document absolute tolerance converted to millimetres, floored at 1e-7 mm (`Precision::Confusion`); a failed or empty result is retried once with 100 times that (at least 1e-5 mm) and the response says so (`diagnostics.retried`). This OCP build exposes no `HasErrors` on the boolean classes, so failure is `IsDone()` plus an empty-result test. `Combine`: first body is the base and keeps handle, name, layer and material; the others are erased unless `keep_tools`. `Split`: the first piece keeps the body, later pieces are new bodies named `<name> (n)`; a face that both pieces inherit keeps its persistent id in the first piece and gets a fresh id with a lineage entry in the later ones (ids stay unique across bodies). Plane tools are finite faces sized from the body box. `Move` bakes through the worker; `Copy` makes N new bodies stepped by the same translate or rotate, with fresh ids.

Reference geometry is the drawing table `refgeo` (record: kind, method, params as given, resolved geometry in document units, status, seq). Planes: world, face, offset, parallel through a point, three points, edge and point, angle about an axis, midplane of two parallel planes, perpendicular to an axis. Axes: two points, edge (line, or circle axis), cylindrical or conical face, two planes, point and direction, plane normal. Points: coordinates, vertex, edge midpoint, circle centre, face centre, axis and plane, centre of mass, offset. Coordinate systems: world, three points, origin and two directions, from a plane. Faces and edges are named by persistent id (a lost id is resolved through lineage only when it became exactly one face, else the item goes to `status: error` and keeps its last geometry; the list is never dropped); vertices have no persistent ids and are referenced by index of the body's current shape. `rebuild` re-resolves in creation order. Deleting an item another one uses is refused; renaming rewrites the users. P1F plane source `refgeo` is registered by `bodies/planesource.py`.

Configurations are the drawing table `configs`: a `Default` record (the values the model has before a configuration changes them) plus one record per configuration holding only overrides for parameters (expression text, validated by the S2 expression parser; they are applied with `param.ops.set_parameter`, so a refused solve rolls the whole activation back), suppression (feature id or label; through `app.part.store` when a P1F tree exists, else kept in the `@suppress` record) and a material per body. A key first used by any configuration is captured into Default from the live value, so switching back always restores the base. `@state` holds the active name. Blank design-table cells inherit. The design table is the same data as a grid; CSV columns are `Configuration`, `Description`, a parameter name (or `param:NAME`), `suppress:FEATURE` with S or U (R01), `material:BODY` (body name, or `#handle` when names clash). Import replaces all configurations in one undo step and re-activates the active one; a bad cell refuses the whole import. Files: `.csv` or the JSON `.fcdt` (format `formcad-design-table`); writes go through `outputs.checked`, reads check extension, regular file and 2 MB; a cell that would start a spreadsheet formula (`=`, `+`, `@`, `-x`) is written with one leading space and trimmed on import. The routes `designtable/export` and `designtable/import` already fall under the macro run-token path denial; the library write routes are named `materiallib` and added to `guard.MACRO_DENIED_LATER`, and the command `materiallib` is in both barred sets.

Materials: `app/data/materials.json` is the shipped library (kg/m3, GPa, Poisson dimensionless) with a provenance column and `single_source_fields`; it holds only the three rows R16 verified (304, 6061, Ti-6Al-4V, the last marked `single_source`). The user adds rows to `<data>/materials-user.json` (validated ranges, no shadowing of a shipped name, atomic write). Assigning a material copies its row into the document table `materials` so a file is self-contained; `refresh` re-reads the library.

Mass properties (`bodies/massprops.py`): the worker integrates with `eps` 1e-9 (adaptive Gauss) and returns inertia about the centre of mass per unit density; the engine sums bodies with the parallel axis theorem, then reports inertia about the centre of mass (world axes), principal moments and axes, and about a chosen coordinate system (a `refgeo` coordinate system or plane, `world`, or an inline definition) expressed in that system's axes, with the centre of mass in it. Units: mm, kg, mm3, mm2, kg*mm2, density kg/m3 (mass = volume mm3 x 1e-9 x density). Volume, centre and inertia are reported only when every body is a closed valid solid; mass needs a material on every body or a `density` override, otherwise inertia is per unit density in mm5 and said so. `bodies/exitcheck` compares bodies carrying `source.expect` with their closed forms.

## Dress-up, pattern and mirror features, turn 7 (kernel/kernel_features2.py, solid/dressup.py)

Worker methods `feature_fillet`, `feature_chamfer`, `feature_shell`, `feature_draft`, `feature_pattern`, `feature_mirror`, `feature_precheck`. Every one answers a structured result instead of raising for geometry failures: `ok`, `message`, `errors` and `warnings` (each `{message, code, edges, faces, vertices}` as worker indices), `failed {edges, faces}`, and on success `id`, `summary`, `history.inputs` in the shape of `boolean`, `created` (output faces and edges no input maps to) and the blend or drafted faces. Malformed input (negative size, bad index) still raises. Fillet and chamfer call the OCP builders directly; fillet reports `StripeStatus` and the faulty contours and vertices, chamfer has no such queries in OCP so a failed build is re-run chain by chain to name the failing edges. Contours ending at a vertex with four or more edges are warnings, not blocks: the kernel documents the case as unhandled but may succeed, and a failure then carries the code. Shell blocks on C0 spline faces and faces below C1 (error) and warns on vertices over three edges and walls over half the smallest size; an inward shell must remove volume, and an outward shell is the wall only (the body becomes the cavity). Intersection flag stays off. Draft takes only plane, cylinder and cone faces, checks AddDone per face, uses ProblematicShape then Remove, and on a failed Build re-runs per face; a positive angle removes material on the pull side of the neutral plane, and `allow_partial` keeps the faces that worked. Patterns copy the seed with BRepBuilderAPI_Transform (copy on) and combine every instance in one parallel BRepAlgoAPI_Fuse or Cut call (a BuilderAlgo) with history on; a bare general fuse would leave a split compound, so the boolean subclass is used. Instance 0 is the seed in place and is expected to be in the body already (`include_seed` applies it too). History per instance is composed from seed face to instance face to result face (`instance_faces`). Face fillet and full round are not offered: R16 shows no OCCT support, so a face selection means every shared edge of that face. `solid/dressup.py` holds schemas, document-unit to millimetre conversion, persistent id to index resolution (a missing id answers `ok: false` with the lost ids) and the history parts for `ids.inherit`; P1F registers the features over it.

## Audit minors closed, turn 7 (solid/planes.py, routers/planes.py, automation/plugins.py, data/sdk/_host.py, data/sdk/formcad.py, formcad_engine.py, automation/cli.py, doc/viewops.py, services/kernel.py, routers/automation.py, lisp/lib_misc.py)

Construction plane algebra moved into the engine: `solid/planes.py` holds the six world planes, plane through three points (collinear refused), plane on a face from a point and normal, local coordinates, the grid step (a power of ten near a twelfth of the visible height), the grid extent and the quantising of a picked point to the grid. `POST /api/docs/{id}/plane` takes `{world}`, `{points}` or `{face:{point,normal}}` and answers the plane (origin, x, y, z, name); `POST /api/docs/{id}/grid` takes the plane, the view target, visible height and radius and answers `{step, extent, center, major}`, plus `snapped` when a `point` is sent (with `step`, else the computed one). The interface keeps only the ray-to-plane hit and the dot product that places the grid centre every frame; it asks for new grid lines only when the zoom (half octave), the radius or the target distance (octave) changes, one request in flight, the newest wins, and the grid stays invisible (alpha 0) until the first answer. A picked point is quantised by the engine only when SNAPMODE is on and no object snap applies. Entry-point plugins: the distribution hash is sha256 over the listed distribution files (RECORD excluded, bytecode excluded) and every code file under the top package the entry point names (so an editable install is covered); it is shown as `manifestSha256`, `set_enabled` demands the shown value and stores it as `distSha256`, and discovery holds the plugin back (state mismatch) when the files differ, when the version differs, or when the record has no hash (enabled before hashes). Gap: the files are hashed at discovery and imported a moment later, a local writer can win that race; folder plugins have the same shape. Macro host: `os.path.exists`, `lexists`, `isfile`, `isdir`, `islink`, `getsize`, `getmtime`, `getatime` and `getctime` now raise PermissionError for a path outside the read roots (they used to swallow the denial as False); `pathlib.Path.exists` follows because it calls the checked `os.stat`. `os.lstat` is left alone because `os.path.realpath` calls it inside the check itself. SDK: a 401 from the engine reads "FormCAD does not let a macro call PATH. Macros draw and edit; saving, opening, exporting, printing and settings are done from the FormCAD window." instead of the bare status text. `formcad-engine --settings-dir DIR COMMAND ...` works with the flag before the command word (applied in `formcad_engine.main`, then the command runs; the same flag after the word still works); `mcp --connect HOST:PORT` exits 2 at start when the host is not 127.0.0.1, localhost or ::1 (the per-message check in `discover_engine` stays). VPORTS 2, 3 and 4 now divide the whole window, so 4 on a split view gives four tiles (the old rule divided only the current tile and gave five); the largest new tile becomes current; the spec text is silent on this and the count is what the owner asked for. The kernel worker, after a call timeout, is killed and reaped before the error is raised, so the next call finds it dead and restarts it (serial changes, loaded ids drop). The interpreter setting accepts only python, pythonw, python3, python3.N, py and pypy names (optional .exe). `(ver)` reads the package version. The control port stays full trust by design and is now said so on Preferences, Agents.

## Audit round three, v0.6.0 (cmd/barred.py, cmd/runner.py, cmd/script.py, core/guard.py, core/outputs.py and every file writer below)

R7 and R8: every writer that takes a caller-given path now goes through `outputs.checked` with its own extension set. TABLEEXPORT (`csv_target`, both the typed and programmatic forms: .csv, .txt), `dataextract.write_file` (.csv, .txt), `blockio.output_path` for WBLOCK (.fcad, .dxf, .dwg, .dwt), layer state export (.fcls; an unknown extension is now refused instead of accepted), `layout.saveas` (.dwt), plot and SVG output in `output/plot.py` (the extension of the chosen device), plot style save (`.ctb`/`.stb` by kind), STEP export (.step, .stp) and `service.save` when a path is given (.fcad, .dxf, .dwg, .dwt). Writers that take no caller path were left: settings, tokens and lock files, autosave and recovery (derived names), audit report (derived), plot device files and alias profile (fixed folders and sanitised names), the macro private copy and output files, temporary inputs for the kernel and for inline compare data, DWG converter staging. `.dwt` accepts an existing text, DWG or container file so a template can be saved again.

Systemic guard: the run token carries the macro's granted folders (`guard.RUN_FILES`, `guard.FILES` context var, copied into a started SCRIPT as `ScriptRun.files`). `barred.args_refusal` refuses, for a macro or an MCP agent, any programmatic argument named path, paths, file, files, filename, folder, dir or out, or ending in _path or _file, unless its value is inside a granted folder (agents: folders listed under `agentFolders` in automation.json, empty by default); the dedicated `formcad_*` file tools are unchanged. `barred.prompt_refusal` stops a typed answer to any free-text prompt whose message mentions a file, folder, path or directory, because prompts carry no file marker. Cost: macros and agents lose file arguments on commands such as INSERT from a file or SCRIPT with a path unless the folder is granted.

## Audit round two, v0.6.0 (core/guard.py, cmd/barred.py, cmd/runner.py, cmd/script.py, automation/{mcp,forms,plugins,macros,control,stdio}.py, data/sdk/_host.py, core/outputs.py, lisp/support.py, routers/automation.py)

Blocker 1, every link closed. (1) `guard.CALLER` is a ContextVar set to "macro" when a per-run token matched and to "agent" by `mcp.Server.handle`; the guard also refuses a run token on `/api/docs` open, recovery, autosave, save, plot, macro, export, xrefs and import. (2) `cmd/barred.py`: a macro caller may only run commands in the drawing groups (draw, modify, format, annotate, constraints, inquiry, block, layout, solids, edit, view, tools, script, settings, extra) plus purge and audit, and never aliasedit, reinit or dsettings; `Runner` checks in `_launch`, the programmatic form, the typed paren and `!` branch and the transparent branch, and every LISP line is refused for a macro, including those inside a SCRIPT run it started (`ScriptRun.caller`). MCP agents are refused pluginenable, aliasedit, reinit, appload, macrosave and macroplay, and `forms.command_tools` no longer lists them. (3) The `support` action is gone from the programmatic `lisp` form; support folders change only through `GET/PUT /api/automation/lisp-support`, which sits behind the session token and which a run token cannot reach, and the interface now calls it. `lisp/support.py` refuses the plugins folder, the macros folder, the data folder root and any folder that holds them, on write and on read. (4) `plugins.set_enabled` refuses an empty `manifestSha256` for folder plugins. Verified in scratch: the audit's macro (support folders, LISP `open` writes, `pluginenable`) is refused at every step, and so are the same calls through the MCP tools; the plugin file is never created. Known edge: the SDK's `doc.save`, `open_document` and `export` now fail for a macro (401), by design; a command typed in the window while a macro-started SCRIPT is paused uses the script's caller and can be refused.

Major 2: `core/outputs.checked(path, allowed, default)` is the one output gate for SAVEAS, EXPORT, alias files and recorded macros: a fixed set of extensions, a default extension when none is given, an existing folder, and a refusal to overwrite a file whose first bytes are another kind (a text file named .fcad, a PDF named .dxf). Major 7: the macro host receives a private copy of the bytes the engine hashed (`macros.snapshot` reads once), re-hashes them against `FORMCAD_MACRO_SHA256` and compiles and runs exactly those bytes; `os.listdir`, `os.scandir` and `os.stat` are checked against the read roots; POST `/macros/trust` needs `sha256` to trust. Minors: the control port reads at most 64 KiB until a request authenticates, then the full limit, and closes after a bad token; `discover_engine` sends the token only to 127.0.0.1, localhost or ::1; a kernel call that times out kills the worker so the next call restarts it; `(ver)` returns "FormCAD LISP 0.6 (en)"; `_.LINE` resolves; the interpreter setting needs a file name starting with py; `TeighaFileConverter.exe` joins the known converter names.

## Integration fixes, turn 6 (display/state.py, display/constraint_marks.py, doc/viewops.py, routers/layers.py, routers/canvas.py, doc/document.py, cmd/commands/panels.py, lisp/interp.py, lisp/support.py) · _v0.6.0

Constraint marks on lines: `display/state.py` owns a `LINE_HOOKS` list that `line_entry` calls after it builds an entry; `constraint_marks` appends its hook when it is imported, so the line fast path no longer depends on the constraints router (the monkeypatch of `state.line_entry` and `install()` are gone). The list is defined before the package imports because `state` and `constraint_marks` import each other. Named 3D views: `save_view` takes an optional `camera3d` (position, target, up, height, projection, fov; checked for shape and finite numbers, rejected with a ViewError otherwise), stores it in the view record, `list_views` and `restore_view` return it; plain 2D views carry none and the record is saved with the drawing. Selection route: `PUT /selection` accepts `faces`, `edges` (`{body, face|edge}` persistent ids) and `owners`, kept in `doc.subselection` with the same set, add and remove modes as the handles; `GET /selection` returns them; undo clears them. Entries without a face or edge id are ignored. Panel words: `LISPCONSOLE` (aliases VLIDE, LISPIDE) and `MACRORECORDER` (alias MACROS) are engine commands that only say the panel belongs to the interface, so a typed word reaches the interface interceptor the way BLOCKSPALETTE does; their programmatic forms return `{panel}`. LISP memory: besides the live-object count, each check (every 4096 steps) compares the process resident size with its value at the start of the run (`/proc/self/statm`, else allocated blocks times 64) against a byte budget, default 128 MB, set by `memory_mb` in `lisp-settings.json` (clamped 16 to 4096). Measured on this machine, fresh process, peak growth: consing lists 128 MB in 2.9 s (was 413 MB in 9.6 s), consing strings 128 MB in 5.1 s (was 482 MB in 20.2 s); with 32 MB the stop comes at 32 MB. Known: the process size includes other engine threads, so a heavy tessellation running at the same time can trip the budget early; the message names the budget. The CLI shared flag `--settings-dir` is only recognised after the command word.

## Embedded LISP dialect · _v0.6.0 (lisp/*, cmd/commands/lisp.py, cmd/prompts.py, cmd/runner.py, cmd/lisphook.py)

Built only from the B6 spec as the package `app/lisp/` (types, reader, printer, interp, lib_* for numbers, lists, strings, misc, input, command, entities, selection sets, tables and system variables, session, hooks). The engine never imports OCP or build123d. Values: nil is Python `None`, T and the null symbol are interned symbols, lists are `Cons` cells, integers wrap to 32 bits, reals are floats, entity names carry the handle, selection sets a handle list. 188 names are implemented (14 special forms and 174 functions: the spec list names 175 function tokens but `cdddr` and `load` each appear twice, and `cddddr` was added); the registry holds 189 entries counting the 14 forms.

Evaluation is recursive with a step budget (500 million), a depth cap (100000 user calls), 60 s of running time (time spent waiting for input does not count), a live-object growth cap (9 million blocks, the stand-in for the 5 million cons cells) and a 2 GB output cap; each stops the routine with a message. A routine always runs on its own worker thread with a 512 MB stack, handing `doc.lock` over with the thread (ping-pong, only one side runs at a time), so deep recursion cannot crash a request thread and input functions can wait. A get* call installs a shell run on the command runner whose prompt carries a parse function (`Prompt.parse`, `hint == "raw"`: the spec messages come from the LISP side); `pause` in `command` wraps the open command's generator in a tap and the runner state poll resumes the routine once the user answered. Esc closes the shell and the routine ends with "Function cancelled" (`*error*` runs once, after unwinding). Programmatic evaluation never waits for the user: input functions take pre-supplied answers and return nil when none are left.

Undo: no outer transaction. Every inner command and every direct edit commits its own step; when the routine ends the steps made since it began are merged into one step labelled with the command (or LISP), earliest `before` wins, so U inside a routine still works and one U after it takes back all of it. `entdel` restores only what the same routine deleted.

Safety: file functions go through `lisp/support.py`: folders in `<data>/lisp-settings.json` (key `support`, default `<data>/support`), realpath containment so `..` and symlinks cannot leave, writes only to the first folder for relative names, 64 MiB per file. APPLOAD (any `.lsp` path, case-insensitive; the user chose it) registers the file by name so `(load name)` finds it. Startup files (acad.lsp once, acaddoc.lsp per drawing, then S::STARTUP) are read lazily on the first routine of a drawing from the first support folder only. No network, no shell, no `vla-` or `dos_` names; absent names give "no function definition". Output is buffered by line; the null symbol prints nothing.

Hooks: `runner._tokens` groups a parenthesised token (strings honoured); `_idle` sends `(` and `!` lines to `hooks.run_line` and tries a defined `c:NAME` before the sysvar fallback (built-in commands win, the lookup is off while a routine runs); `_feed` takes an expression as the answer to a prompt (number, string or point as typed text, nil gives no input); `state()` polls a paused routine. `Prompt` gained `parse`. Left open: transparent `'(...)` forms, the `1>` continuation prompt for an unbalanced typed line (an error is shown instead), custom keyword shortcuts that disagree with the runner's own.

## Automation surface: CLI, MCP, control protocol, Python macros, plugins · _v0.6.0 (automation/*, data/sdk/*, routers/{mcp,automation,docquery}.py, cmd/commands/zz_plugins.py, core/guard.py, core/runtime.py, formcad_engine.py, main.py)

MCP is written by FormCAD as plain JSON-RPC, no SDK and no new dependency (R14: the SDK adds about fourteen packages and has no proven frozen stdio). One `Server` class answers both eras: `server/discover` and per-request `_meta` (2026-07-28: `resultType`, `ttlMs`, `cacheScope`, `-32602` for a missing `_meta`, `-32022` with the supported list) and `initialize` plus `notifications/initialized` (2025-11-25 down to 2024-11-05, which echoes the client's version when known). A request without `_meta` is accepted only after `initialize` on the same stdio stream, or on HTTP when `MCP-Protocol-Version` names an older revision; the HTTP route builds a fresh `Server` per POST, so no state crosses requests. The unverified fields were checked against the 2026-07-28 schema this turn: `cacheScope` is `public` or `private`, `ttlMs` a number, `Implementation` has `name`, `title`, `version`. Tools: 22 fixed (`formcad_*`: command line, script, execute, list commands, documents, inspect, query entities, find text, render, selection, cancel, undo, redo, export, display list, variables) plus one `cmd_<id>` per programmatic command form, sorted, cached until a plugin changes the catalogue. Schemas: 17 core commands have typed curated schemas (`automation/forms.py CURATED`), the rest list the argument names read from the execute function's bytecode (works frozen, where source is absent) with `additionalProperties` true and an `inputs` array for typed answers; commands whose forms hide the names behind a helper show `inputs` only (gap). Arguments are validated against the schema by a small checker (types, required, bounds, unknown fields on curated tools), and a refused call is `-32602`; a command that runs and fails is an `isError` result with the engine's message. Hints: draw, view and inquiry tools are non-destructive, modify tools and file overwrites destructive, listed queries read-only; the hints are only hints, nothing relies on them. `--tools core` serves the 22 fixed tools plus the 17 curated ones for clients that cap the tool count.

stdio: `formcad-engine mcp` runs a headless in-process session (`sys.stdout` is swapped to stderr so a stray print cannot corrupt the stream; exit on stdin close). `mcp --connect HOST:PORT|auto` is a bridge: each line is POSTed to the running engine's `/mcp` with the session token (env `FORMCAD_URL` and `FORMCAD_TOKEN`, or `engine.port` and `session.token` in the data folder for `auto`); a refused connection answers "did not run", a lost reply to a `tools/call` answers "may have run, inspect first" and is never resent; `--connect` without a value is a usage error, never a headless fallback; `--launch` is refused. HTTP: one `POST /mcp` behind the existing token, Origin and Host guard; GET and DELETE are 405; `Mcp-Method` and `Mcp-Name` must match the body for the new era (400, `-32020`). Progress and cancellation are not sent: every tool returns before the next message is read.

Control protocol (F2): newline JSON on 127.0.0.1 when started with `--control-port N` (or `FORMCAD_CONTROL_PORT`, 0 picks a port); each request carries the token; the token is a fresh `token_urlsafe(32)` written O_EXCL, mode 0600, to `<data>/control-<port>.token` (stale ones are removed at start); 16 connections, 256 MiB line, 64 queued, 10 minutes idle, 60 seconds per call with `not-run` or `may-have-run` on timeout; a non-JSON-object line gets one error and the connection is closed. The methods are the F2 groups that exist without a window (line, engine, drawing, capture, app, meta); the UI observation, UI action and window screenshot groups belong to the interface and are not served. The same dispatcher is `POST /api/automation/control` behind the HTTP guard.

CLI (F1, `automation/cli.py`): info, convert, run, commands, mcp, perf, sample, plus plugin (seal, list) and macro. Options are parsed by a small parser that rejects unknown and repeated flags and a value starting with a dash unless written `--flag=value` (negative numbers are values); exit 0, 1 (a step failed), 2 (usage). `convert` forces the output type from the extension (pdf, png 2400x1600, svg, dxf with `--dxf-version`, dwg, fcad), refuses OUT equal to IN without `--in-place` (through `samefile`), reads fcad, dxf, dwg and svg. `run` executes `--sample`, `--new` (metric), `--script`, `--script-file`, `--lisp-file`, `--python-macro`, `--command "id {json}"` in the order typed; the placeholder `"last handle"` at any depth becomes the last handle a step returned. The headless session uses the real service singleton with autosave off. `--lisp-file` calls an `appload` or `load` command when one has a programmatic form; the LISP package has no entry yet at the time of writing, so the step fails with a clear message until it does.

Python macros: the client module `formcad` and the host `_host.py` are standard-library-only files in `app/data/sdk/` (bundled by the existing `--add-data app/data`). A macro runs as `python -I _host.py FILE` in a throwaway working folder with a scrubbed environment (PATH only, no HOME, no PYTHON*), stdin closed, output to files and cut at 256 KB, its own session or process group killed on the timeout, rlimits on POSIX (CPU, address space 1 GiB, file size 16 MiB, open files, processes), a Job Object on Windows (kill on close, process count, memory, CPU; written from the documented API and not run here). The host adds an audit hook that blocks writes outside the working folder and granted folders, reads, directory listings and stats outside the interpreter, the SDK, the throwaway folder and granted folders (the macro's own folder is no longer readable unless granted; /proc and /sys never), network other than the engine's loopback port, new processes and native code. This is containment for mistakes and hostile snippets, not a security boundary: the macro still runs as the user, and an audit hook can be bypassed by code that already runs natively, which is why `ctypes` is blocked and why the preferences must say so. Trust is per file: `automation.json` in the data folder stores the sha256 of the source and the grants (network, subprocess, native, folders); an edited file loses trust; the interface shows the source and the hash and passes the hash back when trusting. The macro gets a per-run token (`guard.issue_run_token`) valid only under `/api/docs` and `/api/commands`, revoked when the run ends. The 0.6.0 text said this meant a macro could not enable plugins; the audit showed that false (programmatic `lisp support` plus `pluginenable`), and the round-two fix below is what makes it true: the token is refused on open, save, plot, macro, export, xrefs and import routes, and the runner refuses every command outside the drawing groups and every LISP line for a macro caller. The interpreter is the user's Python (setting `python`, with detection), the engine's own Python when not frozen, and the frozen engine itself through `formcad-engine macro --host FILE` when no interpreter is set.

Plugins: nothing is imported at discovery. Tier one is the entry point group `formcad.plugins` (enabled flag stored with the installed version; a changed version disables it); tier two is `<data>/plugins/<id>/plugin.json` with `id`, `version`, `api`, `entry`, `permissions` and a `files` table of sha256 per file. Enabling stores the manifest hash the user was shown; loading requires every listed file to match, no unlisted code file in the folder, and the stored manifest hash to match. A plugin defines `register(api)`; `api.command` and `api.executes` register through the normal registry (so menu entries, the catalogue and MCP tools follow), names that already exist are refused, a failed register is rolled back, and disabling unregisters the commands (module code already imported stays in memory until restart). Plugin code runs in the engine process because it must register commands there; enabling is therefore the trust decision, and the manager must say so. `formcad-engine plugin seal DIR` writes the hash table. Enabled plugins load when the command modules load (`cmd/commands/zz_plugins.py`, last in import order).

Guard changes: `guard.py` gains run tokens (scoped by path prefix, HTTP only). `docs.py` already owned `/{doc_id}/selection`, so the macro client uses that route; new document routes live in `routers/docquery.py` (entities, variable, summary, export, render).

## Kernel worker operations and 3D documents · _v0.6.0 (kernel/*, solid/*, doc/kinds/solidbody.py, routers/scene.py, cmd/commands/solids.py)

Units: kernel millimetres, document units converted in `solid/work.py` with `param.units.mm_per_unit`; the scene buffer is converted back to document units. Bodies are entities of kind `solidbody` in the model space (name, colour, layer, source, BREP blob id, version, persistent ids, summary, 2D extent, offset), so undo and redo ride the normal transaction. The BREP text and the cached tessellation are content-addressed blobs in `Drawing.blobs`, written to `parts/<id>.brep` and `cache/<id>-d<detail>.mesh` in the `.fcad` zip (only for bodies still present) and read back on load. Blobs are immutable, so an undo only swaps the record; the in-memory dict is not trimmed until save. Worker shapes are cached by (blob, offset) with an LRU of 96 and keyed to the worker serial, so a restarted worker reloads from the blob.

Moving: the generic MOVE calls `SolidBody.move_by`, which accumulates `offset` (document units); the scene adds the offset, the worker bakes it (`transform`) only when a boolean, mass or export needs the shape. Rotate, scale and mirror of solids are not wired into the 2D commands; `SOLIDMOVE` (translate, rotate, scale, matrix) bakes through the worker.

Commands: BOX, CYLINDER, SPHERE, CONE, TORUS, EXTRUDE (closed polyline, circle or region; negative height goes down), MASSPROP, SOLIDMOVE. UNION, SUBTRACT and INTERSECT dispatch by kind inside the existing commands (single-region edits in `boundary.py`): if the first selection holds a solid the solid path runs, otherwise the region path is unchanged. The first solid survives as the result and keeps its handle; the others are erased. Primitives stand on the world Z axis (no 3D UCS yet); `axis` is accepted by the programmatic CYLINDER only.

Mesh: `BRepMesh_IncrementalMesh` with absolute linear deflection `max(0.0008 x bbox diagonal x detail, 0.001 mm)` and angular 0.3 rad (0.4 and 0.5 for coarser detail); per-face nodes, triangle winding flipped for reversed faces, normals from `Poly_Triangulation.ComputeNormals`, edge polylines from `PolygonOnTriangulation` with a `GCPnts_TangentialDeflection` fallback. Bounds: `bounds` defaults to the exact geometry box (`BRepBndLib.AddOptimal` without triangulation) because the triangulation box is enlarged by the deflection (a 100 mm box reported -0.022); the mesh box in the file header is the min and max of the nodes. Volume is reported only for a closed valid solid (`ShapeAnalysis_FreeBounds` finds no free edge and `BRepCheck_Analyzer` is valid), otherwise `volume` is null with a note.

Persistent ids (`solid/ids.py`): one document-wide counter, ids for faces and edges, inherited through the worker history (Modified, Generated, IsDeleted per input face and edge, input order base then tools). Split policy: the first output keeps the id, later outputs get new ids with a `lineage` entry naming the parent; merged faces keep the first input's id; unclaimed outputs get new ids. Ids are kept through transform, subtract and union (checked); a counter burned by an undone operation is never reused.

Files: STEP through XCAF (names, colours, assemblies flattened with locations applied, compounds of solids split to one body each), written as AP214 unless `schema` says ap203 or ap242; IGES through `IGESControl_Reader` under a lock, read only. OPEN, IMPORT and the interchange open route accept `.step .stp .iges .igs`; EXPORT writes `.step`; SAVEAS refuses them. A STEP document is untitled until saved as `.fcad`. The worker moves OpenCascade console text to stderr (stdout carries only protocol lines). Large buffers travel by temporary files named by the engine, never the JSON line; limits: 512 MB input, 256 MB blob, 4 million triangles, 60 s short and 300 s long calls.

Left open: no colour per face from STEP, no TNaming, no fillet or chamfer operation, no 3D UCS, no taper, rotate and scale of solids from the 2D commands, pruning of unreferenced blobs in memory.

## Scripts, aliases, file commands, inquiry and the macro recorder · _v0.6.0 (cmd/script.py, cmd/macro.py, cmd/aliasfile.py, cmd/lisphook.py, cmd/commands/{scripts,macros,aliases,filecmds,inquire,toggles}.py, doc/{audit,compare,count,queries,recover,savecheck}.py, doc/templates.py, doc/service.py, routers/filetools.py, cmd/runner.py, cmd/registry.py, main.py)

Scripts run through the real runner: `cmd/script.py` keeps one `ScriptRun` on the runner, and `state()` drains it (so a script finishes inside the request that started it, and a poll advances a script that waited). Lines go to `runner._submit`, never `submit`, so the recorder does not record a playing script. A blank line is Enter at a prompt and nothing at idle (a repeat of the last command inside a script would be a trap); a trailing space is one Enter; at a free-text prompt the whole line is the text. Own directives: `^C` cancels the running command, `@pick x,y [shift]` picks the object nearest the point (an entity prompt, an object selection, or the selection at idle), `@box x0,y0 x1,y1` selects by window (x1 at or right of x0) or crossing, `@handles h,h` selects by hex handle. Lines starting `(` go to `cmd/lisphook.lisp_line`, the one function pointing at the LISP evaluator. An error in a line pauses the script on that line; RESUME continues with the next line; Esc (cancel) pauses a running script and a second Esc stops it. DELAY is transparent so it works between the inputs of a command; it never sleeps, it sets a wake time and a timer re-enters the script under the document lock. RSCRIPT repeats until cancelled, with a 50 ms gap, or `times` runs in the programmatic form. A script is limited to 4 MB, 200000 lines per file and 2 million lines run in total; only `.scr` files.

Aliases: FormCAD's own alias file is the `.fcalias` text file, `ALIAS, *COMMAND` per line, `;` comments, `[section]` lines ignored, the published `.pgp` layout is read too; lines for external programs are refused with a warning, never run. Limits: 2000 aliases, 32 characters, 256 characters a line, 50 warnings. Only `registry.USER_ALIASES` changes; the last loaded file is remembered in `<data>/aliases.json` and read again when the command modules load (`registry.load_modules` calls `aliasfile.startup`). Command names always beat aliases (`resolve` order is unchanged).

File commands: NEW asks for a template (default QNEWTEMPLATE, `.` for none) and accepts a built-in name, a path, or a name in `<data>/templates/`; a template file is `.fcad`, `.dwt` or `.dxf` and the result is an untitled drawing. QNEW never asks. CLOSE and CLOSEALL ask Yes/No/Cancel when a drawing has changes and close through `runner.later` (a command cannot close the document whose generator is running); the programmatic forms never ask. SAVE (alias QSAVE) refuses while a reference is edited in place (also enforced in `service.save` and `save_foreign`), writes DXF and DWG with the version and binary choice remembered from SAVEAS, and asks "Save anyway" when `savecheck.loss` finds objects the DXF writer drops; the answer is remembered per file and loss. AUDIT lists up to 100 lines, repairs in one undo step, writes `.adt` beside a saved drawing when AUDITCTL is on; generated dimension blocks do not exist in this engine so that repair is absent, and constraint records are checked through their `handles`, `entities`, `objects` or `refs` lists. RECOVER reads a damaged `.fcad` leniently (`doc/recover.py`: bad objects dropped and counted) and then audits and fixes. COMPARE keeps its result on the document (`doc.compare`), matches by full signature (equal handle first), then changed (same handle and kind), 200000 objects, 1000 listed per kind, a grid of at most 8 x 8 for change areas; COMPAREEXPORT recolours copies (current 3, other 1, both 8) and recoloured block copies, but not styles.

Inquiry: LIST, STATUS, TIME, COUNT, COUNTLIST, COUNTTABLE and the program queries (INSPECTDRAWING, QUERYENTITIES, QUERYTEXTS, ANALYZESHAPES, IMPLIEDLOOPS, ANALYZEUSAGE) are commands with typed and programmatic forms, and also routes in `routers/filetools.py`. COUNT reports dynamic-block instances under their base block. Implied loops treat segment ends within the tolerance as one node, exclude every segment at a junction of three or more ends, and report a spur when both ends of one segment meet.

Macro recorder: the runner calls `runner.rec(kind, ...)` at its public input points; the recorder turns them into script lines (typed text, canvas points as `x,y`, picks as `@pick`, canvas selections as `@box` or `@handles`, cancel as `^C`, programmatic commands as `name {json}`). Python output is `import formcad`, `doc.send(line)` per typed line and `doc.script(marker)` for the markers. Playing a `.py` macro goes through `automation/macros.run` on a thread, never under the document lock; macros this engine saved are trusted by file hash, any other file needs the trust step of the automation module.

Also added: TRANSPARENCYDISPLAY, QPMODE, SELECTIONCYCLING, ISODRAFT, ISOPLANE and DSETTINGS (typed loop and programmatic form). Left open: portable fonts option of SAVEAS, the Template option of LAYOUT stays with F6.

## Drafting constraints, parameters and the Phase 15 sketch · _v0.6.0 (param/*, solve/constraints.py, solve/model.py, doc/tables.py, doc/kinds/constrainthook.py, display/constraint_marks.py, display/state.py, cmd/constraint_flow.py, cmd/commands/constraints_*.py, routers/constraints.py, main.py)

Storage is three new drawing tables (`TABLE_NAMES`): `constraints` (one record per relation or dimension: id, kind, refs `[handle, part]`, sketch, dimension label, expression, value, driving flag, solver context), `parameters` (user parameters: name, expression, unit, comment) and `sketches` (name, plane, entity handles, construction handles). Tables give undo, redo, save, reopen, autosave and the DXF private records for free, and are read through `d.table_set`. Settings (tolerances, inference, bar and dimension display with per-object exceptions) are one header value `CONSTRAINTSETTINGS`. Dimension names (d1, ang1, rad1, dia1) are unique across the document and share one namespace with user parameters; names equal to units, functions or `pi`, `e`, `tau` are reserved.

Solver changes (additive): `Sketch.restore` (re-enter a stored constraint with its id and context), `Sketch.measure` (the value a dimension would have now), `Sketch.add` takes `cid` and `ctx`, per-parameter weights (`set_param_weight`), and Fix now keeps its target in the constraint context (`pt`, `base`) so a reopened drawing keeps its fixed places. Sign contexts (distance direction, tangent side, symmetric pairing) are stored in the record and passed back on every rebuild.

The solver is rebuilt from the records on every operation, only for the connected component the operation touches (sketch members are always loaded whole). Adding a constraint is a trial: the constraint is restored into the throwaway solver, solved and analysed; only if it is neither conflicting nor redundant are records and geometry written. A refusal writes nothing and carries an explanation (`param/explain.py`): for a conflict the set is reduced by deletion (drop each constraint, keep the drop when the rest is still unsolvable from the start or two jittered starts; budget 2 s, `minimal` false when the budget ran out); for redundancy the set is the new constraint plus the earlier ones its Jacobian row depends on, reduced the same way by rank. Over-defining by a parameter change (a new value that cannot be met) is refused the same way after a stepped retry in 8 increments. `force` keeps a redundant (not conflicting) constraint; the status then reads over defined.

Expressions (`param/expr.py`): numbers, names, quoted names `'d1@Sketch1'`, `name@sketch`, `+ - * / ^`, unary minus, functions (sqrt, abs, trig in degrees, min, max, pow, hypot, ln, log, exp, round, floor, ceil, sign, atan2), `pi`, and unit literals (`25mm`, `3 in`, `90 deg`, `0.5 rad`) converted to drawing units (from INSUNITS, else mm or inch) and degrees. A parameter with a unit reads a plain number in that unit. Cycles are found by the lookup stack and named ("Circular reference: a -> b -> a"). A driving dimension cannot use a reference (driven) dimension; user parameters can. Deleting a parameter or dimension that others use is refused ("x is used by y"); a dimension's own name cannot be deleted from PARAMETERS. Rename rewrites whole-name tokens only.

Edit hook (`doc/kinds/constrainthook.py`, priority 60, after the command and before dimension follow): objects erased leave drafting constraints removed with a count, and sketch constraints marked dangling (kept, not solved, restored by undo). Edited constrained geometry is re-solved with the changed parameters weighted 1e8, so a grip drag holds the dragged point and MOVE holds the moved object while partners follow. A Fix whose fixed place was itself edited takes the new place (the user moved it on purpose). If the edit cannot be satisfied it is reverted and "Constraints could not be satisfied" is printed. Operations of this module set `tx.constraint_done` so the hook skips them. CONSTRAINTINFER (the existing system variable) infers coincident, horizontal, vertical, perpendicular and tangent from exact geometry of new objects; parallel inference is not done because snaps are not visible to a hook.

Polylines are chains of solver lines with implicit joints, referenced as `v<n>` and `s<n>` counting from 0; splines, block references, text and others answer "<kind> objects cannot be constrained." Polyline `whole` is refused for Fix (name a vertex). Arcs: tangent is between full circles (solver limit, S1). Angular on two lines accepts up to 180 degrees.

Display: `display/constraint_marks.py` wraps the circle, arc, point, polyline and ellipse builders and, through `install()`, the line fast path, appending one `cbar` prim per constrained object: `{t, state under|fully|over|none, dof, rgb, glyphs[{id,k,p,slot,dangling}], dims[{id,name,expr,value,driving,kind,p}], sketch{name,status,dof}}`. The line path is not a builder so `install()` (called when the constraints router imports) replaces `state.line_entry`; the one Edit in `state.py` only registers the module. Constraint operations touch every entity of the affected component so cached display entries rebuild. Status data is cached per document revision.

Not done: live solve during a grip drag preview (the solve runs at release), midpoint and coradial as named relations (use Coincident with a Fix or Concentric plus Equal), dimension text style, driving a dimension from another sketch except by name, splines, Fully Define Sketch (AUTOCONSTRAIN covers relations, not dimensions).

## Audit minors 7, 24, 25, 26, 30 and LAYOUT Template · _v0.6.0 (cmd/commands/{modify_trim,arrays,mleaders,layouts}.py, cmd/dim_support.py, doc/kinds/array.py, doc/layouttemplate.py, doc/viewops.py, interchange/{dwg,dxf_write}.py, routers/interchange.py, output/{plotstyle,styleops}.py)

TRIM and EXTEND: `run_edit` now returns `(created, erased, changed)`; the change count, the programmatic `handle` form and Crossing count only objects whose data actually differ, so "No objects found." is said once and only when nothing changed (Crossing no longer prints one message per failing object). ARRAYPOLAR: the two axis of rotation points are stored as `AssocArray.axis` (moved and transformed with the array, ignored in 2D, a line says so). DIMLINEAR family: Mtext is its own option, a first line then further lines until an empty line, joined with `\P`; Text stays one line. Corner tie-break kept as written: when the location lies outside both spans the dimension follows the larger offset (farther in x than y gives vertical, equal gives horizontal); the spec is silent, this is the decision. MLEADER: angle limit accepts exactly 180; remembered options carry the style name and are rebuilt from the style when CMLEADERSTYLE changes.

Converter setting accepts only the basenames ODAFileConverter, TeighaFileConverter, dwg2dxf, dxf2dwg (case folded, either path separator), else 400. Converter output is bounded: stdout dropped, stderr to a temp file and only its last 64 KiB read, the message cut to 400 characters. Plot style save forces the table extension (`.fcpsc` or `.fcpsn`, replacing any other); a missing or non text style file answers a StyleError message, no longer a 500.

R2000 frozen layers: an ezdxf round trip (1.4.5) writes group 331 for R2000, R2004, R2007 and R2018 and reads only 331 (a file with 341 reads back an empty list); ezdxf has no API to change the code. Decision: keep 331, no patching of the library; C3 line 80 allows 331. The two silent `except` blocks in the viewport writer now add the warning "Viewport n: frozen layers were not written" or "the clip boundary was not written" (forced with a failing setter: warning appears).

LAYOUT Template (B2 7.1 said unavailable): `layout` action `template` with `path` (.fcad, .dxf or .dwt) and optional `name` (a layout or `*`, default all) imports layouts into the open drawing through new `doc/layouttemplate.py`: page setup, paper space objects (viewports with clip and layer overrides, text, inserts), missing layers, linetypes, text, dimension, multileader, table, multiline styles and plot style tables, and the blocks the paper objects use (anonymous ones renamed `*U<n>`). A name already used gets " (n)". The viewport clip source and dimension associativity are dropped. When template sheet units differ from this drawing the objects are not scaled and a note says so. Typed form: Template asks the file, lists its layouts, asks which. One undo step. Named Viewports: `describe_tiles` adds `previews`, the tile boxes of every saved configuration, for the dialog preview. Known, not mine: VPORTS 4 in an already split view subdivides the current tile (5 tiles).

## Audit follow-up · _v0.5.0 (interchange/dxf_read.py, dxf_write.py, svg_import.py, NOTICE.md)

The 21 section banner comments in the DXF reader, the DXF writer and the SVG importer were removed (they only named the method groups below them: run, header, tables, blocks, layouts, entities, kinds, finish, setup, private, document, shapes, path). The one comment left in `svg_import.py` explains the singular value decomposition used to turn a transform into a stroke scale. `NOTICE.md` now says that DWG converters are optional, installed by the user, run as separate processes and never shipped. Left open from the v0.5.0 audit: R2000 frozen layer group code (C3 asks 341 in a 2000-structure file, ezdxf writes 331), the two silent `except` blocks in the viewport writer, any executable accepted as the converter path, plot style save accepting any path, unbounded converter output. X1's 42 interchange checks rerun after the edit: 42 passed.

## Integration walk fixes: stale display space, Blocks window command · _v0.5.0 (display/__init__.py, cmd/commands/blocks.py, doc/kinds/layervp.py)

`display.build` no longer raises for a layout or block space that is gone (a layout renamed or deleted, the block editor closed): the canvas still holds the old name when the `doc` event arrives and its pull answered 404 (a console error each time). The request now answers with the document's current tab, as a full payload (`since` dropped), and the payload's `space` field tells the canvas where it is; the canvas already adopts `space`. A space that never existed falls back the same way, so the route has no 404 for spaces any more. New command `blockspalette` (aliases `blocks`, `insertpalette`; typed form lists the blocks, programmatic form returns the listing) so the typed word is known to the engine and the interface can open the Blocks window from it; before, the interceptor was registered but the engine answered "Unknown command". `layervp.py` tidied (quotes, one comparison) after the review of the script-edited file.

## File commands OPEN, SAVEAS, EXPORT, IMPORT · _v0.5.0 (cmd/commands/fileio.py)

Typed forms for X1's routes. Each asks for a file name (default offered), and has a programmatic form taking `path` (and `version`, `binary`, `recover`). OPEN calls `service.open`; SAVEAS routes dxf, dwg, dwt to `save_foreign` and anything else to `service.save`, and refuses svg, png, pdf (use EXPORT); EXPORT writes dxf, dwg, dwt through `files.write_any` and svg, png, pdf through the plot runner on the current tab (P1); IMPORT is `files.merge_into` and is undoable. Errors are `CommandError` messages.

## Properties derived fields, polyline area, Quick Select and audit minors · _v0.4.0 (doc/props.py, doc/qselect.py, cmd/commands/qselect.py, routers/properties.py, text/scripts.py, output/pdfwrite.py, formcad_engine.py, cmd/commands/{draw_marks,modify_misc,blocks}.py)

Audit majors 1 to 3 (engine boundary). `doc/props.py`: line `length` and `angle` and circle `circumference` and `area` are settable fields resolved per object in the engine (a zero length line extends along X, angle keeps the length, area at or below zero answers "The area must be greater than zero."); the closed polyline gets a read only `area` field (`measure.polygon_measure`, bulges included, none when open). `PUT /properties` needs no change, it already takes any field name. New `doc/qselect.py` holds the property catalogue (the same properties the dialog offered), the operator tests (equals with 1e-9 relative tolerance, not equal, greater, less, wildcard with `*` and `?`, text compared case blind), scope, include or exclude and append; `cmd/commands/qselect.py` registers `qselect` (typed prompts and the programmatic form, sets the document selection and announces it), and `routers/properties.py` serves `POST /api/docs/{id}/qselect` (body scope, kind, property, operator, value, how, append; returns handles and count, 400 for an unknown property) through that command, plus `GET /api/docs/{id}/qselect?scope=&kind=` for the dialog (kinds with counts, properties with choice lists). The router stays in the properties module so `ROUTERS` is unchanged. The `kind` property compares the kind id (circle, lwpolyline), the same ids the Object type list sends.

Minors. `text/scripts.py` builds the 15 format characters with `chr` from code points (same set, no invisible literals). The Express Tools submenu and menu paths are now "Extra Tools" and the catalogue group `express` is `extra` (draw_marks, modify_misc, blocks). `formcad_engine.py` `--selfcheck` reports uharfbuzz. `output/pdfwrite.py` lost the unused `rect_ring` import, a dead first `alphas` assignment and an unused `contents` list.

## Layer viewport hooks and VPORTS in a layout · _v0.5.0 (doc/kinds/layervp.py, cmd/commands/views.py)

`layervp.py` fills K3's six layer hook lists (usage, info, set, rename, delete, merge) with K4's viewport helpers so VP Freeze and VP Color work through `PUT /layers` with a `viewport` handle. It imports `app.doc.viewports` lazily through `_Lazy` because a top-level import is circular via `model.layout_space`. In `views.py` `vports_exec` hands options 1 to 4 and restore to `mview_exec` when the current space is a layout.

## Interchange · DXF, SVG, DWG converter · _v0.5.0 (interchange/*, routers/interchange.py, doc/service.py, main.py)

DXF through ezdxf 1.4.5 (R12 to R2018, recover mode on structure errors); the Drawing is built by hand so entity handles survive, ezdxf's own table and block handles start above the largest FormCAD handle. Whatever DXF cannot hold natively rides in private XDATA (APPID FORMCAD, zlib+base64 JSON in 1000 groups) and in a FORMCAD_DATA dictionary of XRECORDs; a snapshot is used on read only when its signature still matches the native entity, so edits made elsewhere win. Tables, associative arrays and dimensions are written as geometry blocks (`*T`, `*A`, `*D`) plus an INSERT or DIMENSION, with a snapshot; a foreign ACAD_TABLE reads as a plain INSERT. Unknown entities pass through as raw tag lists. IMAGE is dropped with a note, SORTENTSTABLE is not carried, OLE is out. R12 degrades LWPOLYLINE, ELLIPSE, SPLINE and MTEXT and drops the rest with counts. SVG is an own parser on the stdlib XML reader (DOCTYPE and entities refused, caps from the contract). DWG ships nothing: the converter is off by default, set by the user (ODA File Converter or LibreDWG, detected on PATH and usual folders), run as a separate process with a timeout; the ODA argument order is implemented from the documentation and needs a check on a machine with the converter. `doc/service.py` open and save route by extension (`open_foreign`, `save_foreign`). Hooks filled: `blockio.DXF_WRITER`, `DWG_WRITER`, `register_reader` and `xref.register_reader` for .dxf and .dwg; `blockcache.refresh_all` runs after each read.

## Canvas window selection feeds STRETCH · _v0.4.0 (cmd/runner.py `_canvas_selection`)

A window or crossing drawn on the canvas reaches the engine as a handle list (`selection` input), so `SelState.areas` stayed empty and STRETCH moved every picked object whole instead of only the vertices inside the crossing box (found driving the real interface: a closed polyline with a crossing over its right edge moved all four vertices). `selection` now accepts an optional `box` `[x0, y0, x1, y1]` in the order the corners were given; unless the mode is remove, an area `window` (x1 at or right of x0) or `crossing` with those two corners and the found handles is recorded exactly like a typed Window or Crossing, and STRETCH reads it from `sel_areas` unchanged. Checked: the same crossing now gives x 100, 160, 160, 100.

## Plot and output engine · _v0.5.0 (output/*, routers/plot.py, cmd/commands/plot.py, text/model.py, text/outline.py, text/place.py, main.py)

PLOT, -PLOT, PREVIEW, PUBLISH, EXPORTPDF, PNGOUT, SVGOUT, STYLESMANAGER, PLOTSTYLE and PLOTTERMANAGER have typed prompts and programmatic forms; `/api/plot/*` and `/api/docs/{id}/plot/*` serve the dialog (devices, papers, defaults, jobs, preview raster, style tables). One request dict (`output/request.py`) feeds every output; the sheet is built once into a neutral op list (`output/sheet.py`: strokes, fills, text runs, clips) and the PDF, PNG and SVG writers only walk it. Sheet units are millimetres; a placement is (scale, tx, ty) from drawing units to sheet mm. Model plots default to fit and centre; layouts default to the whole sheet at 1:1 and an explicit fit or scale applies to the sheet box. A zero height or width area (one horizontal line) is allowed as long as the other side has size.

PDF is written by our own small writer (zlib, fontTools, uharfbuzz, no new dependency): user space 1/72 inch, line width never zero (minimum 0.05 mm, settable), explicit cap and join codes, layers as optional content groups, page labels and outline per layout, link annotations with a safe URL filter. Text is real and searchable only when the font is TrueType with an embeddable fsType: glyph runs are captured at the text sinks (`output/textrun.py` patches the sinks per thread; `Glyph.src` records the font and glyph id) and written as Type0 Identity-H CIDFontType2 with a subset FontFile2 and ToUnicode; stroke fonts, CFF and restricted fonts fall back to outline geometry. PNG is written with zlib and pHYs from a numpy rasteriser (anti-aliased strokes, supersampled fills, clip masks, 40M pixel cap that lowers the dpi). SVG has mm width and height and a viewBox in paper mm.

Plot style tables are our own JSON (`formcad-plot-style` v1): `.fcpsc` colour dependent (255 styles) and `.fcpsn` named; five built-ins, drawing tables in the `plotstyles` table shadow them, user files live in the data dir. Devices are `.fcplotter` JSON. Decisions where the spec is silent: diamond cap and join are drawn as round and miter; PNG internal joins are round; viewport text is real only for non-annotative entities under a pure scale; no foreign plot style file is read; ARCH E1 stays unverified in the paper table (25 sizes in `papers.py`).

## Block editor, reference editing, dynamic blocks, external references · _v0.5.0 (doc/blocktools.py, blockedit.py, refedit.py, xref.py, dynblock.py, xclip.py, editstate.py, display/xref_clip.py, cmd/commands/blockedit.py, xref.py, dynblocks.py, routers/xrefs.py, doc/container.py, autosave.py, service.py, document.py, cmd/runner.py, context.py, main.py, display/state.py)

Block editor: BEDIT copies the definition into the scratch block `*BEDIT$name` and records the session in an `edit` dict on the blocks table record, so the session travels with undo, redo and autosave and is stripped on manual save (`editstate.persistent_dict`). BSAVE, BSAVEAS and BCLOSE write back by handle map, so handles in the definition stay stable; a save refreshes references and calls `attribs.sync_block`. Commands that would break the session are refused through `editstate.command_refusal` in `cmd/runner.py`, and PURGE cannot remove scratch blocks, `*REFEDIT` or xref dependents (`block_lock`).

REFEDIT clones the block geometry into the host space through the insert matrix, fades the non-working objects (transparency 85, flag `refedit_ghost`), hides the reference, and keeps the pseudo block `*REFEDIT`. A commit hook (priority 5) reverts any change outside the working set and says so; new objects created during the session join the set. REFCLOSE writes back through the inverse matrix (or discards). Refused: xrefs, generated or dynamic-derived blocks, unequal X/Y scale, arrays, locked layers, self reference. `ctx.locked` also checks `editstate.locked_entity`.

External references are other `.fcad` drawings. A block record gets an `xref` dict; content lives in `block:NAME` and nested ones in `block:NAME|nested`; symbols are named `NAME|symbol` and layer overrides survive RELOAD. Paths are stored relative to the host when the target is below or beside it, else absolute; the resolver tries stored relative, then the drawing folder, then absolute, with depth, count and size limits and circular detection. XATTACH, XREF (list, reload, unload, detach, path, type), XBIND and -XBIND (Bind makes `NAME$n$sym`, Insert merges), XOPEN, XCLIP (rectangle, polygon, delete, generate), clipping applied at display time by wrapping `shapes.BUILDERS["insert"]`. DXF/DWG references go through `xref.register_reader`, X1 registers once.

Dynamic blocks: a definition holds parameters and actions; every distinct value set builds a derived anonymous block `*U<n>` (`dyn_of`, `dyn_key`, `dyn_values`), the insert keeps `extra["dyn"]`. Custom grips, grip drag with snap to increments, and `refresh_references` after a redefinition. Decisions where the spec is silent: values outside min/max are refused rather than clamped; derived blocks are not purged while a reference uses them.
