## Blocks and attributes · _v0.5.0 (doc/blockdefs.py, doc/attribs.py, doc/blockio.py, doc/purge.py, doc/battman.py, doc/dataextract.py, doc/wildcard.py, doc/kinds/blockcache.py, display/attribs.py, display/insert.py, display/state.py, cmd/commands/blocks.py, attributes.py, blockout.py, cmd/grips.py, doc/entities.py, routers/blocks.py)

BLOCK, -BLOCK, INSERT, -INSERT (every option, `*name` exploded, drawing file), BASE, WBLOCK, -WBLOCK, PURGE, -PURGE, BLOCKREPLACE, BURST, ATTDEF, -ATTDEF, ATTEDIT, -ATTEDIT, ATTDISP, ATTSYNC, BATTMAN, DATAEXTRACTION (EATTEXT) and the block listing all have typed prompts, canvas payloads and a programmatic `executes` form. `doc/blockdefs.py` is the one plain module for K2: define, redefine, list, purge, expand (see the K1 handoff for the table). Children of a block keep their drawing coordinates and the record `base` is subtracted at placement (translate(pos) * R * S * translate(-base)); a Basepoint override typed at INSERT is folded into the insertion position so records and display need nothing extra.

Attributes: an insert holds `{tag, value, lock_position, layer, color, linetype, lineweight, shift, at, vis}` per non-constant definition, in definition order; constant attributes are drawn from the definition. Attribute text is derived from the definition and the insert matrix on every display pass (`attribs.reference_text` builds a Text twin), so move, rotate, scale and mirror need no attribute transform; a moved attribute stores `shift`, an offset in block space. `at` and `vis` are caches kept by the priority 95 commit hook in `doc/kinds/blockcache.py` (inside the same undo step), together with `Insert.extra["box"]`, which gives window selection and attribute grips without a display context. ATTMODE 0 hides, 1 shows non-invisible, 2 shows all; it is part of `state.global_sig`. Tags are upper-cased with whitespace turned to underscore; values over 2048 characters are refused. Mirrored attribute text is an approximation (rotation and width factor from the matrix, no backwards flag).

ATTSYNC and the BATTMAN `sync` rebuild every reference's attribute list from the definitions, carrying values by tag (a rename passes the old tag in `renames`). WBLOCK writes `.fcad` through `blockio.write`; `blockio.DXF_WRITER` and `DWG_WRITER` are single hooks for X1, and `register_reader` takes the `.dxf` and `.dwg` readers INSERT uses for a drawing file (X1 must call `blockcache.refresh_all` after a DXF import). Both WBLOCK forms erase the objects by default (`convert` retain, convert or delete in the programmatic form). PURGE walks block, layer, linetype, dimension, text, multiline, table and multileader styles, groups, zero-length geometry and empty text; the interactive form asks per name (default No) after "Verify each name to be purged?". DATAEXTRACTION combines identical rows with a Count column or lists every reference with handle, layer, position and rotation (degrees); CSV and tab separated `.txt`; the table output refuses more than 1000 rows or 30 columns. Modules under `doc/kinds` import block modules lazily (circular import otherwise). Not done: COUNT, COUNTLIST and COUNTTABLE, dynamic block parameters (K2), attribute display below the second nesting level.

## Layouts and viewports · _v0.5.0 (doc/layouts.py, doc/viewports.py, doc/vpmax.py, doc/papers.py, doc/kinds/viewport.py, display/layout_view.py, display/viewport_frame.py, cmd/commands/layouts.py, cmd/commands/viewports.py, routers/layouts.py, doc/document.py, sysvars, cmd/grips.py, cmd/runner.py)

LAYOUT (new, copy, delete, rename, set, move, saveas, list), PAGESETUP, MSPACE, PSPACE, VPMAX, VPMIN, MVIEW (1 to 4, Fit, Object, Polygonal, Restore, On, Off, Lock), VPCLIP and VPLAYER all have typed and programmatic forms. Tiled VPORTS stays with the layer and view agent; MVIEW only makes layout viewports.

Model to paper is model = view_center + (paper - center) * scale; `scale` is model units per paper unit (1:50 is 50). A number typed as a scale is paper per model (0.02 becomes 50); `1:50`, `1/50`, `nXP` and scale table names also work and sync `anno_scale`. Page setup `width` and `height` are the sheet as shown (landscape means width >= height); readers call `layouts.sheet_size`. `paper_mm` true keeps layout units millimetres in imperial drawings. New layouts are A4 landscape with 5 mm margins (6.35 mm letter when imperial); the template layouts stay A3.

Clip vertices are `{p, bulge}`; viewport `center`, `width`, `height` are always the exact bounds of the clip and `recentre` keeps the model mapping when they change; moving a viewport keeps `view_center`. MVIEW Object and VPCLIP consume the source object. Layer off hides the viewport border only; frozen layer or `on` false hides content. PSLTSCALE 0 divides pattern scale by the viewport scale. VPMAX stashes the view and VPMIN writes it back unless the viewport is locked. Paper table lists 25 sizes. VPLAYER also takes LType, LWeight, TRansparency and PLotstyle. Viewport items are sent in paper units but not geometrically clipped; renderers apply `clip`.

## Layers and views · _v0.5.0 (doc/layerops.py, doc/viewops.py, cmd/layer_flow.py, cmd/commands/layer_tools.py, views.py, routers/layers.py, cmd/commands/settings.py, doc/rename.py, doc/tables.py)

LAYER and -LAYER keep their typed loop in `settings.py`; the `stAte` option now runs the layer state loop (`cmd/layer_flow.py`: ?, Save, Restore, Edit, Rename, Delete, Import, Export), Rename goes through `layerops.rename_layer`, and the whole session still is one undo step. Material and rEconcile still answer "is not available yet" as B2 6.2 says. Every other piece lives in `doc/layerops.py` (no command or HTTP knowledge): property validation, batch property changes, rename, delete, merge, isolate, previous state, usage counts, filters, states and the one `apply_ops` list that the HTTP route and the `layer.apply` programmatic command both run. The layer record is unchanged (`new_layer`); per viewport overrides are not in it (K4 keeps them on the viewport) and reach the manager through the hook lists `USAGE_HOOKS`, `RENAME_HOOKS`, `DELETE_HOOKS`, `MERGE_HOOKS`, `VIEWPORT_INFO` and `VIEWPORT_SET`. The `layerfilters` table is new (`TABLE_NAMES`); `layerstates` and `views` and `vports` existed and now have records.

Decisions where B2 and E4 are silent: the manager Delete refuses a layer that holds objects or is referenced (LAYDEL deletes the objects too); making a frozen or xref layer current is refused; freezing the current layer is skipped with a reason, turning it off is allowed (the interface asks first); a layer filter is a property filter (name pattern, on, frozen, locked, plot, new viewport freeze, colour, linetype, lineweight, transparency, description, plot style, used) or a group filter (member names), may nest under a parent (child matches must also match the parent) and deleting a parent deletes its children; the search field matches plain text anywhere and wildcard patterns anywhere too; a state keeps every layer property plus the current layer, Restore recreates deleted layers and can take a property mask, can turn off layers the state does not list and never fails on a missing linetype (it keeps the layer's own and says so); state files are our own JSON (`.fcls`, format `formcad-layer-states`), import also reads the states of a `.fcad` drawing; names starting with `*` are internal (view snapshots `*V:name`) and hidden from lists. Isolate and LAYERP memory is per session (`doc.layer_session`), not saved, and a no-op change never creates an undo step (`prune_noop` drops unchanged keys from the open transaction). LAYWALK is a loop that shows the layers you name (Enter restores, Keep keeps). COPYTOLAYER copies in place or by a displacement.

Views and viewports (`doc/viewops.py`): a view record is `{name, layout, center, width, height, twist, layer_state}`; a layer snapshot is an internal state; Restore switches `doc.current_space`, pushes the view history and answers the "layer snapshot is missing" note; the "too large" limit is 1e12. PAN moves the view centre by minus the displacement (the picture follows the cursor), Left/Right/Up/Down move the view by 20 percent of its size, Enter at the first prompt asks the canvas for realtime pan. REDRAW bumps the revision; REGEN drops the display cache of the document and recalculates tables of the current space, REGENALL of every space (a table is only touched when its values change). Tiled viewports are tiles of the unit square in the `*Active` configuration: the arrangement divides the current tile (the large tile is half for 3, two thirds for 4), at most 16 tiles, no side under 0.08; the current tile index and each tile's live view sync into `*Active` when you switch, arrange or list (that write is not an undo step); `POST .../vports {option: current}` is allowed while a command runs. In a layout VPORTS hands over to MVIEW when that command exists.

## Dimensions · _v0.4.0 (app/dim/, display/dimensions.py, cmd/dim_support.py, cmd/commands/dim_linear.py, dim_round.py, dim_quick.py, dim_edit.py, dim_style.py, doc/kinds/dimlink.py, cmd/grips.py, cmd/prompts.py, cmd/commands/modify_misc.py)

Every dimension command of B1 section 2 exists with a typed prompt sequence (jig at each point prompt), a canvas payload path and a programmatic `executes` form: DIMLINEAR, DIM, DIMALIGNED, DIMRADIUS, DIMDIAMETER, DIMJOGGED, DIMCENTER, DIMANGULAR (two lines, arc, circle, three point, Quadrant), DIMARC (whole, Partial), DIMORDINATE, DIMCONTINUE, DIMBASELINE, QDIM (all nine options), DIMEDIT, DIMTEDIT (and the five Align Text menu commands), DIMOVERRIDE, DIMSTYLE update, DIMSPACE, DIMBREAK, DIMJOGLINE, DIMINSPECT, DIMDISASSOCIATE, DIMREASSOCIATE, DIMREGEN, DIMSTYLE and -DIMSTYLE (Save, Restore, STatus, Variables with compare, Apply, ?, Rename, Delete, Compare, plus the `dimstyle.*` variants and a programmatic form with create, modify, restore, list, status, compare, apply, rename, delete, override). A dimension is one `dimension` entity; `display/dimensions.py` builds its geometry on every display pass from the definition points and the resolved style (style variables merged with the entity's `overrides`), so there is no anonymous block. An imported dimension that still names a block is drawn from that block until DIMREGEN (or any edit) clears it. The generator in `app/dim/layout.py` is a pure function of the dimension, the variables, the scale and a text width function, and returns primitives (segments, arcs, polylines, fills, circles, text blocks) tagged with a role (dimension line, extension line 1 or 2, arrow, text) that `display/dimensions.py` turns into compound parts with the role's colour, lineweight and linetype (DIMCLRD, DIMCLRE, DIMCLRT, DIMLWD, DIMLWE, DIMLTYPE, DIMLTEX1, DIMLTEX2; ByBlock follows the entity) and into `fill` and glyph prims. Text height, text style, width factor and font come from the text engine (`app/text`), so dimension text is laid out like any single line text; a text style with a fixed height wins over DIMTXT. All 69 variables of the stock style are honoured (the spec says 66 names but lists 69); DIMSCALE 0 means the annotation scale factor; DIMLDRBLK draws an arrow on the DIMTMOVE 1 leader.

Entity fields (`doc/entities.py` unchanged). `dim_type`: `linear`, `aligned`, `radius`, `diameter`, `jogged`, `angular` (two lines), `angular3` (vertex, two points, also arcs and circles), `arc`, `ordinate`, `center`. Points: linear and aligned use p13 and p14 as the extension line origins and def_point as a point on the dimension line (linear `rotation` is the dimension line direction, radians); radius has def_point = centre and p15 = point on the circle, diameter def_point = far point and p15 = near point, jogged adds `marks.jog_center` and `marks.jog_point`; angular p13/p14 and p15/p16 are the two lines and def_point the arc location, angular3 p15 = vertex, p13/p14 the endpoints and `marks.ccw` the sweep direction; arc p15 = centre, p13/p14 the arc ends; ordinate def_point = datum, p13 = feature, p14 = leader end, `marks.ordinate` x or y. `measurement` is the raw value (radians for angular, signed for ordinate, rounded to 12 places); DIMLFAC, DIMRND, units and tolerances apply when the text is built. `marks`: `text_angle`, `oblique` (extension line direction), `jog`, `breaks` (point, size, auto, role), `inspect` (shape, label, rate), `sector_pt`, `partial`, `radius`. `text_point` is kept current by `dim/regen.refresh` unless `text_moved`.

Text and fit rules (B1 and B5 give no exact formulas, so these are ours, written down in the handoff): arrow and tick sizes, extension offset and extension, gap and text height scale with DIMSCALE; text fits between the extension lines when its extent along the line plus two gaps is at most the line length, arrows fit when twice the arrow size is, and DIMATFIT 0 to 3 choose what moves out when both do not fit (0 both, 1 arrows first then text, 2 text first then arrows, 3 text stays when it fits and the arrows go out, otherwise whatever fits); DIMTIX forces text in, DIMSOXD drops the outside arrows, DIMTOFL keeps the line between the extension lines; DIMTAD 0 centred with the line broken around the text (DIMTVP offsets it), 1 and 3 above, 2 away from the measured points, 4 below; DIMJUST 1 and 2 sit one arrow size plus a gap from the extension line, 3 and 4 above the extension line end along it; DIMTIH and DIMTOH choose horizontal or aligned text inside and outside; DIMTMOVE 0 moves the line with the text (DIMTEDIT), 1 adds a leader, 2 moves freely. Tolerances stack at DIMTFAC times the text height (DIMTOLJ bottom, middle, top), limits stack at full height, alternate units follow the primary text in square brackets, fractions stack (horizontal bar, diagonal slash) or print flat. Arrowheads (20 names, plain or `_BLOCK` spelling) are drawn from own geometry in `dim/arrows.py`; unknown names draw closed filled.

Associativity (B1 7). `dim/features.py` finds the feature under a definition point (endpoints and vertices, then centres, then midpoints, ties to the first object in drawing order) and stores links in `associativity` per point (`start`, `end`, `mid`, `center`, `vertex` with index, `segmid`, `ins`, `ang` for a fixed angle on a circle or arc). A commit hook at priority 80 (`doc/kinds/dimlink.py`, before hatches at 90) runs inside the same undo step: a linked point follows its object; when every linked point of a dimension moved by the same vector the dimension line point, text and leader end move along (an ordinate datum and the free measured origins of a linear dimension never do); a dimension changed on its own while its object did not loses the links of the points that moved; an erased object drops its links; dimensions on locked layers are skipped; changed dimensions also get their measurement and text position recomputed. DIMASSOC 1 and 2 attach, 0 does not. Cost of the hook is about 4 ms per commit on the 100,000 line fixture.

Shared files touched, one region each: `display/state.py` (import and BUILDER_MODULES), `cmd/grips.py` (dimension grip list, stretch and transform now call `dim/dimgrips.py`: grips d0, d13 to d16, jc, jp and text per kind, rotate and mirror turn the linear rotation and the mark angles), `cmd/prompts.py` (`tail_word` also matches a lower case word followed by a capitalised one such as datumPoint or seTtings by the capitalised part, so P and T work in QDIM), `cmd/commands/modify_misc.py` (EXPLODE of a dimension with no block builds lines, arcs, solids, discs and text objects from the generator, `dim/explode.py`). Verified through TestClient with the real token guard: 493 numeric and prompt checks plus 175 fixture cells, see `sidecar/handoff/DM1.md`; fixture `fixtures/dimensions.fcad`.

## Modify part 1 · _v0.4.0 (cmd/commands/modify.py, modify_transform.py, modify_trim.py, modify_join.py, doc/curveops.py, cmd/runner.py, cmd/jig.py)

ERASE and OOPS completed, MOVE and COPY (Array and Fit with jigs), ROTATE, SCALE, MIRROR, STRETCH, ALIGN, REVERSE (`modify_transform.py`), OFFSET, TRIM, EXTEND, BREAK, BREAKATPOINT (`modify_trim.py`), JOIN and LENGTHEN (`modify_join.py`), every option and prompt of A2, each with a jig at its point prompts and a programmatic `executes` form (selection defaults to the implied selection, a single `handle` is accepted). 749 recorded checks in the M1 handoff (typed prompt sequences and canvas point, pick and selection payloads through the real token guard, results compared with hand computed values, previews, undo and redo of all 17 commands byte for byte); fixture `fixtures/modify-part1.fcad` (60 cells).

`doc/curveops.py` is the shared curve math (M2 and later agents may import it): `path_of(entity)` gives a `CurvePath` of `LineSegment`, `ArcSegment` (signed sweep), `EllipseSegment` and `SplineSegment` (window into a `nurbs.Curve`); parameter `s` = segment index plus a 0..1 fraction. Intersections of every pair of kinds: closed form for line and line, line and arc, arc and arc (tangent when the squared gap is within 1e-12), numeric for anything with an ellipse or spline (sampled polylines seed Newton refinement on the true curves, grid culling above 1600 pairs, hits within 1e-8, dedupe 1e-7). Extension mode treats lines as infinite and arcs and ellipses as the whole curve; `extension_seg` continues an open end (spline along its end tangent as a straight run). Also closest point, `slice` (wraps on closed paths), `build_entity` (rebuilds line, arc, circle, elliptical arc, polyline with recomputed bulges and widths, polyline3d or spline from segments with the source's properties), `offset_entity` and `offset_path` (segments offset, neighbours joined at the intersection nearest the previous end, collapsed segments dropped, a closed polyline that inverts gives nothing), spline editing (`sub_curve` and `insert_knot` by Boehm insertion to multiplicity p, `elevate` through Bezier decomposition, `join_curves` with weight rescaling and junction multiplicity p, `reverse_curve`, `seg_curve` exact rational quadratics for arcs and ellipses, `extend_straight`, `curve_length`, `t_at_length`) and the polygon, fence and crossing tests the runner uses. Tolerances come from the caller (`ctx.d.tolerances["absolute"]`, default 1e-9); JOIN uses 1e-6 as A2 states.

Runner: the selection prompt gained Fence, WPolygon and CPolygon (typed `F`, `WP`, `CP`, Enter closes, 10000 point cap, a rubber polyline in the jig), BOX is a left to right window or right to left crossing, windows and crossings now test geometry (box overlap alone selects nothing; xlines and rays can be crossed), and `runner.sel_areas` keeps the windows and polygons of the last selection so STRETCH can tell vertices in the area from objects picked whole (pick, Fence, ALL, Last, Previous and pick-first move whole). A `pick` payload may carry `shift: true` (TRIM extends, EXTEND trims).

Decisions where A2 is silent: TRIM and EXTEND ignore cuts at an object's own end, quick mode erases an object no edge crosses, a closed object needs two or more cuts and keeps the complementary run as an open object, the first piece keeps the handle and the second is new (BREAK removes the original handle as A2 says), Fence trims at every crossing, Crossing at the point nearest the first corner; OFFSET Enter at the side prompt exits, Through is the distance to the object, ellipse offsets are 720 vertex polylines, spline offsets refit from at most 40 points, closed polylines that collapse are refused; JOIN merges collinear lines across gaps, arcs on one circle by the shorter way round, chains of lines, arcs and polylines into a polyline (closed when the chain closes) and anything with a spline or elliptical arc into one spline; LENGTHEN Percent scales the length, spline lengthening is a straight run on the end tangent and shortening cuts along the curve; MIRRTEXT 1 toggles `extra.mirrored` and rotates text by `2 * mirror angle - rotation` (the display does not read the flag yet); ALIGN ignores the third pair in XY. Known gaps: grazing intersections without a sample vertex on the touching point can be missed by the numeric solver, overlapping stretches are not reported as hits, TRIM and EXTEND only cut curve kinds.

## Modify part 2 · _v0.4.0 (cmd/commands/modify_corner.py, modify_polyedit.py, modify_misc.py, cmd/edit_ledger.py, doc/cornerops.py, polyedit.py, splineedit.py, blockxf.py, kinds/viewport.py, display/viewport_frame.py)

FILLET, CHAMFER, BLEND, PEDIT (with the vertex editing sub-mode and Multiple), SPLINEDIT (fit data, control vertices), EXPLODE, NCOPY, MOCORO, CHSPACE, FLATTEN and a simple path array registered as `arraypath.simple` (the name `arraypath` belongs to the associative array command of H1). Geometry lives in `doc/cornerops.py` (tangent circle solver: closed form for lines, circles and arcs, a Newton solver on closest-point distances for ellipses and splines; kept-side rules by the pick sides of the intersection; trim cuts for lines, arcs, elliptical arcs and splines by knot insertion; blend curves), `doc/polyedit.py` (biarc fit through every vertex with per-vertex tangents, uniform cubic spline frame, join chain with fuzz, straighten, break), `doc/splineedit.py` (Boehm insertion, split, reverse, degree elevation through Bezier pieces plus knot removal, exact rational line and arc conversion, join), `doc/blockxf.py` (block reference matrices, similarity transforms through `grips.transform`, non-uniform scale turning circles and arcs into ellipses). `cmd/edit_ledger.py` is the undo bookkeeping for the Undo option inside a running command (before images of touched entities, created handles, erased entities). Pick payloads accept `shift: true` (one-line edit in `runner._canvas_pick`), which FILLET and CHAMFER read as the zero corner. `prompts.match_keyword` also accepts the capital letters of a keyword that starts in lower case (`eXit` answers `x`, `mEthod` answers `e`). A minimal `viewport` entity kind (paper centre, size, model view centre, scale as model units per paper unit) exists only so CHSPACE has something to convert through; MVIEW and layout commands are not built. Circles, closed splines and full ellipses are never trimmed by FILLET. Splines cannot be extended by FILLET (refused). PEDIT Fit and Spline keep the original frame in `extra.frame` so Decurve restores it. Block attributes are dropped by EXPLODE and annotative scale is not applied. 455 recorded checks plus a 79 cell fixture undone and redone cell by cell, details in `sidecar/handoff/M2.md`; fixture `fixtures/modify-part2.fcad`.

## Leaders, multileaders, annotative scaling and tables · _v0.4.0 (doc/annotation.py, doc/tablecalc.py, doc/tableops.py, doc/kinds/leader.py, mleader.py, table.py, display/annot.py, cmd/annot_grips.py, cmd/commands/leaders.py, mleaders.py, annotative.py, tables.py, routers/tables.py)

New entity kinds `leader` (old style: points, arrowhead flag, spline flag, dimension style, arrow size; the annotation is a separate MText, Text or insert), `mleader` (leaders as `{id, pts}` from arrowhead to the shared landing, `landing`, `dogleg_dir` +1 or -1, `has_landing`, `leader_type`, content `mtext`, `block` or `none`, `content_pos`, optional per object sizes that otherwise follow the style, `size_scale` for SCALE, `more_blocks` for collected content) and `table` (cells as dicts: `text` is the displayed value, `formula` the `=` text, `type` text, number or formula, `span` on the anchor only, own style, alignment, format, fill, borders; `breaks`, `link`, `rotation`). Sizes of a multileader come from its style at display time (DIMSCALE is not used), so a style edit redraws every object; the content anchor is always computed as landing plus dogleg plus gap, `content_pos` is only the stored copy (and the anchor of a leaderless multileader). Arrowheads use `app/dim/arrows.py` (DM1); the small module first written here was removed.

Annotative scaling: the object's scale names are the base `annotation_scales`, representation offsets live in `extra["anno_pos"]` (scale name to a vector; ANNORESET removes them, `annotation.shift_representation` is the one way to move a single representation). `display_entity` (called from `make_entry`) returns a scaled clone: text and mtext height, width and column lengths times the factor (a scaled style record is seeded in `ctx.style_cache`, so the text builders are untouched), dimension `DIMSCALE` override times the factor with `marks.regen` set so DM1's builder regenerates, insert scale, non solid hatch pattern scale, multileader sizes. Factor is drawing per paper units of the displayed scale: the current one when the object supports it, otherwise (ANNOALLVISIBLE 1) its first scale, otherwise hidden (ANNOALLVISIBLE 0). Layout space entities draw at 1:1 and are never hidden. `Context.stamp` calls `annotation.stamp_new`, so every command that adds text, dimensions, multileaders or inserts through `ctx.add` makes them annotative when the style (or block) flag is set. `CANNOSCALE` writes call `annotation.on_scale_changed` (ANNOAUTOSCALE above 0: add the scale to annotative model space objects on visible, unlocked layers) inside the same undo step. The display signature includes CANNOSCALE, ANNOALLVISIBLE and the scale, multileader style, table style and dimension style table revisions.

Tables: formulas are parsed once per recalculation (`doc/tablecalc.py`), evaluated in dependency order with a ready list (no recursion across cells, so a 500 deep chain is fine), cycles and everything that depends on a cycle show `#CYCLE`; errors propagate through references. Limits are enforced as `#LIMIT` (formula 1024 characters, nesting 32, 100000 cells per range, 20000 formula cells, 256 arguments, 4 million visits). Power is right associative and binds tighter than a unary sign (`-2^2` is -4). Insert and delete rewrite formula references (ranges grow on insertion inside them, deleted single references become `#REF`, range corners move inward). Merges: anchor holds the content, texts of absorbed cells are joined, a partial overlap is refused, deleting the anchor row or column moves the merge to the next one. Default column width is 40 for metric drawings (the spec's 2.5 only suits inch drawings); row height is text height plus twice the vertical margin and never shrinks below content. Number type cells with decimal or percent format are formatted on entry; formula results are always formatted by the cell format or the cell style. Table breaks split between rows (never inside a vertical merge) by height, optionally repeating the title and header rows and the last row; part offsets are grips (`p0` moves the table). Borders resolve per edge: own cell border, else the style's (top, inside, bottom, left, right) for the two cells that share the edge. Data links read CSV, `.txt` and `.tsv` (8 MB cap), the path is stored absolute. Cell text is laid out with the multiline text engine.

HTTP: `GET` and `PUT /api/docs/{id}/table/{handle}` (new router in `ROUTERS`): GET returns rows, columns, sizes, breaks, link, errors and every cell with its effective style, alignment and format; PUT takes `cells` (set text, formula, `type`, `format`) and `ops` (insertRow, deleteRow, insertCol, deleteCol, merge, unmerge, colWidth, rowHeight, align, format, fill, textHeight, border, cellStyle, breaks, recalc) as one undo step, 400 on a refused change, 409 while a command runs. Programmatic commands for the automation operations are `table.setcell`, `table.insertrow`, `table.deleterow`, `table.insertcolumn`, `table.deletecolumn`, `table.merge`, `table.unmerge`, `table.cellstyle`, `table.colwidth`, `table.rowheight`, `table.align`, `table.border`, `table.format`, `table.fill`, `table.textheight`, `table.formula` (Sum, Average, Count, Min, Max default to the cells above from the first data row, Cell, Equation), `table.recalc`.

Edits to shared files, single regions each: `sysvars/registry.py` (CANNOSCALE hook inside `write()`), `cmd/context.py` (`stamp_new` call), `cmd/grips.py` (grips, stretch and transform branches for the new kinds; any entity with `apply_transform` is now transformed through it), `display/state.py` (imports, `BUILDER_MODULES`, the annotative clone in `make_entry`, the signature), `main.py` (one `ROUTERS` line), `fixtures/README.md`.

Known gaps and choices: the alias MLEDIT of MLEADEREDIT in the catalogue collides with the multiline editor MLEDIT (the multiline command wins, MLEADEREDIT is reachable by its full name and the menu); no command sets the per object size overrides of a multileader (the fields exist, MATCHPROP or the palette may use them); FIND replace writes cell text without recalculating formulas; wrapped cell text does not grow the row height (only explicit line breaks do); a leader or multileader grip stretch never changes the content text; per scale positions can only be set through `annotation.shift_representation` (no command, the grips route moves the shared position); the property palette has no fields for the new kinds. Verification: 462 inline checks through TestClient with the real token guard (leader 41, multileader 114, annotative 57, tables 199, transforms and grips 20, previews and undo 20 and perf and limits 11), `fixtures/leaders-tables.fcad` reopened with 58 display items.

## Arrays, groups, properties and inquiry · _v0.4.0 (doc/kinds/array.py, display/arrays.py, doc/groups.py, doc/props.py, doc/rename.py, doc/find.py, doc/measure.py, calc/, cmd/props_flow.py, cmd/commands/arrays.py, groups.py, props.py, props_style.py, rename.py, calc.py, find.py, inquiry.py, routers/properties.py, routers/calc.py)

Associative arrays are the entity kind `assoc_array`: the source objects are moved into the array as entity dicts (not kept in the drawing), with shape (rect, polar, path), parameters, `replacements`, `overrides` and an `editing` record for source editing. Items are never stored; `frames()` yields each item's translation and rotation and `display/arrays.py` builds the geometry once per source object and maps it per item (cap 20000 items drawn). ARRAYEDIT Source places an item's objects in the drawing and ARRAYCLOSE inverts that item's frame to make them the new source, so all items follow. ARRAYPATH is associative by default (task wording; A3 says copies, `associative: false` gives copies). Path arrays keep a snapshot of the path curve (no live link). Polar base point matters for rotate off and ring direction, so ARRAYEDIT Base point can move items there. New sysvars ARRAYASSOC, AREA, PERIMETER, DISTANCE, VOLUME.

Groups live in the `groups` table (name, description, selectable, unnamed, members, serial). The selection resolver (`runner._grouped`) expands group members (PICKSTYLE bit 1) and associative hatch sources (bit 2) in `_apply_selection`, the selection payload and `set_selection`; prompts with `hint == "nogroup"` pick single members. Group purge runs before every command commit. Select prompts accept extra keywords (`prompt.options`); the objects already chosen are in `runner.sel_carry`.

`doc/props.py` is the single property engine (common fields, per kind fields, shared and varying values) behind PROPERTIES.SET, CHPROP, the palette route (`GET` and `PUT /api/docs/{id}/properties`, skipLocked default true) and MATCHPROP. `POST /api/docs/{id}/find` and `POST /api/calc` (errors answer 200 with `ok: false`). The CAL engine is `app/calc/`. 715 checks through TestClient with the real token guard, all passing; fixture `fixtures/arrays-groups-props.fcad`. Edits elsewhere: `cmd/grips.py` (assoc_array grips, stretch, transform), `cmd/runner.py` (above), `display/state.py` (BUILDER_MODULES), `sysvars/defs.py`, `main.py` (two ROUTERS lines).

## Hatch, gradient, boundary and region · _v0.4.0 (doc/curves2d.py, planar.py, loops.py, patterns.py, hatchfill.py, regionops.py, commit_hooks.py, kinds/region.py, kinds/hatchlink.py, display/hatch_d.py, cmd/hatch_support.py, cmd/commands/hatch.py, hatchedit.py, boundary.py)

HATCH (H, BH), -HATCH, GRADIENT (GD), HATCHEDIT (HE, -HATCHEDIT), HATCHPICK, HATCH.PATTERNS, BOUNDARY (BO, BPOLY), REGION (REG), UNION (UNI), SUBTRACT (SU), INTERSECT (IN), each with typed prompts, canvas payloads, jigs where a point prompt exists, and a programmatic form. 23 patterns in `doc/patterns.py` (ANSI31 from the spec, the rest FormCAD's own line families, recorded in the module), user pattern files and inline text loaded with `hatch.patterns` (built-in names are kept, bounded counts, families travel inside the hatch). Boundary detection works on exact lines and arcs in a planar graph (`planar.Graph`: split at crossings and T junctions, dedupe, optional gap bridging, prune dangling ends, ray shot from the pick, face walk with the sharpest left turn); ellipses, splines and xlines are flattened (a documented slack of about 2e-4 of the curve size lets a line end on a flattened ellipse or spline); loops come back with arcs merged into bulges, so a circle island is two bulge 1 vertices and areas are exact. Islands: normal alternates through nested components, outer keeps the outermost loop and its first islands, ignore keeps the outer loop only. Region booleans (`regionops.py`) classify the edges of the split graph against the other region, so crossing and coincident boundaries work and arcs stay arcs; the only approximation is the point in region test on edge midpoints, which uses a polygon with 0.05 rad steps.

Shared files edited in single regions: `doc/document.py` (`commit` calls `commit_hooks.run(self, tx)` before the transaction closes, so hook edits join the same undo step; `doc/commit_hooks.py` is the registry, priority order, a failing hook is reported and skipped), `doc/entities.py` (Hatch gains `origin_mode`, `gap`, `annotative`; `bounds()` flattens arcs), `sysvars/defs.py` (HPASSOC, HPSEPARATE, HPORIGIN, HPORIGINMODE, HPISLAND, HPGAPTOL, HPDRAWORDER, HPTRANSP, HPANNOTATIVE, HPBOUND, GRADNAME, GRADCOLOR1, GRADCOLOR2, GRADANGLE, GRADCENTERED, GRADTINT), `display/state.py` (`hatch_d` in `BUILDER_MODULES`), `cmd/grips.py` (region center grip and transform, hatch and gradient angle under rotate and mirror).

Associativity lives in `doc/kinds/hatchlink.py` (a commit hook, priority 90): source objects carry `{"hatch": handle}` in `reactors`; after every committed change the hatches whose sources changed are re-traced from their sources (nearest face to the old loop centroid shifted by how far the sources moved), an erased island loop is dropped, an erased or non closing outer loop or a hatch moved or reshaped on its own stops being associative with a message, and a hatch created by a command carries a one commit `assoc_fresh` flag so it is not re-checked. MOVE, STRETCH, ROTATE, ERASE and grip edits all follow because they all commit through `Document.commit`.

Display (`display/hatch_d.py`, replaces the old hatch builder): pattern lines as clipped `pl` prims from a numpy sweep (`doc/hatchfill.py`, dash phase anchored on the family origin plus the shift per line), solid as even odd slab trapezoids in `tri` prims, gradients as 64 colour steps in `tri` prims that carry their own `c` (a base fill in the middle colour first; no new prim type), regions as outline parts. Budgets per D1 (1024 families, 20,000 lines per family, 50 million dash steps); because the display route has no zoom input, a hatch that would exceed 150,000 runs or 20,000 lines per family is sent as a solid `tri` fill whose prim carries `lod: "solid"`. Programmatic HATCH, GRADIENT, BOUNDARY and HATCHEDIT never change the remembered settings; the typed options do (HP and GRAD variables, undone with the command).

## Text commands and complex scripts · _v0.4.0 (app/text/bidi.py, arabic.py, scripts.py, shaping.py, paragraph.py, cmd/commands/text_*.py, routers/mtext.py)

TEXT (alias DT, DTEXT), MTEXT (T, MT), TEXTEDIT (ED, DDEDIT), STYLE (ST), -STYLE, style.list, style.rename, style.delete, style.current, each with typed prompts, canvas payloads, jigs and a programmatic form; `POST|GET /api/docs/{id}/mtext` (create, replace, read; while an MTEXT command waits for text, a POST without handle completes it) registered with one ROUTERS line. Text engine: own Unicode Bidirectional Algorithm in `bidi.py` (python-bidi is LGPL so it is not used; 91,707 cases of the Unicode BidiCharacterTest file pass, run from a scratch folder and not kept), HarfBuzz through uharfbuzz 0.57.0 (Apache-2.0, wheels exist for macOS arm64, Windows x64, Linux x64 on Python 3.12) for kerning, Arabic joining and ligatures, mark positioning and Thai stacking, glyphs drawn by glyph id. Without the shaper (import failure) a fallback joins Arabic through presentation forms and attaches marks with zero advance, with no kerning. Single line text and multiline paragraphs both shape clusters, then reorder per line; an RTL paragraph defaults to right alignment; script specific fallback font lists run first for Arabic, Hebrew and Thai; a Thai word wider than the box splits at cluster boundaries (no dictionary segmenter). Vertical styles stack characters at 1.4 heights. Style oblique is stored in radians (STYLE takes degrees). Choices where the encyclopedia is silent are in the T1 handoff.

## Clean-room renames and closed API docs · _v0.3.0 (app/solve/, app/main.py)

`SolveError` is now `SolverFault` in errors.py, model.py, constraints.py and __init__.py, behaviour unchanged; the old name collided with a type name in the reference application's source. `/docs` and `/openapi.json` sat outside the `/api` prefix the guard covers and listed every route without the token; FastAPI now builds them only when `FORMCAD_API_DOCS=1`. Verified: selfcheck with 8 routers, a rectangle sketch solves, `/docs` and `/openapi.json` answer 404 by default.

## Draw integration · _v0.3.0 (app/display/state.py, formcad_engine.py, setup/freeze_backend)

Grip ids: the display list no longer builds grips in `display/shapes.py` order. `make_entry` takes `grips_of(entity)` from `app/cmd/grips.py`, so `items[].grips[].id` are the strings the `/grips` and `/grip` routes use (`start`, `mid`, `end`, `center`, `q0`..`q3`, `v0`, `m0`, `f0`, `c0`, `ins`, ...) in that order, and the fast line path emits `start, mid, end` literally. Kinds are `grips_of` kinds (`vertex`, `segmid`, `quadrant`, `insert`, ...). The grip lists built inside `shapes.py` and `extra_d2.py` are now unused by the display route (insert expansion still reads them for children); remove them when those files are next edited. Entities with no `grips_of` entry (a kind it does not know) show no grips, which matches the fact that `/grip` could not edit them.

Frozen engine was broken: it started but served no routes. `--collect-submodules app` is evaluated by the `pyinstaller` command line before `--paths` takes effect, so with the console script `app` was "not a package" and only 9 statically reachable `app.*` modules reached the bundle (`app.routers.system` and the others were missing; `register()` skips a missing router silently, so every `/api` route answered 404 or 405). `setup/freeze_backend` now runs `PYTHONPATH=<repo>/backend pyinstaller ... --paths <repo>/backend`; the bundle holds all 73 `app` modules. `--selfcheck` now imports every module in `ROUTERS` and prints `"routers": 8`, so a bundle without them fails the check. Verified: the frozen engine (142 MB, 224 files) passes `--selfcheck`, starts, creates a document, runs LINE from `/input` point payloads, and answers `/display` with grip ids `["start","mid","end"]`.

Verified through the real interface (see the frontend log): one of every Draw command is created by menu, rail icon or typed command with canvas point, pick and snap payloads; the grip route moves only the dragged end of a LINE and undo restores it.

## Draw commands part 2 · _v0.3.0 (cmd/commands/draw_points.py, draw_splines.py, draw_marks.py, draw_mline.py, cmd/draw_support.py, doc/nurbs.py, doc/curvepath.py, doc/kinds/, display/extra_d2.py)

POINT, POINT.MULTIPLE (moved out of `draw.py`), PTYPE (command `ddptype`), LIMITS, DIVIDE, MEASURE, SPLINE and SPLINE.CV, HELIX, REVCLOUD (rectangular, `.polygonal`, `.freehand`), WIPEOUT, 3DPOLY, CENTERMARK, CENTERLINE, BREAKLINE, MLINE, MLSTYLE and MLEDIT, every option of A1, each with a jig where a point prompt exists and a programmatic `executes` form. 271 recorded checks in `sidecar/handoff/D2.md` (typed prompt sequences and canvas payloads through the real token guard, numeric geometry, undo of each); fixture `fixtures/draw-all-part2.fcad`.

New entity kinds in `doc/kinds/` (loaded by `doc/kinds/__init__.py`, imported at the end of `doc/entities.py`): `helix` (parameters plus a derived cubic spline: control points and knots rebuilt by `rebuild()`), `mline` (style definition copied into the entity, vertices with cut lists, geometry helpers `element_runs`, `cap_shapes`, `joint_lines`, `fill_ring`, `locate`), `wipeout` (polygon, optional per entity frame). Display builders live in `display/extra_d2.py` (added to `BUILDER_MODULES`): helix as a spline geom, wipeout as a polygon frame plus a `fill` prim with `mask: true` (the renderer paints it with the background and draws it in order over what lies beneath), mline as a compound whose parts carry element colour and linetype, caps, joints and a `fill` prim with `c` for the style fill. Revision clouds are closed lwpolylines with bulge 0.5 (or -0.5 reversed) and `extra.revcloud`; break-lines are open lwpolylines with `extra.breakline`; centre marks and centre lines are lines on the CENTER linetype (loaded when absent); 3DPOLY uses the existing `polyline3d` kind.

`doc/nurbs.py` (numpy, no new dependency) holds basis functions, derivatives, rational evaluation, fitting and sampling. Fit splines are always cubic: global interpolation with parameters by Chord, Square root or Uniform, clamped knots at the data parameters, natural end conditions or given end tangents (magnitude from the end secant speed), a repeated fit point is a kink (pieces fitted apart and joined with knot multiplicity equal to the degree), Close is periodic (knots extended by the period, first `degree` control points repeated), a positive tolerance runs least squares approximation with growing control point count until every fit point is within the tolerance (tangents are ignored then). Above 2000 fit points the points become control points (the spec quotes about 4000; the dense solve is capped lower). CV splines are clamped uniform; CV Close appends the first `degree` points with a periodic uniform knot vector. `doc/curvepath.py` turns line, arc, circle, ellipse, lwpolyline (bulges), polyline3d, spline and helix into an arc length path used by DIVIDE and MEASURE (ellipse and spline use a 2048 step length table and exact evaluation at the interpolated parameter).

Choices where A1 is silent: revision cloud arc length is the true arc length (chord = arc length / 1.1639 for bulge 0.5), calligraphy tapers from width 0 to 0.15 times the arc length, the boundary is made counter-clockwise first so bulge 0.5 is outward; HELIX Axis endpoint ends the command with height equal to the distance (the model is 2D, z of a typed endpoint is used when given); BREAKLINE symbol width is the size times DIMSCALE; MLEDIT tools are defined here (CC cuts every element of the first multiline across the second's width, OC also cuts the outer elements of the second, MC cuts the outer elements of both, CT trims or extends the first to the near edge of the second, OT also cuts the second's near outer element across the first, MT moves the first to the second's centre line and cuts that element, CJ moves the nearest end of both to the centre line intersection); cuts are stored per segment as element index plus start and end distance along the centre line segment; LIMCHECK, SPLMETHOD, SPLDEGREE, REVCLOUDSTYLE, REVCLOUDTYPE, HELIXTURNS, HELIXTWIST, HELIXBASE, HELIXTOP, HELIXHEIGHT, BRKBLOCK, BRKSIZE and BRKEXT were added to `sysvars/defs.py`; LIMCHECK is stored but does not yet refuse points. Typed z in `x,y,z` is kept by `parse_point`. A typed option that is the whole line (for example `turn Height`) is fed as one token by `runner._active`. · _v0.3.0 (app/cmd/commands/draw.py, draw_curves.py, draw_shapes.py, cmd/prompts.py, cmd/context.py)

LINE, PLINE, RECTANG, CIRCLE and ARC were completed in place in `draw.py`; XLINE, RAY, the ten arc menu commands (`arc.sce` to `arc.csl`, `arc.continue`), `circle.cd`, `circle.2p`, `circle.3p`, `circle.ttr`, `circle.ttt`, ELLIPSE (`ellipse.axis`, `ellipse.arc`) and the programmatic forms for arc and pline live in `draw_curves.py`; POLYGON, DONUT and SOLID in `draw_shapes.py`. No new entity kind was needed: xline, ray, ellipse, solid and lwpolyline already existed and the display builder already drew them. A donut is a closed two vertex polyline with bulge 1 on both vertices and constant width (outer minus inner) over two; a solid keeps its corners in pick order (3 for a triangle) and draws 1, 2, 4, 3.

Bug found and fixed: a distance or angle prompt that also accepts a point (`ctx.distance`, `ctx.angle` with a base) delivers a picked or typed point as `Input("point")`, and CIRCLE, ARC and PLINE only accepted `Input("number")`, so a radius given by a point silently ended the command. `dnum`, `anum` and `sweep_num` in `draw.py` turn either kind into a distance, an angle or a sweep and every prompt of the part 1 commands uses them. `_build_rect` ignored the programmatic mode dict (fillet, chamfer) on the `p2` path; it now takes it.

Decisions. Arc forms: the keyword switches of the main ARC prompt and the ten menu commands share generator steps (`step_included`, `step_chord`, `step_end_angle`, `step_direction`, `step_radius`, `flow_center_end`, `flow_end_center`) so a bad value says "Invalid arc or circle for these values." and re-asks instead of ending; centre-start-end uses only the end angle; every arc is stored counter-clockwise start to end (start-end-centre from (0,0) to (10,0) about (5,0) runs 180 to 0 degrees). The reverse-direction modifier of centre-start-end and continue has no input channel and is not built. Ttr and Ttt solve lines and circles (arcs and polyline segments as their whole circle or infinite line) in closed form: Ttr by intersecting the offset curves, Ttt as the eight sign combinations of the linear system in (x, y, r, x2+y2-r2) with one quadratic; the candidate whose tangent points are nearest the two or three picks wins; none gives "Circle does not exist." and re-asks. They need entity picks (`{pick}` payload or `args.objects`), so typed text cannot drive them; ellipses and splines as tangent objects are refused with "Select a line, circle, arc or polyline segment.". PLINE: a taper (Width, Halfwidth) applies to the next segment and later segments continue at the ending width (`hist` restores the pair on Undo); CEnter sweeps in the direction consistent with the incoming tangent; Radius gained the Angle option. RECTANG: Rotation persists with the other modes and shows in the "Current rectangle modes" line (reset it with Rotation 0); a rectangle that does not fit its fillet or chamfer keeps square corners; chamfer distance 1 runs along the incoming side and distance 2 along the outgoing side at every corner. POLYGON: a typed radius orients the bottom edge horizontally for inscribed and circumscribed alike, a picked point orients by the vertex (inscribed) or the edge midpoint (circumscribed); the last sides count and mode are the next defaults (sides in `POLYSIDES`, mode in session memory). DONUT: inside larger than outside is refused with a message and the outside diameter is asked again; DONUTID and DONUTOD change with the command's undo step like CIRCLERAD does. ELLIPSE: a distance larger than the first half axis makes the new axis (perpendicular, on the side of the pick, counter-clockwise for a typed number) the major axis; arc angles are measured from the major axis and converted to parameters with t = atan2(sin a, ratio cos a); Isocircle is offered only while SNAPSTYL is 1 and follows SNAPISOPAIR (left 120, top 0, right 60 degrees, ratio 1/sqrt 3, semi-major radius times sqrt 1.5). XLINE Offset remembers the distance in OFFSETDIST (negative for Through). Shared prompt changes: `shortcut_of` and `match_keyword` accept the capitalised last word of a keyword such as "chord Length" (type L), `Prompt.hint == "bare"` prints `msg [A/B]` without "or", and `Context.angle` takes a `jig`.

Verified through TestClient with the real token guard (no token answers 401): 337 numeric checks over every command and option, each with typed input and canvas point payloads, preview answers at each step, undo and redo, plus `fixtures/draw-all-part1.fcad` (113 objects, 92 cells, each driven, undone and redone before saving; reopened with the same count; display items built for all). Table in the D1 handoff.

## Display list and text pipeline · _v0.3.0 (app/display/, app/text/, app/routers/display.py, app/data/fonts/formcad-stroke.json)

`GET /api/docs/{id}/display?since=<rev>&space=<model|layout name>` answers the contract item shape from a per document, per space `DisplayState` (display/state.py) that keeps one JSON fragment per entity and joins fragments into the response, so a full response is a string join and a delta never re-encodes untouched items. `since` omitted, older than the first build's floor (newest layer, linetype, text style or header revision seen when the state was first built) or newer than the document revision gives `full: true`; otherwise only items whose fragment changed after `since` come back, plus `removed` handles (erased, moved to another space, hidden by layer, visibility or isolate, or no longer drawable). Detection uses `ent_rev`, `removed_rev` and `space_rev`; a change to LTSCALE, LWDEFAULT, PDMODE, PDSIZE, FILLMODE, TEXTFILL, FONTALT, FONTFALLBACK, the linetype or text style tables or the session hidden set rebuilds every item and compares fragments; a layer change rebuilds only that layer's items (and block users); block edits rebuild the inserts that use the block. When nothing bumped `rev` but the display changed, the state calls `doc.bump("display")` so `since` stays truthful. `order` is a sparse key (1024 apart, appended items get last plus 1024, restored items get the midpoint) so erasing one item never renumbers its neighbours; only a draw order change renumbers. Line items use a format string fast path (a test proved it equals the generic builder byte for field).

Resolved properties: index and true colours (`#RRGGBB` strings or `[r,g,b]`) through the 256 entry index table (palette.py, generated by hue, five brightness levels 255, 165, 127, 76, 38 and the half saturation variants; 8 and 9 follow the spec values), ByLayer and ByBlock (block parents), `color7` for index 7 reached directly, by layer or by block; lineweight ByLayer, ByBlock, Default (LWDEFAULT, out of range falls back to 0.25), always sent resolved with the top level `lwdisplay` flag so the renderer toggles without a refetch; transparency capped at 90 percent as 0 to 0.9, top level `trdisplay`; linetype pattern in drawing units with positive dash, negative gap, zero dot (text and shape elements dropped, equal sign runs merged), `scale` = LTSCALE times the entity's own scale times the enclosing insert scales (CELTSCALE only seeds the entity's own scale when a command creates it, which `Context.stamp` already does); continuous patterns send `lt: null`; `lt.gen` marks polylines with linetype generation. PSLTSCALE has no effect until viewports exist.

Extra fields beyond the contract, all optional for the renderer: `color7`, prim `c` (RGB) and `c7` for per run colours from `\C` and `\c` (index colours are converted before sending), part level `color`, `color7`, `lw`, `lt` inside insert and dimension block compounds, point `rel` (percent of view height when PDSIZE is zero or negative) and `rot`, layer summary `color7` and `plot`, the top level `lwdisplay` and `trdisplay`. Polyline widths come as `fill` prims with one ring per sub segment (non zero fill). Hatch: solid fill rings, or parallel line `pl` prims from a small pattern table (ANSI31 family, LINE, NET, ANGLE, STEEL; unknown names use 45 degrees); gradients draw solid; full pattern libraries are not built. Dimensions draw only their geometry block (when `block` names one) and otherwise send an empty compound with bounds. Block arrays honour `rows`, `cols`, `row_spacing`, `col_spacing` keys if present.

Text (app/text/): `fonts.py` resolves a style font by stroke names (txt, simplex, romans, anything ending .shx falls to FONTALT then the stroke font), installed file stem, installed family name (name tables read lazily, bounded), FONTALT, then the built in stroke font; per character fallback is FONTFALLBACK, FONTALT, well known wide coverage faces, then a bounded scan (60 files, 512 MB), then the stroke font, then a missing glyph box; wide (CJK) characters fill a one height square cell. System folders: macOS `/System/Library/Fonts`, `Supplemental`, `/Library/Fonts`, `~/Library/Fonts`; Windows `%WINDIR%\Fonts` and the per user folder; Linux `/usr/share/fonts`, `/usr/local/share/fonts`, `~/.fonts`, XDG data folders; plus `<data>/fonts` and `FORMCAD_FONTS`. Outlines come from fontTools 4.68.0 (MIT) flattened at 0.0007 of cap height; text height equals cap height. Outline glyphs are triangulated once per face and character (ear clipping with hole bridging, `tri.py`, original code) and become `tri` prims when TEXTFILL is on, closed `pl` outlines when off; stroke glyphs become `pl` prims. The default stroke font is an original design (`formcad-stroke.json`, ASCII plus degree, plus-minus, diameter and bullet on a 6 by 8 grid, Apache-2.0). Single line text follows the C5 justification rules (alignment point versus insertion, aligned scales height, fit scales width, bottom is a third of a height below baseline for the stroke font, Middle centres on ink extents); backwards and upside down mirror about the alignment point. MTEXT supports every inline code in C5 (`\P \N \~ \\ \{ \} \L \l \O \o \K \k \S \f \H \W \Q \T \C \c \A \p`, `^I ^J ^ `, percent codes), word wrap, tabs of each kind, centre, right, justified and distributed paragraphs, space before and after, at least and exactly spacing, nine attachments and static or dynamic columns. Not built: bidirectional reordering, Arabic joining and Thai shaping, lists generated from `\p` (a bullet, a tab and a hanging indent work as written), kerning.

Entity units decision: text and mtext rotation, text oblique and text style oblique are radians (as arc angles are). The fixtures `lines-100k.fcad` and `text-codes.fcad` were made by the document service in one transaction each. Measured on this machine (single process, Python 3.12): lines-100k open 3967 ms; first `GET /display` (cold build plus HTTP) 1186 ms, 100,000 items, 45,494,868 bytes; warm full response 135 ms; `since=rev` with no change 1 ms and 1255 bytes; one new LINE then `since` 40 ms and 1619 bytes (one item), undo reports the handle as removed, redo returns it with `order` last plus 1024; LTSCALE 1.0 to 2.0 rebuilt in 1415 ms and returned the 77,179 items that carry a pattern; text-codes full 155 ms, 467,104 bytes, 61 items.

## Canvas input, jigs, selection and grips · _v0.3.0 (app/cmd/runner.py, jig.py, snaps.py, grips.py, prompts.py, context.py, app/routers/canvas.py, app/routers/docs.py)

`POST /api/docs/{id}/input` takes `{text}`, `{point, snap?}`, `{pick}` or `{selection}`; the runner routes each into the same prompt machinery as typed text (frames for FROM, M2P and the rest, selection prompts, point and distance and angle prompts). Command-line state gains `expect`, `base` (now [x, y]), `preview` and `snapOverride`. A typed object snap word (END, MID, ...) now sets a one-shot override shown as `Endpoint of:`; the next point or pick is taken from the interface's `snap` when its mode matches, otherwise the engine searches the document (`snaps.search`, aperture from `snap.aperture` or 2 percent of the view height; a pick searches the picked object with no aperture). Perpendicular and tangent are resolved by the engine from `snap.handle` and the base point; with no base they are deferred: the provisional point is the pick location, and the same list object the command stored is rewritten in place when the next point (or a second deferred snap, solved as a pair) arrives. Preview resolves a pending deferred point for the cursor and restores it. Pair solving covers circle and arc tangent-tangent (four common tangents), tangent-perpendicular and perpendicular-perpendicular with lines and circles; splines have no evaluator yet and are refused.

`jig.py`: a prompt carries an optional `jig` function of the cursor point (`ctx.point(..., jig=fn)`); `POST /api/docs/{id}/preview` runs it under the document lock and never touches the document. E1's display builder was not present, so `jig.item` builds the display item shape from the contract itself (handle null, `jig: true`, index 7 colour flagged `color7`, ACI 1 to 9 only); swap `entity_item` for the builder when it lands. A selection window with one corner previews its rectangle. Jigs: LINE, CIRCLE (centre radius, diameter, 2P, 3P), ARC (three point, centre, end, continue), PLINE (line, arc, tangent, angle, second point), RECTANG (with fillet, chamfer, rotation, dimensions, centre mode), MOVE and COPY (moved clones, capped at 2000).

Selection: `GET` and `PUT /api/docs/{id}/selection` hold the implied selection; PICKFIRST 0 makes PUT store an empty set; commands already consumed it through `_preselected`. At select prompts `selection` modes set, add, remove act on the pending set, a pick adds the object (ends single-object prompts), a point starts an automatic window (right is Window, left is Crossing, still bounding-box tests). Idle pick adds or replaces by PICKADD.

Grips (`grips.py`): grip sets per kind from B4 8.1 with string ids (`start`, `mid`, `end`, `center`, `q0..q3`, `v3`, `m3`, `c2`, `f2`, ...); modes stretch, move, rotate, scale, mirror, copy, base, reference (`ref`), multifunctional `option` (add_vertex, remove_vertex, convert_arc, convert_line, lengthen with optional `value`, radius), one undo step per request. Rotate, scale, mirror and move apply to the whole implied selection when the hot object is in it; stretch also drags coincident grips of other selected objects. Locked layers are refused. Added `GET /api/docs/{id}/grips`. Fit splines move only the fit point (no fitter exists).

## Clean-room renames · _v0.2.0 (app/doc/service.py, app/routers/docs.py, app/routers/sysvars.py, app/cmd/registry.py)

`DocError` became `DocumentFault` and `CommandSpec` became `CommandEntry` because both collided with type names in the reference. Behaviour, attributes (`message`, `status`) and HTTP mapping are unchanged; no other file used either name. The generic lowercase identifiers from the audit (`state_id`, `next_handle` and similar) were left as ordinary vocabulary.

## Templates, document kinds and the frozen engine · _v0.2.0 (app/doc/templates.py, app/doc/container.py, app/doc/service.py, app/doc/autosave.py, setup/freeze_backend)

`POST /api/docs` takes the Home template ids (`drawing-mm-a3`, `drawing-inch-ansi-b`, `part`, `assembly`, `drawing-sheet`, `surface`, `form`, `graph`) and the plain `metric`, `iso`, `imperial`, `inch`; each sets kind, units, limits and the layout sheet; an unknown id is a 400. The manifest records `kind`; open and recovery read it back and treat a missing or unknown kind as `drawing`. The 3D kinds open in the drafting view until their workspaces land. The freeze puts `app/data` at `app/data` (the kernel record lookup path) and names numpy and ezdxf as hidden imports because nothing imports them yet; without both the frozen selfcheck failed.

Verified: all eight ids answer 200 with their kind and units, `bogus` answers 400, a saved surface document reopens as surface; the frozen engine (135 MB, 215 files, no OCP, build123d or OCCT inside) passes selfcheck and the licence scan.

## Engine core, document core and kernel link · _v0.2.0 (app/main.py, app/core/, app/services/, app/routers/, app/doc/, app/cmd/, app/sysvars/, formcad_engine.py, kernel/)

Routers load from one list, `ROUTERS` in `app/main.py`, as `(module, prefix)` pairs through importlib, so a phase adds a router with one line; route paths inside a module are relative to its prefix. The guard (`core/guard.py`) checks a loopback Host, an Origin allow list and the `X-FormCAD-Token` header; the token is new per start, written to `<data>/session.token` with mode 0600, injected as a meta tag when the engine serves the built interface; `/api/health` is the only open route. The autosave folder is created 0700.

The OpenCascade kernel never loads in this process. `services/kernel.py` finds the worker through `FORMCAD_KERNEL` (a frozen program or a `.py` path), `<data>/kernel/<release>/formcad-kernel`, then the repo's `kernel/venv`; it speaks JSON lines on stdio (`hello`, `shutdown`). OCP exposes no OCCT version constant, so the worker reports the first three parts of the OCP version as the OCCT version and the full OCP version beside it. The install path extracts with the tar `data` filter, requires every file to match `app/data/kernel-licence.json`, refuses extra files and installs nothing on a mismatch; the record ships with `release: null` until formCAD-binaries publishes a build.

The document is a handle-keyed store (handles from 0x100) with an entity kind registry; unknown kinds round-trip untouched in the foreign-data slot. Undo is a change journal of before-images per transaction, not document copies; marks, BEgin/End groups and a 2000-step cap. New drawings without a template follow MEASUREINIT (imperial by default). Typed object snaps that need a picked point answer that a canvas pick is required until the drafting canvas lands; window and crossing selection use bounding boxes until exact hit tests exist. Linetypes whose dash values the spec did not give numerically use our own consistent values.

Verified: selfcheck exits 0; health open, capabilities 401 without the token, 403 for a foreign Origin or Host; websocket refused without a token; the dev worker reports OCCT 8.0.1, OCP 8.0.1.1, build123d 0.13.0; LINE with four inputs makes two lines, save and reopen keep two, U removes both, REDO restores them.
